The milestones are also the review gate's clock: a crown-jewel row a milestone flips to `authored` enters R-05-150's independent specification review at the flip, not in a release-time batch, so each specification is read at the edition the proofs will be stated against rather than re-read from a corpus that has moved on.

**An upstream's license is read at the milestone that would incorporate it, never at release.** A license is a property of the *arrival*, not of the shipped image, and it is the one property whose discovery cannot be repaired downstream: work built on an upstream whose terms forbid the composition is not re-licensed by finding out later, it is discarded. Two rules follow, and both bite before a line is written. **A vendored tree binds this repository and a pinned submodule does not**, so pinning an upstream to read it is free and vendoring it is a commitment; [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) is the standing account of which each upstream currently is. And **an upstream's terms are read from its own license file rather than inferred from its lineage**, because a design lineage and a code provenance are different facts about the same project: taking an idea from a system carries nothing, and translating that system's artifacts carries whatever those artifacts carry. Every milestone below that would move an upstream from pinned to incorporated names its terms in its own entry. Four upstreams on this plan are not permissive, and they do not resolve alike: three of them foreclose options rather than merely complicating them and each carries a decision item ahead of the work, M1.1a for the compiler, M6.0a for the control-plane compiler, and M4.1a for the kernel specification, which is the one of the three whose artifact would reach the shipped image; the fourth carries a containment rule at M2.1 instead, the emulator fork being a development instrument no image links against. [COPYRIGHT.md](../COPYRIGHT.md) holds the licenses this repository's own content is offered under, which is the thing the gates exist to keep available.

One crown-jewel row retires off this clock: the pinned Wasm guest semantics (inventory row 25, R-14-013b) gates no M-milestone, because the platform interpreter it serves ships with the §18-deferred application program rather than the base system, and its curation is scheduled there: it is the inventory's cheapest retirement, a version-pin of the mechanized WasmCert lineage whose soundness and confinement theorems arrive with the artifact, so the authoring cost is the pin, the subset cut, and the review, not a formalization. The M1 CertiCoq → Wasm host-side oracle is unrelated to it: that is build-farm machinery on the host, and R-05-085's device claim is untouched by it.

Four more rows take their upstreams off this clock while their authoring rides the release program: the radio reference state machines (inventory rows 19–22) arrive with the roster the first release carries whole (R-18-004), and under R-12-043e each begins as a version-pin of the machine-checked symbolic analysis of its protocol, a transcription into the one prover, and a formal-to-formal review, not an original reading of the prose standard. The pinning sweep, deciding which edition of each Tamarin or ProVerif artifact serves as the row's upstream, is the first task of each row's authoring and the cheapest part of it, the analyses' security statements arriving with the artifacts as producer-side evidence; the RRC row additionally names which procedures fall outside the published analyses and take the R-05-050 hand-transcription posture instead, so the thin lane is scoped before it is walked.

### Checklist conventions

* Checked items include concise completion evidence.
* **Every item carries one estimate cell, and only what is inside it is authored.** An open item reads `· 6 h, range 4–8 · 0.7%`, a completed one `· 1.9 h actual · 0.2%`; the range and the actual are the estimates, in attended agent-session hours, and are re-priced when split. The rest is arithmetic: the midpoint is the mean of the range ends, and the percentage is that midpoint's share of the grand total. An item whose children carry the estimates carries none of its own.
* **Every subtotal, the grand total, its range, and the progress figures are sums over those cells**, recomputed by `tools/check.py` (`--fix` rewrites them), so a re-priced item moves everything resting on it in the same edit.
* `Parallel` means the item can proceed in a separate worktree and build directory.
* **`Parallel` is about the build lane and not about attention.** A linked worktree derives its own lane and builds under `/root/build/lane-<name>/`, one directory holding that lane's build tree, its iterate tree and its SMT cache ([I7](#build-loop-instruments)), so two items that both write `model/` share no build tree, no log, and no emulator, and a build holds its own lane rather than the toolchain: a second `model.py build` over a live one in the same lane is refused by name, while a sibling lane's build runs beside it. **Lanes isolate files and build trees, not CPU.** What stays one thing is the machine, twelve cores under one WSL VM, and the M0.4 oracle's tree that no curation here reaches, so concurrent model work is priced in wall time rather than in correctness, and what overlaps a running build for nothing is still the work that opens no lane at all: a provenance decision, a document act, or a checker rule.
* Later milestones remain deliverable-level until entry.

### Current summary

* Completed: M0.1–M0.5, M0.6a, M0.6b, M0.6c (c1–c4), M0.6d, M0.6e (e1–e5), M0.6f, M0.6g, M0.6h, M0.7, M0.8a, M0.8b, M0.8d, M0.9, M0.10, M0.11, M0.12, M0.13, M0.14, M0.15, M0.16, M0.17, M1.1, M1.1a, M1.5, M4.1, M4.1a, M6.0a, and the build-loop instruments I0, I2, I3, I4, I5, I6, I7, I8, I9, I10 and the initial check/emit/FAST tooling. Five of those instruments carry **declines carrying a measurement**: I4's second clause, I2, I6 and I8 whole, and I5's prefix-map clause each had its premise removed by the act of measuring it, which is a result rather than a shortfall.
* Current serial path: **none in M0**. The C-class freeze is taken: M0.6 closed the decode surface, M0.14 and M0.16 put both latency classes and their maintenance in the model without adding an instruction, M0.8a fixed the per-class parameterization at the C class, M0.9 put the timing annotations over it, and M0.10 generated the emulator and gave it the revision every downstream artifact records. What is left in M0 is M0.8c, and it is not a datapath waiting its turn: M0.8b landed the V class, M0.8d landed the Keccak unit and the FEC decoders' surface, and M0.8c's scratchpad landed while its instruction surface is a second-act item conditioned on R-15-116's margin (R-15-014a (ix)). The next serial work is M1's toolchain spine, and it is ungated: M1.1a is taken, and the pin builds whole on this toolchain through to a linked `ccomp` that emits capability assembly, so M1.2's contingency for an unusable artifact is not triggered. What that build also measures is where the item's cost actually sits, the capability pretty-printer being stubbed at 232 of its 262 instruction arms.
* Available parallel work: M2.1 and R1, and M1.3, M1.4, M1.6, M1.8 and M1.9 once M1.2 has landed. **R1 gates on the M0 freeze rather than on M1**, which is taken, and R-18-003b puts the microarchitectural absence contract among the day-one deliverables that gate on nothing, so the RTL track runs beside the toolchain spine rather than behind it; what holds R1's next step is a placement call rather than work, the pins binding nothing and the curated RTL having no home in the map. **M1.8 carries a second gate and it is M1.4, not M1.2.** [The measurement contract](freeze-measurement-contract.md) joins three inputs into the analyzer's one table, the provenance sidecar stream from S1 and S4, the link map from S5, and the encoded image from S7, and two of the three are M1.4's linker and image composer rather than the backend's; the analyzer can therefore be built and wired against M1.2's output and cannot be run end to end until M1.4 lands beside it. **One M0 item is open without being work in the ordinary sense**: M0.8c landed the half the profile books and carries a surface the profile does not name, so what closes it is a profile act rather than a model one, and it gates no milestone's entry here. What holds that act is that it is second-act and nothing before M1 can supply the measurement R-15-116 conditions it on. **M0.8d's act is taken**: its scope was categorical rather than measured, so R-15-014b owed the surface at the provisional freeze, a specification act with no build prerequisite (R-15-014a), and nothing outside this repository gated it once the 3GPP read landed on the table-free arm; the profile now names `ldpcdec` and `polardec` with their operand form and their encoding, authored parameterized over TS 38.212's parameters and fixing none of them, which is the deliverable R-18-004 puts on the critical path to first parts. **[I1](#build-loop-instruments) is the one build-loop instrument still open and it is open the same way**: its parallelism clause landed and its `/mnt/c` clause is refused on a measurement, so what stands are two settings on the human's box rather than in this tree, and no benchmark reaches a posture call. **The four decisions that gated the next milestones are taken**: M4.1 dropped the quiescent point without amending the register, and M1.1a, M4.1a and M6.0a resolved the three provenance forks as contain, author instead, and contain, so M1.2, M4.2 and M6.1 are ungated. The two arms not taken stay open on both contained instruments, which is what containment is for.
* Total estimate: 1,147.9 h midpoint, range 797.9–1,497.9 h.
* Progress by estimate: 133.9 of 1,147.9 h complete (11.7%); 1,014 h remaining (88.3%).

Measured gating may defer 42 h of that past the gate, M2.2 and M2.3 at their midpoints over a 28–56 h range; the critical chain through it is approximately 360–411 h, a serial-path figure no sum gives, the serial Sail path ahead of the C-class freeze now closed while M6.7 and M6.8 are parallel and add none.

### M0 · Hardware reference

Curated Sail model (§1) → single-core RV64IMV+CHERI emulator; ISA tests green. Define the profile-freeze measurement contract (§0): versioned corpus manifest, emitter-provenance schema, report schema, admitted region classes, and per-choice acceptance thresholds. Publish the shared [differential-testing corpus and the capability-widened commit-trace schema](differential-corpus.md) (§10) that every later executor emits.

#### Baselines

* [x] **M0.1 · Pin upstream models** · 0.7 h actual · 0.1%
  * `sail-riscv` pinned at `ac2a5855`; `sail-cheri-riscv` pinned at `bb07488d`. The vendoring baseline is `8f91355e` (M0.6a); the pin tracks the edition the curation is reconciled against, so advancing it is the act of carrying an upstream change across rather than a record of where the tree came from.
  * Finding: the CHERI tree embeds older `sail-riscv` commit `b748a82`; reconciliation therefore requires a semantic transplant, not configuration of one shared base.
  * Upstream carriage is a **disposition per commit**, not a merge: the curated tree has deleted most of what upstream touches, so each change is either applied, or refused with the deletion that makes it inapplicable. Against `8f91355e..ac2a5855`: the `Zvknh` predicate grouping (#1881) **applies**, a missing parenthesis that let `zvknhab_check_encdec` guard only the `Zvknhb` disjunct, and lands in `zvknhab_insts.sail`; the `config_file` build dependency (#1885) **applies** to the two prover-backend rules that survive, SMT and Rocq, the three `rmem` rules it also fixed having gone with the dormant targets in c1; the widening-accumulate source overlap checks (#1884) **apply** whole, `vd` being read as well as written by `vwmacc*`, `vfwmacc*` and `vfwmaccbf16` while the overlap chain guarded only the destination, so eight `valid_src_overlap` guards land across five instruction forms in `vext_arith_insts.sail`, `vext_fp_insts.sail` and `zvfbfwma_insts.sail`; the WFI external-interrupt wake (#1868) is **inapplicable**, its bug being that `MEIP`/`SEIP` are not held in `mip` so a bare read hides them, where M0.6d deleted the fields, their sources, and `read_mip` itself, leaving `mip` carrying `MTI` alone and `shouldWakeForInterrupt`'s bare read total; the Hypervisor extension (#1769) is **inapplicable in every hunk**, R-15-003 and R-15-006 excluding the hypervisor so that `extensions/H/` was never curated in, and each hunk it lands in a file that did survive being conditioned on virtualization this tree does not have, `inVirtualPrivilege`, `vsstatus`, `read_henvcfg`, the `LoadExecute` access type and the three guest-page-fault `xtval` flags alike, the two fragments that read as general included: the `memory_exception` signature change belongs to a restructuring of `sync_exception` into an `ExceptionContext` whose three added fields, `excinfo_is_gva`, `excinfo2` and `excinst`, are read by `mstatus`/`hstatus` `GVA`, by `mtval2`/`htval` and by `mtinst`/`htinst` and by nothing else, where the struct here carries `trap`, `excinfo` and `ext` and not one of those readers exists; and the `currentlyEnabled_measure` correction lifting `Ext_D` above `Ext_F` and adding `Ext_Zcd` has no site at all, the measure table here naming none of the three after the scalar F/D and Zc\* deletions; and the `mhpmevent` VSINH/VUINH relaxation (#1889) is **inapplicable** twice over, being conditioned on H and landing in a `Zihpm/zihpm.sail` that went with R-15-077's deletion of the counters.

* [x] **M0.2 · Stand up the Sail toolchain** · 0.5 h actual · 0.0%
  * WSL Ubuntu 26.04, opam 2.5.0, OCaml 5.4.0, Sail 0.20.2, and z3 5.1.0.
  * Sail 0.20.2 is the required and fidelity-pinned compiler version, installed as `opam install sail.0.20.2` because the bare `opam install sail` resolves to whatever is current and drops the pin without saying so, and the OCaml the switch is built on stays the distribution's and moves with it, because fidelity is a property of the emitter. The solver is now pinned too, and on a weaker ground that is worth stating rather than eliding: soundness is not a property of which sound solver answered, so no version here is *owed*. What the pin buys is that the solver is one this tree names instead of one the archive happens to carry, checked before the move by typechecking the model under 4.13.3, 4.16.0, and 5.1.0 for the same verdict. What it costs is the distribution's patching, which becomes this tree's to carry.

Fixed-width fetch is a *deletion of gating*, not of the 16-bit path: the parcel check stays, because ILEN=32 makes a 16-bit parcel an illegal instruction rather than a misaligned fetch, so `C_ILLEGAL` and the compressed decode mapping are load-bearing with zero real clauses behind them. The alignment relaxation lives in four places, not one, `fetch`, `rvfi_fetch`, `jump_to`, and the `xepc` legalization pair, and only the first two are obvious from the extension registry.     * Net change: 5,006 lines removed and 742 added across 68 files, seven deleted outright and three renamed as their surviving content moved (`vmem_types.sail` → `mem_access_types.sail`, `vmem_utils.sail` → `mem_access.sail` with `vmem_read`/`vmem_write` → `data_read`/`data_write`, and the `mstatus` SXL/UXL unit test replaced by one over the properties this batch establishes).

Under the max configuration the same cut excludes 17 tests, the RV32 and RV64 halves of the supervisor family and `pmpaddr` plus the two RV64-only rows; the RV32 machine-CSR test still passes and is not excluded.
    * Four findings. `minstret` had a consumer that is not architectural, RVFI's `order` field, so deleting the counter would have deleted the differential rig's instruction ordering with it; a model-internal `retire_count` register with no CSR address replaces it. The reservation flag on the memory API was never only a reservation flag: `res`/`con` selected the reserved-read and conditional-write kinds of the concurrency interface, and after `Zalrsc` its one caller is the AMO, whose two halves are exactly those, so the parameter is *derived* from `is_amo_access(access)` rather than deleted, and stops being passable independently of the access it describes. Two tests hang rather than fail and so cannot be left to a ctest timeout: `rv32si-p-dirty` and `rv64si-p-dirty`, whose handlers wait on a page-table A/D update that never comes with S off, which is the same behaviour c2 booked as a profile refusal now appearing where it blocks. And `pagesize_bits` survives the walker: PMA regions are still required to be 4 KiB-aligned so a PMA property cannot change inside one access, so the constant is renamed `pma_granule_bits` rather than deleted, under a name that no longer implies pages.

  * [x] **c4 · Vector fork** · 3.5 h actual · 0.3%
    * Removed `vstart` and, with scalar floating point, the whole coupling it was named beside: the `f0`–`f31` register file, `fcsr`/`frm`/`fflags`, `mstatus.FS`, the `F`/`D`/`Zfh`/`Zfhmin`/`Zfa`/`Zfinx`/`Zdinx`/`Zhinx`/`Zhinxmin` extensions with their instruction files, and the scalar half of the bf16 converts. The two forks the profile books (R-15-040, R-15-040a) are now the model's shape rather than a residue in its configuration: `Zve32f` no longer requires `F` and `Zve64d` no longer requires `D`, and the validator rule that demanded them is gone.
    * The vector unit keeps every floating-point form it had. The scalar operand of a `.vf` instruction is the low SEW bits of an **integer** register, which is where a soft-float-register calling convention already puts a floating-point scalar (R-15-039, R-15-040, [spec.md](spec.md) §15); `vfmv.f.s` writes its element zero-extended into one rather than NaN-boxed, there being no wider register to box into; and rounding is the static RNE of R-15-083, since RVV encodes no rounding-mode field and the conversions that name round-toward-zero pass it themselves.
    * Net change: 5,448 lines removed and 430 added across 56 files, nine deleted outright. The `c_emulator` harness is in that count: the GDB stub's floating-point annex, the `--trace-fpr` option, and the `freg`/`fcsr` accessors and write callback all named registers that no longer exist.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 261/261 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 129 keys ([tools/model.py config-keys](../tools/model.py)), 29 fewer than M0.6d's 158.
    * Expected profile refusals: 53 of 199 physical-variant tests, 52 refusals and the standing `rv64si-p-dirty` hang. This batch adds 24 to M0.6d's 29: eleven `rv64uf`, twelve `rv64ud`, and one more `rv64uzfh`, the test that had passed on F/D register moves while the residue stood. The ctest suite loses the same three families in both XLENs, 66 tests, which is the whole of 327 → 261.
    * Four findings. **The `.vf` forms had to be re-homed, not deleted**: ten instruction families name an `f` register upstream, and deleting the register file leaves the encodings needing a source, so the operand moves to the integer file and no encoding changes; the cost is that `vfmv.v.f` and `vfmv.s.f` become bit-for-bit duplicates of `vmv.v.x` and `vmv.s.x`, and `vfmv.f.s` differs from `vmv.x.s` only in zero- versus sign-extension, which is three encodings the freeze can reclaim and not a curation batch's call. **`fflags` has no destination and two comparisons still read it**: 44 accrual sites go, and the softfloat interface's flag half is discarded everywhere except `fp_gt`/`fp_ge`, where the invalid-operation flag *is* the result (a quiet comparison answers false on a NaN), so it is read where the interface returns it; the software-visible consequence is that no sticky IEEE flag is readable and a program that must detect an invalid or inexact result tests the result. **The scalar files were carrying the vector unit's classification predicates**, 33 of them (`f_is_*_{H,S,D}`) plus `negate_{H,S,D}`, and the Sail float library's width-generic `float_is_*` are the same tests, so each three-way dispatch collapses to one line and the negate to a sign-bit flip; only `canonical_NaN` and three flag constructors had to move, to [float_classify.sail](../model/model/core/float_classify.sail). And **the `vstart` cut deleted an implementation-compatibility hack**: the four scalar-move instructions computed an LMUL-based element count only to match the bound other implementations chose for the `vstart` check, so the count, the LMUL read, and the question go together; the whole-register load and store lose their partial-register prologue, and the vector-crypto element-group start collapses to zero.
    * Two residues, both booked rather than closed. R-15-039a states that a vector operation is all-or-nothing, and the model writes elements as it goes, so a fault at element *i* leaves the elements before it written with nothing recording where; on this machine that is a fail-stop program error rather than a resumable trap, but whether the register-group write should be buffered to make the claim literal is a question for the capability-fault path M0.6e authors and is settled there. And the dormant first-party test harness (`FIRST_PARTY_TESTS` is off) now names scalar-FP surface too, `test_bf16_nan_boxing.S` and the hard-float ISA strings every test compiles with, joining the PMP, `satp`, `minstret`, and LR/SC tests c1–c3 left stale; the Lean emulator's initializer is corrected here as c2 corrected it, but the first-party set wants one pass of its own.

* [x] **M0.6d · Close the CSR bank** · 2.5 h actual · 0.2%
  * Narrowed `mie`/`mip` to the machine-timer bits, hardwired the implementation identifiers to zero, made `misa` read-only, and deleted `Sdtrig`'s `tselect`. `MEIE`/`MEIP` and `MSIE`/`MSIP` are gone as *fields*: the `Minterrupts` bitfield carries `MTI` alone, so the unnamed bits of the underlying `xlenbits` are the hardwiring and no legalization can set them. `mip` becomes read-only outright, its one writer the timer comparator.
  * Net change: 455 lines removed and 135 added across 19 files, three deleted outright. Two stragglers of c3's delegation cut go with them, both dead rather than wrong: a CMake variable holding the delegatable-bits mask nothing has read since `mideleg` left, and the `WFI` comment stating an exemption over a register that no longer exists.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 327/327 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 158 keys ([tools/model.py config-keys](../tools/model.py)).
  * Expected profile refusals: 29 of 199 physical-variant tests, every one explained: ten `Zfh` tests the profile excludes, the three c1 cuts, the three c2 cuts, the standing M0.6b pair, c3's ten, and this batch's one, `mi-p-breakpoint`, which opens by writing `tselect` to count the triggers it may use. The sweep is now a repository instrument rather than a per-batch shell loop ([tools/model.py sweep](../tools/model.py)), which is also what classifies a hang as a refusal that blocks rather than leaving it to a ctest timeout.
  * Four findings. The trap on an unallocated CSR address was already total and needed no work: `is_CSR_accessible` defaults to false in `csr_end.sail`, so `check_CSR` rejects every address no clause claims, and c3's exclusion of `mi-p-pmpaddr` for trapping rather than reading zero is the standing evidence; what this batch adds to it is four more addresses, `tselect` and `tdata1-3`, whose name-map rows went with the register. Deleting the two interrupt *fields* deletes their two sources with them: the Simple Interrupt Generator device existed only to drive `MEIP`/`MSIP` and is gone entirely, and the CLINT's `msip` door with it, which leaves the CLINT as the machine timer and nothing else. The external-interrupt bit was also the sole reason `mip` read one value into `rd` and a different one into its own read-modify-write, so its deletion collapses `doCSR`'s two-value split into one and takes the platform-interrupt OR, the `XipReadType` enum, and `read_mip` with it. And two enumerations follow the same one-reachable-value rule c3 applied to `Privilege`: `InterruptType` keeps `I_M_Timer` alone, and `breakpoint_cause` goes entirely, `ebreak` being the only breakpoint source once the trigger module is deleted, which also removes a non-injective mapping whose two arms both encoded cause 3.

* [x] **M0.6e · Transplant CHERI capability semantics**
  * Port capability types and compression, merged registers, tag memory, PCC/sentries, machine trap capabilities, load/store checks, and ISAv9 trap causes.
  * Differentially compare RVFI-style traces with the M0.4 oracle.
  * The transplant carries ISAv9's **128-bit** parameterization rather than the frozen one, and that is the batch's shape rather than a delay: the M0.4 oracle is the differential reference, it is a reference only while the two models agree on the format, and what this batch owes M0.6f is an algebra whose field widths are the only thing that has to move (R-15-007a).
  * The extension hooks the transplant hangs off (`ext_data_get_addr`, `ext_control_check_pc`, `ext_fetch_check_pc`, `ext_check_CSR`) have been in their final, privilege-free shape since c3.

  * [x] **e1 · Fix XLEN at 64** · 1.2 h actual · 0.1%
    * `xlen` stops being a configuration key and becomes the constant 64, and the RV32 configuration goes with it: the generated `rv32*` configurations, the `--rv32` default and its embedded JSON, the RV32 test lanes in all three corpora, and the RV32 prover-backend target. The base is RV64 (R-15-001), no plain-RV32 target exists anywhere on the platform (R-18-002), and there is one model of one machine (R-15-005).
    * Net change: 385 lines removed and 254 added across 40 files. Deleted with the base: `mstatush`, which is the RV32 high half of `mstatus` and whose address is now unallocated and therefore trapping (R-15-014); the `Architecture` enumeration with its two-bit encoding and the `architecture()` and `in32BitMode()` queries over it, `misa.MXL` becoming the constant `0b10`; `Zbkb`'s RV32-only `zip` and `unzip`; the RV32 encodings of `zext.h` and `rev8`; the RV32 arms of the `Zbs` mask computation; and `asidlen`, left over from address translation. Thirty-seven vacuous `xlen == 64` guards collapse with them.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 128 keys ([tools/model.py config-keys](../tools/model.py)), one fewer than c4's 129, the key being `base.xlen`. The RV64 profile sweep is unchanged at 53 of 199, 52 refusals and the standing `rv64si-p-dirty` hang, which is the point of running it: the freeze removes no RV64 surface, and 261 ctest cases become 150 because the corpus carried an `rv32*` half for a machine this model can no longer be asked for.
    * Four findings. **The transplant forces this cut rather than merely profiting from it.** A register is a capability whose address field is the register width (R-15-007i), and the 128-bit encoding e2 ports has no 32-bit instantiation in this tree, so an RV32 configuration would be a machine with no capability format to run under; the first act of the transplant is therefore a deletion M0.6c did not reach. **Fixing XLEN changes the generated C++ interface, not only the model.** A configuration-dependent `bits(xlen)` is emitted as the length-carrying `sbits`, and a static one as a native `uint64_t`, so sixteen callback signatures in the emulator harness follow it, and the physical-address ones follow too because `physaddrbits_len` was conditional on XLEN as well; register, CSR, and PC values reach the harness as machine integers rather than as length-carrying structures. **`in32BitMode` was already unreachable and the enumeration under it nearly so**, which is the one-reachable-value rule c3 applied to `Privilege` and M0.6d to `InterruptType`, arriving here from the configuration rather than from a deleted mode. And **the RV32 half of the corpus was never evidence for this profile**: it exercised a base the profile does not have, so its removal costs no coverage, and every excluded RV64 row is still excluded for the reason it was.

  * [x] **e2 · Port the capability format and algebra** · 0.5 h actual · 0.0%
    * [cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail) carries the field widths and the packed encoding; [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) carries CHERI Concentrate over them: decode and encode, the null-capability memory transform, bounds, permissions, sealing, and the monotone derivations with their representability checks. 638 lines of Sail across three new files, the third being the unit test below, with the upstream licence vendored as `model/LICENCE.cheri` because the port derives from a BSD-2-Clause artifact this repository otherwise holds only as a submodule.
    * The capability layer lands in `core/` rather than under `extensions/`: the machine is purecap-only (R-15-001, R-18-002), so the capability *is* the register type, there is no hybrid mode to gate, and an extension gate would be a configuration key with one admitted value. The extension directory takes the instruction surface in e5.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass, and the eight properties in [test_capability.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_capability.sail) pass in the model's own `$[test]` harness: the all-zeroes granule decodes as untagged NULL and NULL stores back as all zeroes (R-15-182, R-15-060), encode and decode round-trip, the reset capability spans the address space, an aligned narrowing is exact and reads back, a five-byte narrowing is byte-exact (R-15-007c), an unrepresentable address move yields an untagged result rather than a trap (R-15-007h), sealing changes only the object type, and permissions round-trip.
    * Two findings. **The relocation guards are dead at the source and are not carried across.** `have_cheri_relocation` is a constant `false` upstream and both the PCC and DDC relocation paths hang off it, so the port folds them out rather than transplanting unreachable branches into a model that has no DDC to relocate. And **the tag plane needs no new machinery.** Sail 0.20's concurrency interface already carries a capability tag on its read and write requests, and the runtime the emulator links implements `emulator_read_tag` and `emulator_write_tag` behind it, so e3's tag memory is a parameter of the memory interface the curated tree already instantiates rather than the hand-wired side channel the upstream model carries.

  * [x] **e3 · Merge the register file and stand up the tag plane** · 0.5 h actual · 0.0%
    * `regtype` becomes `Capability`, so the file is merged by construction (R-15-007i): `X(r)` reads the address field, `C(r)` reads the whole register, and an integer write yields an untagged capability at that address, which is the merged file's discipline and the reason no capability is reconstructed from a bit pattern (R-05-136). Reset writes the null capability into all 31, because a machine whose registers hold authority cannot leave them undefined the way the base ISA leaves the integer file.
    * The validity tag becomes the metadata every physical access carries, keyed per granule, and the memory path needed no new plumbing for it: the modern tree already splits `mem_read_meta` from `mem_read` and `mem_write_value_meta` from `mem_write_value`, with the plain forms passing `default_meta`, so a data write clears the tag of every granule it covers for free, which is tag clearing as a property of the write path rather than an instruction (R-15-007r, R-15-115a).
    * Net change: 118 lines added over five files. Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; four more properties in the model's `$[test]` harness, that an integer write clears the authority, that the two readings are one register, that `x0` reads null whatever is written to it, and that the tag plane is granule-keyed and cleared across a wide write.
    * Finding: **the granule keying is the model's to do.** The Sail runtime stores one tag per address it is handed, and the concurrency interface's own tag field is keyed by the access address, so nothing below the model rounds an address to a granule. `__WriteRAM_Meta` shifts the address down by the capability size and writes every granule the access spans, which is what makes a tag belong to a granule rather than to a byte, and what makes a wide vector store clear all of them rather than one.

  * [x] **e4 · Port the checks, PCC and sentries, and the trap capabilities** · 0.7 h actual · 0.1%
    * PCC authorises every fetch and control transfer and the base register authorises every data access, so no unchecked path remains. `MTCC`, `MEPCC` and `MTDC` are the architectural trap state (R-15-073): a trap installs MTCC as the executing PCC and saves the interrupted PCC in MEPCC, `mret` unseals a sentry out of MEPCC into the executing PCC (R-15-008, R-15-071), and the integer `mtvec` and `mepc` CSRs become views of those two addresses. A capability violation raises ISAv9's cause 28 and reports the violation type and the register that raised it in `mtval` (R-15-073a). Privilege becomes the capability: `ext_check_CSR` and the `mret` check both read the access-system-registers permission off PCC (R-15-003).
    * Net change: 402 lines added and 131 removed over 13 files, two of them new. The page-table-walk accumulator and its error types go with them: they described a walker deleted in c3.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; the profile sweep is unchanged at 53 of 199; four more `$[test]` properties, that a trap saves PCC into MEPCC and installs MTCC, that `mret` unseals a sentry, that a fetch outside PCC's bounds and a fetch under an untagged PCC each raise their own violation, and that a store without store permission and a load through an untagged base register each fault where the access is rather than where the value was made.
    * Two findings. **The transplant cannot delete DDC on its own schedule, and the corpus is why.** Every test in `riscv-tests` addresses memory with an integer base register, so on a machine with no default data capability every load and store in the corpus faults at its base: the first build with DDC deleted hung five machine-mode tests on exactly that. Hybrid mode and DDC therefore stay until M0.6f, where the plan already puts their deletion, and the capability-mode arm beside them is the profile's own data path. What that schedules with it is M0.6f's exit criterion: after the cut, `riscv-tests` is not a corpus this machine can run, so the batch is gated on purecap test programs (M0.12) rather than on the suite that gates every batch before it. **The trap raise has to sit in the postlude.** A check is core surface because it reads PCC, but raising a trap calls the exception machinery in `sys`, which is compiled after `core`, so the three `ext_handle_*_error` hooks and the two privilege-failure hooks move to `postlude/cap_traps.sail`, the first point at which both halves are in scope.
  * [x] **e5 · Port the instruction surface and stand up the differential harness** · 3 h actual · 0.3%
    * The ISAv9 capability instructions land in `extensions/CHERI/`, ungated as the format and algebra in `core` are: the capability-mode control transfers (`auipcc`, `cjal`, `cjalr`) behind a second decode mapping, the two mode-independent jumps and `cinvoke`, the eleven inspection instructions, the monotone derivations with the three `csetbounds` forms sharing one narrowing, `cclear`, `cspecialrw` over the bank the profile keeps, the explicit-authority data loads and stores, the capability loads and stores, and `cloadtags`. 999 lines of Sail across two new files, plus the plumbing the second decode needs.
    * Three ISAv9 groups are **not** carried across, each because the machine no longer has what they act on rather than because the profile excludes them: `FPClear` clears a scalar floating-point file c4 deleted; `LoadRes*`, `StoreCond*`, and the sub-word `lr`/`sc` decodings ISAv9 adds to the base need a reservation c3 deleted with `Zalrsc`; and `AMOSwapCap` is a capability-width atomic swap where `A` is narrowed to `Zaamo` + `Zabha` at byte, halfword, word, and doubleword (R-15-024), so the width it names is not one the adopted atomic surface carries.
    * The differential rig is [tools/model.py](../tools/model.py) `trace-diff`, over the trace dialects and adjudication [tools/vos/trace.py](../tools/vos/trace.py) holds: either executor's dialect becomes one record stream, the two are aligned on the first program counter the curated model retires, and the report names where they stop agreeing. The record set is the intersection of what the two print today, a retire, a register-write address, and a data read.
    * Net change: 1,940 lines added and 35 removed across 13 files, six of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; the profile sweep is unchanged at 53 of 199; the ten new properties in [test_cheri_insts.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_cheri_insts.sail) pass, bringing the model's own harness to 28; and the rig runs the 54-member `rv64ui-p-*` corpus with an agreeing prefix of 97 records on every member.
    * Four findings. **The read path was discarding the tag.** e3 keyed the tag plane by granule and wired it into the write path, but `checked_mem_read` returned `default_meta` unconditionally, an upstream `TODO` about folding metadata across splits, so no capability could ever be loaded with its tag set; e5 is the first batch with an instruction that reads one, which is what surfaced it. The fold is now the conjunction over the splits, which for an unsplit access is that granule's tag exactly and for a split one is the conservative answer at the same polarity as the write path's clearing of every granule it covers. **`lc` collides with `cbo.zero`.** ISAv9 puts the capability load at MISC-MEM `funct3` 010 and the cache-block operations are there too, separated only by a zero destination and a small immediate; upstream never meets them because it does not implement `Zicboz`, which the profile adopts (R-15-060). The destination separates them here, a capability load into the null register discarding what it loaded, and the frozen dialect re-encodes this anyway (R-15-007), so what is owed at the freeze is a decision and not a repair. **The two executors neither start at the same instruction nor report a register write at the same width.** The oracle enters through a reset-vector ROM at 0x1000 this platform has no counterpart for, so the streams align on the first PC the curated model retires rather than on the step number; and the oracle prints the whole capability on a register write where this side prints the address alone, which [core/regs.sail](../model/model/core/regs.sail) already books as M0.12's to widen. The store side of the memory trace is not comparable at all, the oracle emitting no store line. And **`riscv-tests` cannot reach agreement past its own prologue, which is a fact about the corpus rather than about the transplant.** Each test points `mtvec` at the end of a group and then writes `medeleg`, which is an illegal instruction on a machine c3 stripped of delegation, so the trap skips the group the oracle retires and both machines reach the same result by different routes. The prefix is 97 on all 54 members, so the regression the rig carries today is that it must not *shorten*, which `--floor` enforces; a corpus both executors run in lockstep is M0.12's.
    * One refactor the surface forced, and it removes a duplicate rather than adding one: the explicit-authority loads and stores name their own authority where an ordinary access lets the mode pick it, so [core/addr_checks.sail](../model/model/core/addr_checks.sail) splits into `cap_mode_auth`, which selects, and `cap_data_checks`, which decides, with `ext_data_get_addr` composing the two.

* [x] **M0.6f · Re-parameterize to 64+1 bits** · 5 h actual · 0.4%
  * The format is [isa-profile.md](isa-profile.md) §4.1's and spends the 64 bits exactly: a 36-bit address in the low bits, a 4-bit object type, a 5-bit permission field, a 5-bit exponent, and 8- and 6-bit mantissas with the top's high two derived, the validity tag outside them and the tag plane's granule narrowing with the capability to 64 bits (R-15-007, R-15-002a, R-15-203). No reserved field, no software-defined permission field, no capability-mode flag, and no revocation colour appears, each needing a bit the table has already spent (R-15-007n, R-15-001c, R-08-004b).
  * **Permissions become an enumerated lattice rather than one bit per permission**, which is the one place the narrowing changes the algebra (R-15-007b). Bit 4 of the field is local versus global, orthogonal to authority; the other four name sixteen shapes, from the bottom element through the read and read-write data shapes, the three transitivity levels a capability load passes on, the stack's `store-local`, the execute shapes, the privileged code root, and the two sealing authorities. The decode is **total**, so the field has no illegal encoding to reject; the two lattice exclusions are finite checks over the table rather than properties of one distribution, no admitted set holding both store and execute (R-15-007l) and none holding both seal and unseal (R-15-007o); and what software sees is an *expanded* 12-bit bitmap, which is what lets `candperm`'s mask stay an ordinary bitwise operand over a non-orthogonal field, its result the largest admitted set inside the intersection. R-15-074's `load-global` and `load-mutable` transitivity arrives with the permissions that express it, applied on the load path and exact rather than rounded: the read half of every admitted set is itself an admitted set.
  * Removed with hybrid mode: `DDC` and its `cspecialrw` number, its `mtval` register index, the capability-mode flag with the second decode mapping and its `NOT_CAPMODE` wildcard, `cgetflags`/`csetflags`, the four explicit-authority data forms and the four explicit-authority capability forms, ISAv9's `cclear`, and the two mode-independent jumps. Removed on their own grounds: the reconstruction group (`cbuildcap`, `ccopytype`, `ccseal`, and the `cgethigh`/`csethigh` pair), the runtime bounds-rounding group (`cram`, `crrl`, `csetboundsexact`), `ctestsubset`, `ccleartag`, `cinvoke`, and the software-defined permission field. Six cause codes go with the mechanisms they named.
  * Net change: 1,125 lines added and 1,255 removed across 23 files, one deleted outright, `extensions/I/jalr_seq.sail`, whose one clause was the integer reading of `jalr`.
  * Exit evidence: build green; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 128 keys, unchanged, the re-parameterization touching no configuration key; and the model's own `$[test]` harness passes at **36 properties against 28**, thirteen of them new, covering the field budget, decode's totality over the encoding, the permission enumeration's totality and its two exclusions, narrowing's monotonicity over every codepoint and every single-bit mask, the split root set, the frozen object-type space, the out-of-range address, the sentry split at `cjalr`, and the load's transitivity.
  * Five findings.
    **The exponent field carries the case flag, because the table leaves no bit beside it.** CHERI Concentrate spends a bit saying whether the exponent is stored inside the mantissas; here the exponent is an explicit five-bit field, so the flag rides the field itself, IEEE-754 fashion: zero is the denormal case and *k* + 1 is the normalized case at effective exponent *k*. The effective exponent tops out at 30, so *k* + 1 tops out at 31 and the five bits are spent exactly. The packing is forced by the field table rather than chosen, and it costs no mantissa bits where upstream's costs three.
    **The byte-exact threshold the frozen widths deliver is 128 bytes and the padding above it is one part in 64 per side, and R-15-007c is amended to them.** The exponent is chosen so the decoder knows the length mantissa's top two bits, which is exactly what lets the top mantissa be stored in six bits and derived into eight, so an exponent of zero reaches half the mantissa's span rather than all of it. The register's 256 bytes and one-part-in-256 read off the mantissa width alone, where both figures are the encoding's; the same two bits separate each from the width. Both are now held as properties of the model over an unaligned base, so a later change to the widths or to the exponent's packing cannot move them silently, and every consumer of the pair moves with it, R-15-007e and R-15-007f's admissibility argument for the indexed load and store (which the narrower threshold **strengthens**), R-15-007k's relocated rounding, R-08-004b's closed bit budget, and the alternatives register's declined bounds-check elision.
    **An integer register is 64 bits and an address is 36, so a register's integer reading is its whole 64 data bits, and R-15-007i is amended to say so.** The address field is their low 36, which keeps "an integer operand is that register's address field" true of every integer that *is* an address (R-15-002a) and leaves the rest of the width to RV64I's arithmetic; the reading at the address width, which the register's wording implied, would truncate every integer write to 36 bits and leave the machine without XLEN arithmetic at all. Nothing is forged by the wider reading: an integer write is untagged, so the encoding's other fields carry data and never authority, and the round trip rests on decode being total over the 64 bits, which R-15-007b's permission enumeration and R-15-007a's bounds characterization are what supply. The null-capability transform the memory interface applies is applied to the register reading too, so `x0` reads integer zero and a register stored with `sd` and reloaded with `lc` is the capability it was. The one root is likewise a **pair**: W+X being unrepresentable, reset hands PCC the execute side and `c1` the store side, and the store side carries `store-local` because `candperm` can only remove (R-15-007p).
    **The corpus goes with DDC, and it goes entirely rather than family by family.** Every program in `riscv-tests` addresses memory with an integer base register, which a purecap machine reads as an untagged capability and faults on, so eleven extension-by-extension filters collapse into one ground and the suite is excluded from registration. The profile sweep, which asks a different question, still runs it and now returns 199 refusals of 199 against 53. The differential rig loses its second executor at the same edge and for the same reason M0.6e booked: an oracle is a reference only where it implements the same machine, and the prefix falls from 97 to 67 on all 54 members, which is the prologue's first load. The rig outlives the oracle; its standing second executor is M2's CHERI-QEMU fork and its corpus is M0.12's.
    **Membership is by enumeration, which reaches four instructions and one otype decision this item's own list does not name.** Nothing enters the profile by inheritance (R-15-007j), so `cinvoke` goes with the call gate `cjalr` replaces (R-15-068), ISAv9's `cclear` with the split file its two quadrants address (R-15-069b), and the two mode-independent jumps with the second mode that gave them a reason. The same rule decides the object-type space, which is frozen here: three of the sixteen codepoints are reserved, the unsealed one and **two** sentry edges, because R-15-071's forward/backward split is a reservation in that space and not a later amendment to it. `cjalr` enforces the split by role, a jump that writes a link entering only a forward edge and a return address reachable only by one that writes none, and `csealentry` mints the forward edge alone, so a return capability is never software's to forge.

* [x] **M0.6g · Add profile rows absent upstream** · 4 h actual · 0.3%
  * The six rows land as one batch: `cmovz`/`cmovn` in the dialect's own encoding, the revocation plane with its filter and its bitmap, `fence.t`, the capability indexed load and store, `cclear`, and the machine-level AIA pending array. `cloadtags` needed nothing, being ISAv9's and already carrying the two parameters the profile fixes (M0.6e, e5). No measurement-conditioned provisional row is modeled, and the single-check multi-register save is struck rather than deferred (R-15-036n).
  * **The three custom-opcode rows spend one opcode between them.** ISAv9 already spent custom-2 on the whole capability surface, and custom opcode space is exactly what the freeze's single measured act makes the remaining candidates compete for (R-15-014a, R-15-067e), so `cld`, `csd` and `cclear` take custom-0 separated by `funct3` and leave custom-1 and custom-3 whole; the indexed pair carries `ld`'s and `sd`'s own width code, so the width reads off the three bits the base ISA puts it in. `cmovz`/`cmovn` take the dialect's encoding rather than that space and so contend for nothing, at the first free `funct7` pair above ISAv9's highest three-operand allocation.
  * Net change: 1,845 lines added and 46 removed across 23 files, eight of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **43 properties against 36**, seven of them new; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at **15 of 15** with every trace digest matching, four members and 37 checks new and the eleven existing digests unmoved; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Five findings. **The revocable interval's width is the model's to bound and the covered union is a predicate rather than a list.** R-08-005a sizes the payload at Σ|I|/64 bytes and charges it against the SRAM capacity budget, which is a composition property, where a Sail register is a fixed width; so the plane fixes 4096 granules, `validate_config` refuses a composition that does not fit rather than letting its top granules fall silently outside the filter, and a second interval is a clause in `revocation_covered` rather than a mechanism, every consumer quantifying over the predicate. **Deleting the AIA indirect interface is what puts the pending array in the address map.** Upstream an interrupt file exposes `seteipnum` to senders and leaves the pending bits to `miselect`/`mireg`/`mtopei`; with those excluded the array has nowhere else to be, and *software reads pending bits with ordinary loads* is R-15-065's own sentence, so the file has two doors and the receiver's is also how a bit is cleared. **`fence.t`'s Sail content is the enumeration and not the barrier**: the flush set is one structure and this model has no store buffer, so what the file carries is why every other structure is absent from the set, and its reserved fields trap where `fence`'s are ignored, this instruction having no future extension to reserve them for. **The generated test-matrix configurations are a second declaration of the platform**, and two new keys broke the build at schema conformance on `rv64d_*.json` rather than on the profile's own configuration: a key added to one and not to [config.json.in](../model/config/config.json.in) is a build failure rather than a divergence found later. And **the model's own harness runs under the generated *max* configuration rather than under the profile's**, so a property that reads a configured policy is a property about the wrong machine: an assertion that a misaligned indexed load faults is the profile's answer and not that configuration's, which defers misalignment to the PMA. Profile-conditional behaviour belongs in the corpus, which is what the corpus runs under.
  * One repair outside the model, because the gate could not otherwise run: [typecheck.py](../tools/typecheck.py) looked for its pinned `ty` and `ruff` beside the running interpreter, which is where a POSIX interpreter and a Windows virtual environment put console scripts and not where a Windows *system* install puts them, so a stock host install read as *not installed* however many times it was installed. A pinned-version gate whose failure mode is indistinguishable from an absent tool is a gate that gets skipped, so it takes both directories.

* [x] **M0.6h · Add the three bespoke block instructions** · 5 h actual · 0.4%
  * `vmclear` (R-15-069d), `creclaim` (R-15-007s), and `cbo.scrub` (R-15-177a) land as one batch, none of them inherited from either upstream model. `vmclear` and `cbo.scrub` join `fence.t` under `extensions/platform/`; `creclaim` lands beside `cloadtags`, whose authority checks it shares and whose group it walks.
  * **The four custom-opcode rows still spend one opcode between them.** `vmclear` takes custom-0 `funct3` 001 beside `cclear`'s 000 and below the indexed access's width-coded 011 and 111, so custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). The other two contend for none of that space: `creclaim` takes the first sub-opcode of ISAv9's two-operand group that ISAv9 never allocated, so a reader checking it against ISAv9 finds free space rather than a re-used opcode; `cbo.scrub` takes MISC-MEM `funct3` 010 at immediate 5, at a zero destination as every cache-block operation has, rather than one of the three points `Zicbom` vacated, because reusing a named standard encoding makes a stock disassembler print the wrong instruction where an unallocated one makes it print none.
  * Net change: 1,172 lines added and 53 removed across 17 files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **46 properties against 43**, three of them new; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at **17 of 17** with two members and 19 checks new and the fifteen existing digests unmoved; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    **The vector gate is the class and not the context, and running it is what found that.** R-15-069d's word is *vectorless class*, which is a static property of the hart, and the model has two predicates that both read as "V is available": `hartSupports(Ext_Zve32x)`, which is that property, and `currentlyEnabled(Ext_Zve32x)`, which folds in `mstatus.VS != Off`. The first build gated on the second and failed its own decode assertion at a reset whose `VS` is `Off`. The repair is not cosmetic: `VS` is writable by the executing partition, so a partition could dirty the vector unit, write the gate off, and leave the switch's `vmclear` trapping on exactly the residue it exists to erase (R-07-014a, R-15-214); it would also make the clear conditional in the sense R-15-069c rejects, the switcher having to read `VS` before issuing it. Nothing in the clause writes `mstatus` either, the extension-context gate being the switch's to restore over every register a partition can name (R-07-015).
    **The scrub had to become an arm of the access type rather than a convention of its caller.** `CacheAccess` carried one arm, and the four matches over it were wildcards or single-arm, which was exactly right while `cbo.zero` was the only cache-block operation. The scrub differs from it in both directions: it reads *and* writes, so it needs both permissions where `cbo.zero` needs store alone, or a holder of a read-only view could cause a write to memory it cannot write; and it verifies an error-detecting code, so it belongs over main memory rather than over whatever `supports_cbo_zero` admits, a device register having no codeword to scrub. Both distinctions are properties of the operation, so `CB_scrub` is a second arm and the four matches gain a case each.
    **The welded block size stopped belonging to `cloadtags`.** It was defined beside the first instruction that needed it; four instructions in three modules now read it, so it moves to `core/cap_format.sail` beside the granule it is a multiple of. That is also where it is legible as the parameter M0.13 scores against two geometries at once (R-15-007q, R-15-014a (vii)): a value satisfying one consumer and not another is a defect rather than a choice, which is easier to see when the consumers share a declaration than when one of them owns it.
    **`cbo.scrub`'s telemetry and fail-stop have no architectural state to hang on, and are not invented here.** This model has no error model: no codeword carries a syndrome, so nothing is ever correctable and nothing is ever uncorrectable, and a fault-injection register would model the harness rather than the machine. What the file carries instead is where each half lands. Correction telemetry goes to the sentinel, which is a device and a core class rather than a structure the scrubbing hart can read, which is also what makes correction invisible in the timing (R-15-179, M0.8). And the fail-stop needs no new channel when it arrives: the block read already returns `MemoryOpResult`, so a detection joins the path a PMA refusal takes and the clause changes by a case rather than by a mechanism.
    **What the scrub *is* in an architectural model is the tag-preserving pass, and that is only visible against the write path.** An ordinary store of a granule's own bits back to it clears that granule's validity tag, tag clearing being a property of the write path rather than an instruction (R-15-007r), so maintenance of a capability-bearing array through the store path would destroy the authority in it. That is the whole reason the scrub is a block operation of its own rather than a load-and-store loop, and it is what [platform-scrub.s](../corpus/platform-scrub.s) checks by doing both to the same block.
    **`creclaim`'s clear lands in memory where the load filter's lands in a delivered value**, and the two are the reclamation half and the containment half of one protocol rather than two spellings of one act. Clearing a revocation bit puts the object back in service for the filter, and does not for `creclaim`: the tag is gone from the granule rather than from a value (R-08-007a). The clause reads each granule as **bits and a tag** rather than as a decoded capability and writes back the bits it read, so the decode feeds the predicate alone, the surviving granules are untouched bit for bit, and a cleared one differs in its tag and nothing else, which is what keeps R-15-007r's exclusion of a *bulk* tag clear undisturbed.

#### Remaining M0 deliverables

* [x] **M0.7 · Model Ztso and static-only prediction** · 3.5 h actual · 0.3%
  * The model stops being silent about its memory model and states it. `Ext_Ztso` enters the extension registry ([core/extensions.sail](../model/model/core/extensions.sail)) hardwired `true` and read from no configuration key, which is the requirement rather than an oversight: R-15-004 adopts Ztso normatively in place of RVWMO, so a key would make the memory model a composition parameter and admit a composition of this machine that is not this machine. It is the one row in that registry that is not a `config` read, and `hartSupports` is what the generated `riscv,isa` string and `riscv,isa-extensions` array are built from, so an attested devicetree now names the model where it named nothing.
  * **`fence` collapses to a drain or a no-op and `barrier_kind` collapses with it.** The eleven-member enumeration ([core/phys_mem_interface.sail](../model/model/core/phys_mem_interface.sail)) becomes one, `Barrier_RISCV_drain`: nine were the RVWMO predecessor and successor lattice R-15-017 deletes, `Barrier_RISCV_tso` was `fence.tso`'s and that instruction is now a bare retire, and `Barrier_RISCV_i` had been unreachable since `Zifencei` left at c1. `fence.t` emits the same member, so the flush set and the drain are one structure rather than two spellings (R-15-213), and the difference between the two instructions is the condition rather than the effect.
  * **`fm` stops being an operand.** It is the literal `0b0000` in the `FENCE` encdec, so every reserved value reaches no clause and traps (R-15-014, R-15-017), and the drain predicate reads the I and O bits directly as one mask per side rather than as an arm of a 256-case lattice.
  * Net change: 499 lines added and 173 removed across twelve files, two of them new and one deleted.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **56 properties against 52**, four of them new; `verifiedos.json`'s key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **166 keys, unchanged**, no key added because the memory model is not configurable; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 2,741 bytes against 2,733, inside the 4 KiB ROM region it is written to, and naming `ztso` in both renderings; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at **20 of 20** with one member and 8 checks new, the manifest at version 4, and the nineteen existing digests unmoved; the checker green on all 57 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    **The item's honest reach is small, and stating it is part of the deliverable rather than a caveat on it.** `sail_barrier` is a no-op in the C backend, no emulator source names a barrier kind, and the model runs one hart, so nothing about ordering is observable end to end. The nineteen existing trace digests being unmoved by a batch that rewrote the fence is the measurement of that, not a coincidence. What the model now does is *state* the memory model; R-15-016 puts the guarantee itself on the RTL side over the store buffer and the fabric beneath it, and this batch does not touch it. The one architecturally observable change in the whole item is the reserved-`fm` trap.
    **That trap is the profile diverging from the base ISA's semantics rather than from its encoding, and it is a deleted rule rather than an added check.** The base ISA instructs an implementation to treat every reserved `fm` as `fm`=0000 and execute it, for forward compatibility with fences a later extension might define. R-15-014 refuses that for the whole encoding space. So `fm` leaves the `FENCE` union and becomes a literal the encdec matches, and the reserved values trap by reaching no clause: no arm was written to reject them, and the decoder does with them exactly what it does with any unallocated word.
    **The drain condition reads the I and O bits, which is what lets `is_fiom_active` go.** R-15-017 states the surviving semantics over `PW` or `PO` and `PR` or `PI`. Upstream ignores I and O in `fence`'s execute and made the implication conditional on the `FIOM` bit of `menvcfg`/`senvcfg`, both of which left with the mode they gated at c3, leaving a function hardwired to `false` with one caller. The unconditional reading that replaces it is strictly stronger than the bit ever made it, so nothing is lost; but it is a semantic divergence from upstream `fence` and not only an encoding one, and it belongs in front of a reviewer rather than in a diff.
    **One enumeration member had been dead since c1 and nothing said so, which is the shape of defect the narrowing removes rather than the one it found.** `Barrier_RISCV_i` lost its only constructor site when `Zifencei` was excluded, and an enum member with no site is not an error in Sail or anywhere else. Narrowing to one member also orphaned [handwritten_support/riscv_extras_sequential.lem](../model/handwritten_support/), which names all eleven constructors and which no `CMakeLists.txt` in the tree references: it is scaffolding of the RMEM lane c1 retired, and it goes with the members it names. The `hgen/` herdtools directory beside it is unreferenced on the same footing and names no barrier constructor, so it is booked here rather than deleted on this batch's authority.
    **Static-only prediction adds no Sail at all, and what the model gains is a citation.** A search of the model tree for predictor state returns nothing, which is the state R-15-019 requires and also the state an unwritten requirement would leave, so from inside the tree the two are indistinguishable. R-15-021 says why that is correct rather than a gap: the deletion is discharged structurally by rows A-04 through A-06 of the [absence contract](absence-contract.md) and not by RTL refinement, because Sail carries architectural state and no model can state *there is no branch predictor* (R-15-098). So the row numbers land in [fence_t.sail](../model/model/extensions/platform/fence_t.sail), the one file in the tree that already made the claim, and a reader who follows it arrives at the register rather than at the claim.
    **One residue is named for the auditor who runs the absence contract's search rather than left to be re-derived as harmless.** `model/test/first_party/src/common/encoding.h` still carries three `MATCH_PREFETCH_*` macros for the excluded `Zicbop`, inside the vendored riscv-tests header that the dormant first-party harness reads. It is not model surface and it is not this batch's to remove, but it is what a grep for prefetch finds first.
* [ ] **M0.8 · Parameterize by core class**
  * Split at entry, and the split is the C-class freeze's: M0.8a is the class table and the roster, which is all the freeze needs from the parameterization and adds no decode surface, and M0.8b to M0.8d are the datapaths those classes name, deferred until before M2.3 as the cell always said. They are parallel with each other because they touch different extension directories, and each is one build configuration of the same model rather than a fork of it (R-15-005).

  * [x] **M0.8a · The core roster and the class table** · 7 h actual · 0.6%
    * The classes land as composition-time declarations and nothing else: [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) carries R-15-113's five-class enumeration, the per-class datapath record, and the roster `mhartid` indexes; both enter the attested devicetree R-09-007 puts them in ([postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)); and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface**: the class is not architectural state and no CSR reports it, one kernel binary running unmodified on every class and selecting its per-hart state, the core's class, and the island binding out of `mhartid` against that devicetree at boot (R-15-052b, R-07-012, R-04-009).
    * **Counts are derived and declared nowhere** (R-15-113). The roster enumerates harts and a class's count is the number of its entries, so the reference instantiation's six C, four plus two V, two M, one S and one RoT is a claim about the composed roster with exactly one thing to be checked against. `frozen` beside the numbers does what `qualified` does beside the memory classes' (M0.14): R-15-108's exploration selects VLEN per class, core count, issue width, pipeline depth and scratchpad size, it has not run, and a table carrying R-15-113's reference figures with nothing separating them from selected ones is how a placeholder becomes an architectural input.
    * **K-60** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py), over the new [vos/coreclass.py](../tools/vos/coreclass.py), holds the table itself. Four artifacts write it, three of them prose in three different idioms, and the rule requires every class's vector geometry to be one figure across all four and every count the specification states to be the composed roster's. Report-only and never repaired, for K-57's two reasons: the composition is under a `-text` tree, and which geometry a class takes is R-15-108's exploration rather than arithmetic.
    * Net change: 1,522 lines added and 32 removed across 22 files, three of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **59 properties against 56**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **228 keys against 166**; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at **21 of 21** with one member and 7 checks new and the twenty existing digests unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green, the generated tree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 4,466 bytes and accepted by the emulator's own bound against the region it is written into, and refused by that bound against the region it had; each of the **fourteen** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on **all 62 rules against 61**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean.
    * Seven findings.
      **`mhartid` was a selector into a table that did not exist.** R-15-052b's criterion is that the reachable value space is a composition-time constant fixed by the core roster, so a hart identity outside the roster is not a value the machine can present. The model had the selector and nothing selected from: `platform.hartid` was read, hardwired into a register, and held against nothing, so any composition could declare any identity and the criterion had no instrument at all. That is the shape M0.13 found for the welded block size and M0.17 for the bank grant, arriving here from a criterion rather than from a number, and the roster is what closes it.
      **The class table cannot derive the vector geometry, so the binding is a refusal.** `vlen_exp` in [core/vlen.sail](../model/model/core/vlen.sail) is a **type**-level configuration: it sizes the vector register file, so one build realizes one VLEN and a value-level record cannot compute it, nor can the machine derive its VLEN from its class. What is left is a refusal, a composition whose roster puts this hart on a class declaring some other geometry being rejected before it runs, and the generated configurations' template binds the C row to their own exponent so all eight of them stay consistent with their own class table rather than passing only in the profile.
      **The `Zvl` ladder stopped at 1024 and would have described a V-class build as narrower than it is.** Upstream stops at `Zvl1024b` because upstream stops there; this profile's V class is VLEN=4096 and its M class VLEN=1024. A truncated ladder is invisible today, every rung above 1024 reading false at every geometry this model is built at, and would have surfaced at M0.8b as a generated `riscv,isa` string and an attested devicetree understating the machine's own guarantee. Both rungs land here with the `currentlyEnabled` clauses the scattered function needs, and the generated tree is unmoved.
      **The `cpu` node hardwired hart zero.** `reg = <0>` and `cpu@0` were literals in the emitter while `mhartid` was configurable, so a composition at any other identity would have generated a devicetree describing hart zero to a machine that is not it. Both now follow `plat_hartid`, and the label stays `CPU0` because it is a DTS label rather than an identity and the interrupt-controller reference names it. Every configuration this tree ships is at zero, which is what makes the repair free and is also what hid it.
      **The roster is one property and not sixteen nodes, and both halves of that are forced.** A devicetree property carries cells or a string and not both, so the class column is an index the class nodes emit beside their own names, which puts the decoder in the tree with the thing it decodes. And a `cpu` node asserts a hart the emulator runs, which is one (R-15-005), so sixteen of them would describe cores that are not there and would not fit the 4 KiB ROM region besides.
      **A devicetree source is a grammar and not a rendering, and `dtc` is what says so.** Two shapes that read correctly as text are errors in it: juxtaposed cell arrays are not a concatenation but a syntax error, and a property written after a subnode is refused outright rather than reordered. Both were written the wrong way first and neither is visible from inside the model, whose emitter is string concatenation and typechecks either way. That is why the compile is in the exit evidence rather than the generation, and why the size is measured on the blob rather than on the source.
      **The tree outgrew the region it is written into and nothing said so, because the bound was on a path no ordinary run takes.** The blob went from 2,741 bytes to 4,466 against a 4 KiB ROM. The emulator does carry the bound, `dtb_within_configured_pma_memory`, but it sits on the `--device-tree-blob` path and `--validate-config` exits before reaching it, so a *generated* tree that no longer fits was silent on every build, every corpus run, and every sweep. Three things follow. The ROM goes to 16 KiB, because 4 KiB is upstream's spike-bare default rather than a budget anybody set and R-09-007 has this tree declare five things of which exactly one has now arrived. The per-class core count comes back *out* of the tree, the roster being three properties above it and a count beside a roster being one fact written twice inside a single attested artifact. And the measurement stops being a hand check: `model.py devicetree` generates, compiles, and hands the blob to the emulator's own bound rather than to a second copy of it, which costs one corpus member's ELF because that bound runs just ahead of the program load. It was fired in both directions before landing.

  * [x] **M0.8b · V-class long-vector datapath and per-element capability checks** · 6.5 h actual · 0.6%
    * The class at VLEN=4096 runs, and the capability semantics of a vector access are stated on every clause of the surface that has one. **No instruction, no encoding, no register, and no CSR**: the datapath was already in the model and gated by the geometry, and what this item adds is the mask, the citations, and the evidence (R-15-115, R-15-115a, R-15-115b, R-08-003).
    * **The V class is a *configuration* and not a build, and that is the reverse of what M0.8a's finding implied.** [config/verifiedos-v.json](../model/config/verifiedos-v.json) is [verifiedos.json](../model/config/verifiedos.json) with two keys moved, the composed hart and the vector geometry that hart's class declares, and the same emulator binary runs both: there is exactly one Sail model parameterized by core class (R-15-005), so a second core of the composed roster is a second file rather than a second tree. The refusal M0.8a wrote still bites and still says what it should, because one *run* realizes one VLEN.
    * **Only an **active** element raises, and the masked-off element's check is performed with it and discarded** (R-15-115b). [extensions/V/vext_mem_checks.sail](../model/model/extensions/V/vext_mem_checks.sail) carries the two functions that took: `vector_body_inactive`, which separates the elements the vector mask turns off from the two kinds of tail the model's one mask folds in with them, and `vector_masked_element_check`, which runs the element's own check through `ext_data_get_addr` and drops the answer. Seven of the ten clauses of the vector memory surface have a mask and take it; the whole-register pair and the mask pair carry `vm` as a literal one and say in place why they owe nothing.
    * **K-65** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) is what the second configuration owed: it holds the two shipped files to the same 199 values and to differing in exactly the two that name the core each composes, in both directions. A key that drifted makes the second file a different machine; a declared divergence that stopped diverging makes it the first machine under another name, and the V-class evidence measured against it would be evidence about the C class.
    * Net change: 1,831 lines added and 21 removed across 18 files, five of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **68 properties against 62**, six of them new; `verifiedos-v.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys**, the same figure `verifiedos.json` agrees at; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at **23 of 23** with **216 checks against 193**, two members new and the twenty-one existing digests unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes; the checker green on **all 65 rules against 64**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
    * Seven findings.
      **The type-level configuration is compile-time for the provers and load-time for the emulator, and only the second half was written down.** M0.8a found that `vlen_exp` in [core/vlen.sail](../model/model/core/vlen.sail) is type-level and concluded that one build realizes one VLEN. That holds of the SMT, Rocq, Lean and Lem lanes, which take `--config` on the Sail command line and bake the geometry into the artifact. It does not hold of the C++ lane, which takes no `--config` at emission at all: the emitted model reads the configuration when it loads one, and `sizeof(vlen_exp)` answers 8 or 12 according to which file it was handed. The validator's own refusal says *this model was built at 2^8*, which is a sentence about the configuration and not about the build. Nothing was wrong with the refusal, and the conclusion beside it was one word too strong.
      **The model has one inactive-element mask and R-15-115b's obligation attaches to a third of it.** `init_masked_result` and `init_masked_source` return a single bit vector that is clear for three unrelated reasons: the element is past `vl`, it is past a fractional LMUL's real element count, or `v0` turns it off. Only the third is a *mask*, and only the third is what R-15-085 forbids observing: `vl` and `vtype` are architectural registers a partition reads back, so an operation shorter than VLMAX leaks nothing by being shorter. Checking every inactive element would have made the discarded check a function of `vl` and put the tail into the memory traffic, which is a cost with no obligation behind it. The separation is derived from the existing function rather than restated, `init_masked_source` under an all-ones mask being exactly *in the body*, because a second copy of the tail arithmetic is how the two would come to disagree.
      **The model pads every masked-off access and not only the failing one, which is stronger than the requirement rather than short of it, and it is forced.** R-15-115b pads the *failing* masked-off access so that an address the domain holds no authority for never reaches the fabric while the bank cycle is still spent.
