This model executes no cycles, so an access issued here would contribute no timing and would instead contribute exactly what a masked-off element must not have: a store would write memory, and a load of a device endpoint would be a read with a side effect. The cycle both cases spend is [core/timing.sail](../model/model/core/timing.sail)'s `Timing_VectorMemUnit` and `Timing_VectorMemIndexed` rows (M0.9, R-15-085a), which is where a latency lives on this machine. The consequence for the evidence is worth stating rather than eliding: a discarded check has no architecturally observable result, so the property asserts both halves against one address, the operation retiring and the same check at the same address reporting the violation it did not raise.
      **Two of the item's four requirements were already satisfied and nothing said so.** Every clause of the vector memory surface already reached memory through `data_read`/`data_write`, which compose `ext_data_get_addr` over `cap_data_checks` ([core/addr_checks.sail](../model/model/core/addr_checks.sail)), so R-08-003's per-element check has been in the model since the e5 transplant and a vector-specific check would have been a second copy of it. R-15-115a's tag clear is likewise the write path's: `mem_write_value` carries `default_meta` down to `__WriteRAM_Meta`, which clears every granule the access spans, partial-granule writes included (R-15-007r, M0.6e e3). What was missing was not the mechanism but the statement, the citation, and anything that would notice either going.
      **The extension-context dirty fired once per element and put the geometry into the commit trace.** `wV` calls `dirty_v_context` on every element it writes, and that function wrote `mstatus` and fired the CSR callback unconditionally, so one vector load emitted VLEN/SEW `mstatus` records: `vector-memory` traced 585 records at VLEN=256 and 1,425 at VLEN=4096 for the same program doing the same thing, which makes a member's digest a fact about which class ran it. Made conditional on the value moving, which is the idiom `write_vxsat` in the same file already uses and which deletes no architectural effect, the trace is 515 records and byte-identical at both geometries. No existing member's digest moved, because none of them dirties the vector context twice.
      **`vlseg<nf>e<eew>ff.v` was a vector operation that was neither all nor nothing, and the register has since answered it: the form is excluded.** The fault-only-first load was in the model's decode surface and in the profile's admitted set, and its defined result on a fault at any element past the first was to trim `vl` and retire rather than to raise. R-15-039a says a vector operation is all-or-nothing, and the profile's own `vstart` row says the element loop runs from zero in every vector instruction's definition, which the trimming honoured and the all-or-nothing did not. This item left the clause exactly as it found it and added only the discarded check to it, because either the all-or-nothing rule was narrower than it read or the fault-only-first forms left the profile, and neither is a curation batch's call. R-15-039b takes the second arm, and the bullet below is the deletion it makes owed. R-15-115b is not the requirement that was in conflict, and this finding read it as one: it restricts *which* elements may raise, the active ones alone, and does not require that every active failure raises.
      **The assembler's vector rows needed one operand that is spelled by being absent.** `v0.t` marks a masked operation and nothing at all marks an unmasked one, which is how every RISC-V assembler spells it and how the model's own `maybe_vmask` mapping reads it back, so the mask is the one operand whose *absence* is a value rather than a missing argument. It is handled where the arity is checked rather than by giving each masked form a second mnemonic, which would have doubled forty-three rows to say one thing.
    * **The fault-only-first forms are deleted, which is a profile amendment being carried and not this item reopened.** R-15-039b excludes `vle<eew>ff.v` and the `vlseg<nf>e<eew>ff.v` segment forms, so `VLSEGFFTYPE` leaves [extensions/V/vext_mem_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/V/vext_mem_insts.sail) entire, union clause, `encdec`, execute clause and assembly mapping together, 84 lines of Sail; [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py) loses the four `vle<eew>ff.v` rows with them, so that no member of the corpus can assemble a word the model no longer decodes, and carries in their place the sentence saying the `lumop` has no row on purpose.
      **`lumop` 0b10000 decoding to nothing at all is the end state rather than a hole in the encoding space**, and R-15-014 is what makes it one: every reserved, custom and unused encoding traps rather than silently executing, so a point no clause claims reaches the illegal-instruction path by the same route every unallocated point does. That is also the whole of what the deletion costs, R-15-040a's disposition covering the residue unchanged, hand-written RVV assembly being rejected as an undefined instruction rather than miscompiled, with no calling convention and no compiler naming the form.
      Nothing else goes with it, and every candidate was checked against its callers by name rather than assumed dead: `get_vtype_vta` keeps eight callers and `AGNOSTIC` eleven arms across the arithmetic, floating-point and utility clauses, which is the tail policy every vector form with a tail reads; `vector_body_inactive` and `vector_masked_element_check` keep the six surviving masked memory forms and [test_vector_cap.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_vector_cap.sail); `init_masked_result` and `nfields_string` are every vector form's; and `vl` keeps exactly the writers R-15-039b names it by, `vsetvl`/`vsetvli` and the partition clear.
      **One residue is named for the auditor who runs the absence contract's search rather than left to be re-derived as harmless.** `model/test/first_party/src/common/encoding.h` still carries sixteen `MATCH_VLE*FF_V` and `MASK_VLE*FF_V` macros and eight `DECLARE_INSN(vle*ff_v, ...)` rows, four of the eight widths being the withdrawn 128- to 1024-bit element encodings this model never carried at all. It is the vendored riscv-tests header the dormant first-party harness reads, so it is not model surface and it is not this batch's to remove; it joins the PMP, `satp`, `minstret`, LR/SC and scalar-FP surface c4 left in that same file wanting one pass of its own, and it is what a grep for the fault-only-first form finds first.
      Re-measured after the deletion: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness unmoved at 77 properties, no property having stood on the deleted clause; the differential corpus green at 24 of 24 with 224 checks and every trace digest unmoved, no member having ever written the form; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; `model.py devicetree` unmoved at 4,466 bytes; the checker green on all 65 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * [ ] **M0.8c · M-class matrix extension and its scratchpad** · 8.5 h, range 6–11 · 0.7% · Parallel with M0.8b
    * **The scratchpad half is in the tree and the item stays open on the other half, which the profile has not yet enumerated.** The software-managed scratchpad is an addressable region class ([sys/pma.sail](../model/model/sys/pma.sail)): capability-governed plain memory at a fixed address range, on neither of main memory's two latency classes, coherence-exempt, and not executable, with a composition declaring a wrong one refused rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). The partition switch's single unconditional pass covers exactly the declared range and takes the validity tags with it (R-07-014c, R-15-007r, [extensions/platform/vmclear.sail](../model/model/extensions/platform/vmclear.sail)); the attested devicetree carries the link from a class to its range rather than the range twice (R-09-007, [postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)); and R-15-009's standing re-pin obligation is recorded beside the geometry the model declares for the unit ([core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail)). **No instruction is added, and what stops it is a gap in the enumeration rather than a shortfall in the work**: the frozen profile books the matrix extension as a *unit* and names no mnemonic, operand form, or encoding for it, and nothing enters the profile by inheritance (R-15-007j).
    * **The custom-opcode ledger does not move.** M0.6g's three rows spend one opcode between them, M0.6h's four still do, and this item adds no fifth: custom-0 (`0b0001011`) carries `cclear` at `funct3` 000, `vmclear` at 001 and the indexed pair's width-coded 011 and 111, leaving 010, 100, 101 and 110 unspent in that opcode and custom-1 and custom-3 whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). The matrix extension is not one of those rows *yet*, and which act admits it is now written down: R-15-014a's second-act delta carries it at **(ix)**, its instruction surface, operand sourcing and encoding together, conditioned on R-15-116's sustained dense-GEMM margin, which no backend and no composed image can supply before M1. So custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole against it exactly as M0.6g and M0.6h left them whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits, and spending either here would be modelling a measurement-conditioned provisional row, which is the one thing M0.6g's exit condition forbids.
    * **What remains, and why it is a gap rather than a remainder.** R-15-118's coprocessor line and R-08-003's per-element checks need an instruction to be stated over, so they arrive with the surface and not before it; R-15-116's admission is already taken, dense int8/bf16 inference clearing the 8–10× bar and the extension kept, so what is unwritten is the encoding and not the decision. Closing it needs the profile to enumerate the extension's instructions the way it enumerates every other bespoke row's, which is a register and profile act ahead of a model one (R-15-001a).
    * **The re-pin target R-15-009 names by lineage has an edition, which changes what closing that gap costs.** The **Integrated Matrix Extension** is the IME half of that lineage, and the shape it takes is the shape this item's own findings derive rather than a different one: no new architected state for matrix data, which is R-15-117's software de-quantization and the declined tile file read from the other side; matrix data held in the existing vector registers, which is the operand sourcing [architectural-alternatives.md](architectural-alternatives.md) books as an exploration question rather than an ISA commitment; 32-bit encodings and a stated expectation of low opcode pressure, which is the ledger above. Its task group's own ratification plan puts the specification frozen at v0.9 on 2026-06-27 with public review opening the same day, TSC approval at v0.99 on 2026-08-17, and ratification at v1.0 on 2026-08-27. **AME**, the arm that does introduce architectural state of its own, took a one-year task-group extension in April 2026, and it is the arm this design's deletions argue against.
      Three caveats ride that, and none of them is about quality. The dates are the task group's own targets rather than history, so the edition is read at the act that would pin it and slip is ordinary. An *integrated* extension makes the matrix geometry a function of the vector geometry, so IME over the M class's declared VLEN is a question against [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) rather than a free choice, and it is the first thing a re-pin has to answer rather than the last. And a ratified extension is not thereby an admitted one: nothing enters the profile by inheritance (R-15-007j), so the second act still admits the surface against R-15-116's margin and still enumerates what it admits.
      What this changes is the disposition and not the schedule. If IME ratifies, the surface R-15-014a (ix) admits is a version pin rather than an authored encoding, which is the same profile act against a cheaper object, and R-15-009's standing re-pin obligation acquires a target it can name instead of a lineage it can only watch.
    * Net change: 576 lines added and 22 removed across ten files, one of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **65 properties against 62**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys, unchanged**, the region class being a property of a list member rather than a key of the configuration tree; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes, no configuration here declaring a scratchpad for it to carry; each of the **eight** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 64 rules with every mutant killed; and both type checkers clean.
    * Ten findings.
      **The profile books the matrix extension as a unit and names no instruction, so there is nothing here to admit.** Every other bespoke row in [isa-profile.md](isa-profile.md) §3 names its instruction, its operand form, and its encoding decision, down to `cclear h, mask` and `cld rd, cs1[rs2 << imm]` and `rcstep rd, rs1, rs2`; the matrix row names a unit, a disposition, and a re-pin target. R-15-007j runs over semantics and not membership, so a construct the enumerations do not name is absent and is admitted only on a stated ground. Three neighbouring decisions are already taken and none of them is this one, which is worth separating because they read as though they were: no architectural tile file, the ACE/SME state being evaluated and not imported; no block-scale register, R-15-117 keeping de-quantization and microscaling in software on the same class's vector unit; and outer-product sourcing recorded as a proof-aware microarchitectural exploration question *rather than a new ISA commitment* ([architectural-alternatives.md](architectural-alternatives.md)). All three are about what the extension does not **hold**, and an instruction surface whose operands come from vector registers and memory needs none of it; R-15-117's own wording, that no such state enters *the frozen matrix ISA*, presupposes the ISA it excludes state from. What is left undecided is the mnemonic, the operand encoding, and the opcode, and authoring those is an ISA act rather than a curation one.
      **The empty profile cell was the symptom, and the missing sorting rule was the defect.** What this item ran into is that no Sail file in the tree carries a matrix instruction: what [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) holds is geometry, `matrix_int8_dim` and `matrix_bf16_dim` beside `core_class_has_matrix`, which is a composition declaring how large a systolic array is and not a machine knowing what it executes. What sorts the surface is R-15-116's own admission, which takes the unit only against a **sustained dense-GEMM margin**: a margin is a measurement against generated output, so the surface belongs to the second act by R-15-014a's own rule rather than to an amendment, and it is booked there at (ix). R-15-014b now states the rule that was missing, that a unit booked without an instruction surface names the act that will book it and a unit booked with neither is a review-gate finding. **So this half of the item is out of scope rather than blocked**, which is the better place for it: nothing here waits on a decision, and modelling the surface now would be exactly the measurement-conditioned provisional row M0.6g's exit condition refuses.
      **A scratchpad is a placement and not a structure, which decides how much of one a Sail model can carry.** R-15-167 makes it plain memory at a fixed range with no fill, no eviction, no valid or tag array, and no lookup that is not the ordinary address decode, so it is declared beside every other property the composition fixes about an address range rather than modeled as a second memory. That leaves exactly one irreducible new fact, *whose* it is, and everything else is a refusal over a field the PMA already had: not coherent (R-15-167), not executable (R-18-009 leaves the instruction scratchpad open and unspecified), and not on a latency class. R-15-165's own test is what makes the thinness correct rather than lossy: the contents are a function of an explicit software placement and never of access history, and it is history-dependence and not address-indexing that carries the channel.
      **It is on neither latency class, and stating that relaxes an M0.14 invariant rather than adding a third class.** R-15-247's two classes are two media of *main* memory and R-15-168 makes the scratchpad the local tier above them, so the validator's *every array is on exactly one class* becomes *every array that is not a scratchpad*, with the scratchpad refused a class from the other side. A third `MemoryClass` arm would have been the natural wrong move: it would misstate R-15-247's own count, move the figures K-60 holds, and give `memory_class_at` a value no latency constant stands behind.
      **The matrix half of `vmclear`'s inventory is empty, and that is an enumeration result rather than a milestone's residue.** M0.6h booked two absent members and said the clause grows by two lines when they land; one of them lands and the other turns out to be nothing to clear. R-15-117 keeps de-quantization and block-scale application in software, the tile file is not imported, and R-15-118's coprocessor line leaves the unit no state of its own to hold operands in, every byte it moves being core-issued against an explicit capability operand. So what a partition can dirty is the scratchpad and nothing else, and the file says so in the paragraph where a clause that cleared nothing would otherwise sit.
      **The scratchpad has no size, and no configuration in this tree declares one.** R-15-108 selects scratchpad sizes and the exploration has not run, which is what `frozen` is false for in every class row; a range written into a shipped configuration ahead of it is exactly how a placeholder becomes an architectural input, which is the register M0.8a established for the class table and M0.14 for the memory-class constants. So the model carries the shape, refuses a wrong one, and carries no range, and the generated devicetree is unmoved because there is nothing yet for it to declare.
      **The scratchpad is unreachable from the C-class golden emulator, so this item adds no corpus member and its harness installs its own address map.** The frozen emulator is the C class (M0.10), R-15-165 puts the scratchpads on the V- and M-class datapaths, and the differential corpus runs against that emulator, so no corpus program can address one and a member claiming to would be a member about a machine this build is not. The three new `$[test]` properties therefore install a region above every region the configurations declare and restore what they found, which is the same reason M0.6g gives for profile-conditional behaviour belonging in the corpus, read from the other end: a property about a structure no shipped configuration has is a property that must supply it.
      **A new per-region property does not move the key-set gate, and it is schema conformance that catches it instead.** M0.6g found the generated test-matrix configurations to be a second declaration of the platform, so a key added to one and not to [config.json.in](../model/config/config.json.in) is a build failure rather than a divergence found later. The region class is exactly that class of key and the count cannot see it: `memory.regions` is a *list*, so a member's properties are not keys of the configuration tree and both declarations agree at 270 before and after. What bites is the schema, which grew the property and refuses a region without it in either file. The gate still holds, but an unmoved key count is not evidence that nothing was added to a configuration, and a later milestone reading it that way would be reading it wrong.
      **The build tree is one tree and a second worktree's build fails at `configure` rather than at compile.** `cmake` records the source directory in its cache, so a lane building the same canonical build directory from a different checkout is refused with a cache mismatch before a line is compiled. The lever needed no change to any tool, [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) already reading `VOS_BUILD_DIR` and `VOS_LOG_DIR`, and it is worth writing down beside the conventions above because it is the mechanism by which two items that both write `model/` serialize whatever their `Parallel` marks say.
      **This item owes the checker no rule, and the reason is that the fact it would hold is a model fact rather than a document one.** The one cross-artifact agreement it creates is between a class's declared `scratchpad_bytes` and the address map's declared range, and both live in the configuration the Sail validator reads, so the refusal is stated where the composition is refused and fires on every run of every loop rather than on a document sweep. A checker rule over the same pair would be a second reader of one file, which is the defect this repository is built to catch rather than a rule it is short of.
  * [x] **M0.8d · FEC and Keccak units** · 11 h actual · 1.0%
    * The LDPC and polar decoders as fixed-geometry core-issued capability-operand instructions with deterministic iteration bounds and no firmware (R-15-119), and the frozen Keccak-f[1600] permutation as a single vector instruction on the vector-bearing classes (R-15-056, R-15-057, R-15-059), whose Sail semantics are fresh against FIPS 202 and the NIST ACVP vectors (R-15-058). Both are attachments to a class the roster already names, so what this item adds is surface and not parameterization.
    * **Both halves are landed and the profile names all three things it books for each of them.** [isa-profile.md](isa-profile.md) §3 carries the Keccak row's shape and re-pin target and now carries the FEC row's mnemonics, operand form and encoding beside them, so nothing on this item enters the profile by inheritance and no instruction here is surface a row does not name (R-15-007j, R-15-014b).
    * `vkeccak.vi` lands in [extensions/keccak/](../model/model/extensions/keccak/), which is its own directory because the fork is bespoke: everything in `extensions/vector_crypto/` beside it is a ratified extension with an upstream clause behind it, and RVG-84 has not ratified, so the permutation and the instruction are authored here and diffed against the draft at each PQC Task Group milestone, on the ground `extensions/platform/` already sits on. [keccak_p1600.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_p1600.sail) is the permutation as a **pure function** and [keccak_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_insts.sail) is the element group, the class placement, the round-count immediate, and the encoding; the split is R-15-057a's own, since the re-pin exchanges an encoding and leaves the semantics where they are.
    * **Both round counts are frozen and neither is added later** (R-15-056a). Keccak-*p*[1600,24] is SHA-3 and SHAKE and Keccak-*p*[1600,12] is TurboSHAKE and KangarooTwelve, and FIPS 202 makes the short form the **last** twelve rounds of the long one, so it is a round range over one constant table rather than a second table. Both sit on the `Zvkt` fixed-latency list as two rows of the timing-annotated model ([core/timing.sail](../model/model/core/timing.sail), R-15-053), two rather than one because a single row scaled by the immediate would be a latency computed from a field of the instruction, which is the shape that table's signature exists to refuse. **The re-pin obligation is recorded where a curator meets it**, at the foot of the instruction file and as two named deltas rather than as a note: what the fork states and R-15-057a does not fix are the disposition of the 448 bits the 2048-bit element group carries above the 1600-bit state, written zero here, and the vector-length mandate.
    * **The seven custom-opcode rows still spend one opcode between them.** `vkeccak.vi` takes custom-0 `funct3` 010, between `vmclear`'s 001 and the indexed access's width-coded 011, and `ldpcdec` and `polardec` take 100 **together**, the code family riding `funct7`, so custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits and 101 and 110 stay unspent besides (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). A fork takes custom space rather than an unallocated point in the standard OP-V opcode deliberately: the vector major opcode is space the standard allocates, and moving into it **is** the re-pin rather than something a fork may do ahead of one, so a reader checking this encoding against the RISC-V opcode map finds an instruction where non-standard instructions belong and a stock disassembler prints nothing rather than the wrong thing, which is the ground M0.6h took for `cbo.scrub`. The three RVV instructions the Keccak corpus member needs around it are **not** in the dialect table: they are the V-class datapath's surface and M0.8b's to add, so `keccak-perm` spells those three words out with their fields.
    * **The FEC surface is two mnemonics over one operand form**, `ldpcdec cd, cs1, rs2` and `polardec cd, cs1, rs2` in [extensions/fec/](../model/model/extensions/fec/) (R-15-119b): `cs1` authorises the soft-symbol block the decoder consumes, one signed 8-bit log-likelihood ratio per received coded bit, `cd` authorises the hard-bit block it produces and sits in the `rd` field read as a source the way `csd`'s does, and `rs2` packs the block descriptor, the soft count, the hard count, the composition's own index into its declared code set, and a reserved field. The split between the file that declares the codes and the file that carries the instruction is R-15-119a's: [fec_codes.sail](../model/model/extensions/fec/fec_codes.sail) is the shape a composition may declare and holds none of the numbers, and [fec_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/fec/fec_insts.sail) is the surface, which is the same division the Keccak fork makes between its permutation and its instruction.
    * **The invariance R-15-119a requires is a mechanism and not a promise, and the mechanism is one word.** A descriptor names a code by **index** into what the composition declared, so a base graph, a lifting size, a reliability sequence and a rate-matching schedule have no instruction field to reach and two builders holding two sets of TS 38.212's tables assemble the same words. It is asserted from both ends: a property takes the encoding of one instruction under three different declared code sets and requires the three words to be equal, and [fec-surface.s](../corpus/fec-surface.s) reads each faulting word back out of the trap value and requires a changed descriptor register's *contents* to move no field of it.
    * **The gate is the composed roster entry's FEC attachment and nothing else, which is a third answer to the question this item's first finding asks.** What decides which predicate an instruction takes is whose instruction it is **and what state it touches**. `vmclear` is the switcher's and gates on the static property alone; `vkeccak.vi` is the partition's own and folds in `mstatus.VS` because it writes the architectural vector register file. These are the partition's own too, and they touch **no extension context at all**: no vector register, no vector CSR, no unit state, their whole effect being memory reached through two explicit capability operands (R-15-118, R-08-003). There is no context bit for a context gate to read, so the static half is the whole gate, and it is the roster's rather than the class's because the decoders follow the statically pinned radio cores (R-12-038, R-15-114).
    * **The capability checks are the existing path and there is no second copy of one.** A code block runs past the memory interface's widest access, so the pass is a walk and each step goes through `data_read` or `data_write`, which compose `ext_data_get_addr` over `cap_data_checks` ([core/addr_checks.sail](../model/model/core/addr_checks.sail)); that is exactly what M0.8b found for the vector surface, arriving here from the other side. What the two authorities buy is that a fault names the register whose authority was short: the soft block and the decoded block are different objects held by different code for different spans, and an in-place form would have let a decoder's write reach every byte its read covered.
    * Net change: 1,211 lines added and 8 removed across 14 files, four of them new, for the Keccak half; and 1,302 added and 51 removed across 23 files, four of them new, for the FEC surface.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **84 properties against 77**, seven of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **290 keys against 267**; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the differential corpus green at **25 of 25** with **233 checks**, one member and 9 checks new, the manifest at version 7, and every other member's digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green, the generated tree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at **4,890 bytes against 4,466** and accepted by the emulator's own bound against the region it is written into, the growth being the four FEC geometry properties on each of the five class nodes; each of the **eight** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration, with the unmutated composition and a well-formed FEC-bearing one both accepted; the checker green on all 72 rules with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green; the proof gate closing 19 constants; the co-read ledger whole; and both type checkers clean.
    * Eight findings.
      **The gate is the class *and* the context, and M0.6h's answer is right for its instruction and wrong for this one.** `vmclear` gates on `hartSupports` and deliberately not on `currentlyEnabled`, because it is the **switcher's** instruction and must not be trappable by the partition it exists to erase. `vkeccak.vi` is the **partition's own** and reads and writes the architectural vector register file exactly as `vsha2ms.vv` does, so it folds in `mstatus.VS`; gating it on the class alone would let a partition that has turned its own vector unit off write that file, which is the extension-context gate deleted rather than honoured (R-07-012, R-15-049). What decides which predicate an instruction takes is therefore whose instruction it is, not whether the register's word is *class*. The class half is real and is the other conjunct: it reads `keccak_unit` straight out of [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail), which is that table's first consumer and leaves nothing for a second declaration to disagree with.
      **No row enters the extension registry, and that is the requirement rather than an omission.** `hartSupports` is what the generated `riscv,isa` string and `riscv,isa-extensions` array are built from ([postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)), so an `Ext_Zvknhk` row would have an **attested** artifact name a standards-track extension this machine does not implement, RVG-84 not having ratified. What the tree says about the unit instead is the vendor property M0.8a already put on the class node, which says exactly what is true. The re-pin is what adds the registry row, and it adds it on the day the name means something.
      **R-15-057a's `Zvl128b` mandate is unreachable, and the model is correct either way.** An element group of 2048 bits needs LMUL × VLEN ≥ 2048 and LMUL tops out at 8, so the effective floor is VLEN = 256 and no `vl` makes the instruction legal at 128. The mandate is therefore **not** written as a conjunct, a condition no configuration can fail being the dead arm M0.16 found; the geometry check is strictly stronger and is the operative one, and the disagreement is booked as the second re-pin delta. Whether the draft's mandate is `Zvl256b` and the register's record of it is short, or the draft admits a geometry its own instruction cannot decode at, is a reading of the draft and a register question. **The figure that falls out of it corroborates the placement**: VLEN = 256 is the C class's own geometry, which is exactly where R-15-059a states its figures, and the harness holds the register-pressure half as a property rather than a declared number, the group being LMUL=8 and eight of the thirty-two architectural vector registers there.
      **The two defects the known-answer vectors found were both transcriptions, and both are now held by a property that names the cause.** `keccak_mod5`'s wildcard swallowed twenty as well as nineteen, so *pi* sent lane (4, 4) to row 4 where the standard says row 0, dropping one lane and writing another twice; and a Sail vector literal is `dec`-ordered, so the round-constant table was being read backwards, which the model's own S-box already carries the subtraction for. Neither is algebra and neither would have been found by reading the file. What is worth recording is that the property which **names** the first, that *pi* is a permutation of the twenty-five lanes, was written before the vectors and never ran: the harness runs alphabetically, so the answer that reports the symptom aborted the executable ahead of the table check that reports the cause. A known-answer vector says *wrong*; a structural property says *why*, and the ordering between them is not something a test file gets to choose.
      **Where this model stops on the FEC half is correction, and it stops there for the reason `cbo.scrub` stops at the syndrome.** The pass computes the hard decision, the sign of each log-likelihood ratio, which is what every declared decoder geometry agrees on before a single message passes; belief propagation above it corrects a *channel*, and this model has none, memory returning the bits some program wrote and no codeword carrying an error. That is the same absence [cbo_scrub.sail](../model/model/extensions/platform/cbo_scrub.sail) states for a correctable codeword and it takes the same sentence: on this model the decoded word is the hard decision, the correction being empty, and on a machine with a channel it is the corrected block, with nothing here changing but the value the pass computes. It is worth stating rather than eliding because the alternative reading is available and wrong: a clause computing one schedule's answer would state a **microarchitectural** choice as an architectural result, R-15-119a having put the decoder parallelism and the polar list width in R-15-108's frozen set, and a property asserting a corrected word would be a property about a harness.
      **The corpus found an encoding defect the harness structurally cannot, which is the argument for carrying both.** A hand-written `vse64.v` merged the `rs1` and `funct3` nibbles and named `c11` where it meant `c10`, an uninitialized register whose untagged value faulted the access; the run reported a wrong permutation and the commit trace named a tag violation on register 11. A `$[test]` calls `execute` on a decoded instruction and so never sees a mis-encoded word at all. This is the same division [test_keccak.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_keccak.sail) makes for the permutation itself, properties about the function and a program about the instruction, arriving from the other side.
      **A property that saves a list register and restores it by assigning the saved binding does not restore it, and only the harness's per-property reset hides that.** The gate property attached a roster entry, put back the value it had bound before the change, and found the entry still attached; reading the register it is restoring and dropping the head it prepended works. The reset is what keeps the defect invisible: the property that ran before it did the same thing, left the entry standing, and the next property still found a clean roster, so the leak never crosses a property boundary and no run is wrong today. It is worth recording rather than quietly repairing because [test_matrix_scratchpad.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_matrix_scratchpad.sail) stands on the same idiom over `pma_regions`, where it is correct as run for exactly the same reason and would stop being correct the moment a property wanted to observe its own restore.
      **The one-directional refusal set is the point, and the natural symmetric one would have refused every composition in this tree.** A composition declaring codes with no hart carrying the decoders is refused, because a channel code with no unit to decode it is a receiver with nowhere to run its physical layer. The converse is deliberately not refused: a hart carrying the units on a class that declares no geometry is R-15-108's exploration not having run, which is the state every configuration here is in. The same asymmetry decides the instruction's own maximum-block refusal, which is conditional on a geometry having been *selected*, a zero reading as *not selected* and not as a zero-length datapath; an unconditional one would have made every well-formed descriptor illegal on every machine nobody has sized yet. That is M0.8c's scratchpad register read from the refusal side rather than the declaration side.
    * **The re-pin's clock is nearer than the fork's framing implies, and the entry that enumerates its milestones now names all of them.** The PQC Task Group approved its development-complete milestone on 2026-07-13, and its ratification plan runs v0.6 on 2026-07-16, v0.8 stabilized on 2026-07-30, **v0.9 frozen on 2026-09-03**, public review from 2026-09-22, v0.99 TSC approval on 2026-11-20 and v1.0 on 2026-12-31, scoped throughout at one opcode in standard encoding. R-15-057a's accept named the stabilization, the public review and the ratification target and did not name the freeze, which is the milestone the two booked deltas change character at: before it a diff is taken against a moving draft and a divergence may be the draft's next revision, after it a divergence is one of the two specifications being wrong. The accept now carries it and says why it is among them, which is a register act taken here rather than a gap reported here.
      Two consequences, and both strengthen this item's own conservatism rather than undercut it. The custom-0 encoding is a bridge with a stated end rather than an open-ended fork, which is a better reading of why it declines standard OP-V space than the one the encoding paragraph gives. And the task group carries **Sail model integration** among its own deliverables, so the ratified semantics arrive as a second transcription of FIPS 202 against the one [keccak_p1600.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_p1600.sail) already holds, which makes the pair a differential check at re-pin time rather than one transcription replacing another.
    * **What remains, and it is neither half of this item.** The `Zvbb`/`Zvbc` software Keccak R-15-058 retains as the portable path and the differential reference is a software artifact rather than model surface and belongs with the code the toolchain milestone emits; and an FEC decode that *retires* is unreachable from this rig, the golden emulator being the C class and the reference composition attaching the decoders to the pinned radio V pair, so [`fec-surface`](../corpus/fec-surface.s) reaches the encoding and the refusal and the properties reach the pass. Neither is work this item defers: the first is another milestone's and the second is a composed image's, and both are booked where they belong ([differential-corpus.md](differential-corpus.md)).
    * **The FEC decoders are on the first release's critical path, and nothing holds them any longer.** R-18-004 carries the radio roster whole, cellular, the eUICC, carrier certification, the HARQ hard-real-time class and the 5G-AKA hierarchy arriving with the first parts, and puts FEC-unit bring-up (R-18-005) and carrier certification on the critical path to first parts rather than behind them; the single roster deferral is the browser. The roster question underneath the surface is closed in the register: R-12-038 pins the PHY servers on the radio V-class cores with the FEC units, R-12-041 makes the cellular stack NR RRC and 5G-core NAS with no older protocol state machine in it, and R-15-129 makes the FEC units the silicon half of the 5G/6G generation floor, decoding LDPC and polar and nothing else. A 5G NR physical layer is what that roster **is**, and certification is a first-release deliverable beside it, so the base graphs a certified receiver decodes are TS 38.212's own and a bespoke parameterization is not a substitute available to it. This plan's order did not hold the surface back either: R-15-119 carries no measurement conditioning, so R-15-014b owed it at the provisional freeze, and R-15-014a makes that a specification act with **no build prerequisite**. **The licence call is taken and its arm is to publish table-free**: 3GPP TS 38.212 is neither pinned nor vendored, and the read at its terms is that in-house copies for product design are carved out while reproduction in a conveyed artifact is not, so a base graph transcribed into a tracked Sail or Gallina source in a public repository is the one act the carve-out does not reach. The surface is accordingly authored **parameterized over the codes' parameters and fixing none of them**, the numbers arriving at composition from the copy the builder holds, so no normative table is a tracked file here and an auditor's check is a search of the tree rather than a reading of it. What it forfeits is self-containment, and the forfeit is stated rather than absorbed: the tree alone does not compose an FEC-bearing image, the reproducible-build story is correspondingly narrower for that one component, and every builder takes the 3GPP read themselves rather than inheriting one taken here. **The permission arm stays open** and composes with this one: seeking 3GPP's own copyright form later would make the tables trackable and retire the forfeit, and nothing in this arm forecloses it, which is why it is the arm a proof-of-concept spends while what the first release is remains open (R-18-004).
    Two consequences reach past this item. **The register states the parameter and not its value**, which is the same shape R-15-247p's bank count and R-15-014a (vii)'s block size already take, a frozen parameter named with its value owed; the difference is only that the value is owed to a builder's own copy rather than to a measurement, and the register is a public tracked document to which the reproduction restriction applies exactly as it does to the model. The composition files here take the same shape from the other end, declaring the *shape* of a code set and an empty list of them, exactly as they declare a scratchpad's shape and no range. And **TS 38.331 rides the same arm**, the RRC state machines being owed by R-05-048 and R-18-029 against a standard whose terms are the same instrument's, so the radio reference machines are authored against procedures rather than transcribing normative tables into the tree.
    * The same read covers a second row: TS 38.331 is already owed by R-05-048 and R-18-029 for the RRC state machines, and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries no 3GPP entry in any of its inventory tables.
    * **The decoder mathematics have a Rocq start-from and neither of the two things blocking this item is among what it supplies.** `affeldt-aist/infotheo` is a Rocq formalization of information theory and linear error-correcting codes over MathComp, carrying **LDPC** with encoders and decoders beside Hamming, Reed-Solomon and BCH, and distributed on opam. It fixes no base graph and no lifting size, which is exactly what a start-from on this arm may not do: the code parameters are TS 38.212's and composition-supplied under R-15-119a, so a Rocq development that stated them would be the reproduction the table-free arm declines. Where it bears is one layer under both, on the decoder mathematics the geometry would eventually parameterize, which is a Coq-native reference this repository would otherwise author from the literature. Three caveats before it is treated as more than that. Its LDPC development is the *acyclic* case rather than the protograph-lifted codes a 5G-class decoder uses, so it is the theory and not the code family. A formalization of a code is not an implementation of a decoder, still less a fixed-geometry, deterministic-iteration-bound one of the kind R-15-119 fixes the category as. And its terms are unread here, as every start-from's are until the milestone that would incorporate it reads them.
* [x] **M0.9 · Add documented timing annotations** · 5 h actual · 0.4%
  * The annotated layer lands as one table of operation classes in [core/timing.sail](../model/model/core/timing.sail): the profile's §9 contracts as latency rows, the two partition-switch constants that are per core class, the relation R-15-085b states between the on-list and off-list vector accesses, and `qualified` beside all of it. The shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)), and nothing the machine executes reads any of it: the model runs no cycles, so this is documentation the tools hold rather than behaviour they check.
  * **K-63** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) is what the item turned out to owe, and it is not the rule it set out to owe. It holds every requirement id the model files this tool can read cite against the register, 166 of them across five files, which is a class of citation that reached no rule at all before: `model/` is outside the checker's corpus wholesale and K-11 reads only tracked documents, so a mistyped id in a Sail comment rendered as an argument and pointed at nothing.
  * Net change: 679 lines added and 17 removed across 13 files, one of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **62 properties against 59**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys against 228**; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes, the timing table deliberately not being in it; each of the **six** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on **all 64 rules against 63**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    **The contract is the signature and not a comment beside it.** `timing_cycles` takes an operation class and takes no operand, no register, no address, and no prior state, so there is nowhere for a value to enter it. That is what data-independent latency looks like in a model that executes no cycles (R-15-053, R-15-095): a future consumer wanting an operand shows up as a changed signature rather than as changed arithmetic, which is the device M0.16's sequencer already uses to hold R-15-247r by what its functions take.
    **The scalar table is one table because the front end is one front end.** Scalar front ends are one shared microarchitecture across every class, which is exactly what makes kernel-path WCET a single analysis (R-15-112), so a per-class scalar latency would be a second front end stated by arithmetic. Two rows *are* per class, the eager zeroize and the padded fence, and the reason is not that their cost varies but that one kernel binary budgets a partition switch on every class it may run on (R-07-012, R-15-220).
    **The table is deliberately not in the attested devicetree, and R-09-007 is why.** That requirement enumerates what the static devicetree declares, core classes, islands, the NoC schedule, OPP tables, and radio calibration and limit values, and a latency is none of them. These constants are inputs to an off-device WCET derivation rather than values a booting kernel reads (R-11-015, R-18-024), so emitting them would grow an attested artifact by a table nothing on the machine consults, in the same item that had just resized the ROM for the one that does belong there.
    **The crown-jewel row does not move, and `qualified` is what keeps that honest.** R-17-041 makes the latency *magnitudes* the crown-jewel specification and not one is measured: `Zicntr` and `Zihpm` are implemented in no lifecycle state so nothing on this machine can read a cycle (R-15-077), development measurement rides lifecycle-gated Debug Module trace on real RTL, and the freeze contract refuses a host-timed column outright. What landed is the table those magnitudes will be written into. What would earn the flip, and with it R-05-150's review at the flip, is a total map from every instruction to its class, which is this item's successor rather than part of it.
    **The membership rule this item set out to owe cannot be written without becoming the defect it would catch.** 11 register entries say an operation is one entry in the timing-annotated model, and holding that set against the table needs a mapping from each entry's prose to a class member, which is a third copy of the correspondence. A body-pattern selection was tried and returns two of the 11, the register stating one obligation in five different wordings. So membership stays a person's, resting on the profile's §9 table that K-14 already requires to carry every §15.12 requirement, and the rule budget went to something a machine can actually decide.

That is cosmetic upstream, where builds are usually in-tree. It is freeze-blocking here: the freeze contract requires a report to carry the Sail model revision its cycle columns came from and rejects one that does not, and an executable reference that cannot say which model it is cannot supply it.
    **The exit criterion had to be re-read, because the suite it names is one this machine cannot run.** M0.6f excluded `riscv-tests` from registration outright: every program in it addresses memory through an integer base register, which a purecap machine faults on, so *ISA tests green* as written names a corpus that was retired two items before this one entered. What stands in its place is this repository's own pair, the model's `$[test]` harness and the differential corpus, and `reference` prints both beside the revision, because a reference's identity is what it is **and** what it has been shown to do.
    **The stamp is set at configure time and the working tree decides it.** `model.py build` reconfigures on every run, so the revision is never stale, but an emulator built from a tree under edit names the commit it was configured at with `-dirty` after it, and only one built from a clean checkout of a landed commit names something a third party could reproduce. `reference` prints the dirty marker and does not refuse it, because a gate that failed on an edited working tree is a gate that gets turned off; it refuses an **unknown** revision, which is the state this item closed.
    **`--version` still reports upstream's release number and that is right rather than residue.** 0.13.1 is a true fact about the lineage this tree was curated from, and the model's own identity is the revision beside it, which `--build-info` already carries. * [x] **M0.14 · Two latency classes in the Sail model** · 5 h actual · 0.4%
  * The two classes land as composition-time declarations and nothing else: [core/memory_class.sail](../model/model/core/memory_class.sail) carries the enumeration and the per-class record, the placement is a PMA beside every other property the composition fixes about an address range ([sys/pma.sail](../model/model/sys/pma.sail)), the constants enter the attested devicetree R-15-247m puts them in ([postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)), and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface** (R-15-247h, [absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17): no access path calls the class query at all, a machine consulting the class at run time being the hierarchy R-15-247 refuses.
  * The record is eight fields per class, `qualified` beside seven numbers: usable density, bank count, the retention corner as a floor and a ceiling, and the four latency constants. Both configurations declare a second-class region beside the first-class one, at 8 GiB above the existing RAM, so both classes are live at distinct constants in the profile's own configuration and in the generated max configuration alike.
  * Net change: 907 lines added and 18 removed across 13 files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **48 properties against 46**, two of them new; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at **18 of 18** with one member and 8 checks new and the seventeen existing digests unmoved; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 2,224 bytes, inside the 4 KiB ROM region it is written to; each of the six new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Five findings.
    **The record is symmetric and the machine is not, and the validator is where the difference is held.** R-15-247m names the same four groups per class, so the record is one shape read twice; R-15-247n says the first class is latched and the second stores charge on a gate node with no restoring feedback, so three of those fields have no meaning on the first class at all. They are declared zero there under the model's own zero-is-absent idiom and the validator refuses anything else, which puts an architectural distinction where a reader can see it rather than leaving a composition free to declare a refresh deadline over a latched array.
    **The retention corner is two fields because only one of them is ever read.** R-15-247m states it as a measured interval rather than a point, and R-15-247c consumes retention by the refresh deadline and by nothing else, so a mean with a tolerance would invite the ceiling into a guarantee. A floor and a ceiling with exactly one consumer named in one function is that requirement realized: a second consumer would show up as a new call site rather than as new arithmetic.
    **There is no separate fetch constant to declare.** R-15-247j prices second-class code against "that class's fetch constant", and with R-15-164 deleting every cache there is no instruction cache, no separate instruction path, and nothing to amortize, so a fetch is a read of whichever class the code resides on and the fetch constant *is* the read constant. The delta the placement rule makes admission-visible is therefore the difference of two numbers the record already carries, computed by the static memory plan (M1.9) rather than by anything the machine executes.
    **A placeholder that nothing distinguishes from a measurement is how it becomes an input, so the qualification state is attested.** R-15-247m admits no density figure as an architectural input ahead of the measurement, and bring-up needs numbers before R5 has any. `qualified` is carried in the record and emitted into the generated devicetree, false in every configuration here, so a tree says on its face which of the two it holds. It is a property of the configuration rather than of the machine, which is why no property asserts its value: a test fixing it would be a test about the calendar.
    **The corpus member is a set of claims that nothing is observable, which is what makes it worth running.** R-15-247s puts no trust gradient on the boundary, so [platform-memclass.s](../corpus/platform-memclass.s) checks that a capability round trip, the tag a data store clears, `cloadtags`, `cbo.zero`, `cbo.scrub`, an atomic, and a bounds violation all answer the same on the second class as on the first, and that the revocation filter is keyed by the loaded capability's base rather than by the class of the slot holding it. A divergence would be the finding; the real difference is a latency constant, which is invisible from inside the machine and is exactly the quantity the memory plan charges placement against.

The count is stated: the composition declares 4096 banks over the second class and [the bank-count exploration contract](bank-count-dse-contract.md) holds that declaration inside the candidate set, but it is a placeholder on its own face while `qualified` is false on both classes, R-15-014a carries the count as undecided delta item (viii), and the freeze contract's §10 records that this instrument does not reach it either. They are held by inequality against the bare plane they include, and the one figure in that sentence that *is* a product of the others, the 40% a sidecar would consume of the largest first class, is computed and compared while its 100% companion is left alone, being a deliberate cap on a 120% product.
    **`compounds` stopped being about one document, and only the group's name survived the widening.** It was written entirely about the estimate archetype band; the two-class placement is the same defect at a different granularity, a synthesis over rows restated by hand where nothing renders wrong when the two part. Making room for it meant splitting the group's `run`, because the band half returns early on three paths and the placement must be decided on all of them.

* [x] **M0.16 · Refresh and discharge sequencer in the Sail platform model** · 5.5 h actual · 0.5%
  * The sequencer lands as §12 matter and nothing else: [sys/memory_sequencer.sail](../model/model/sys/memory_sequencer.sail) carries the composition-fixed cadence table, the bank-staggered phases, the mode-exit discharge path (R-15-247q), and the fail-stop read taken once after a fixed dwell (R-15-247f); the register slave's window enters the address map in [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail) and **answers no requester, at any width, in either direction**; the cadence enters `platform_config` and the attested devicetree beside the class constants M0.14 put there; and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried. **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface** (R-15-247h, [absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17), and nothing in the sequencer reads `mtime`, a counter, an occupancy figure, an access record, or a temperature, which is R-15-247r held by what these functions take rather than by a rule about them.
  * Net change: 963 lines added and 14 removed across 12 files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at 166 keys, seven of them new; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **52 properties against 48**, four of them new; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members; the differential corpus green at **19 of 19** with one member and 8 checks new and the eighteen existing digests unmoved; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 2,733 bytes, inside the 4 KiB ROM region it is written to; each of the twelve new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    **The discharge needed no mechanism of its own, which is R-15-247e read as a model.** The requirement realizes the drain through the cells' *existing* write devices, every write wordline in the bank asserted against grounded write bitlines; this model's ordinary write path is those devices, and a write through it already clears the validity tag of every granule it covers, tag clearing being a property of the write path rather than an instruction (R-15-007r). So the exit-path discharge is a **schedule over the write path** and adds no instruction, no access type, and no second way to reach the array, which is what keeps A-17 an auditor's search of the decode surface rather than a claim about something this batch invented. It also lands where R-15-182 and R-15-060 already put an all-zeroes granule, an untagged NULL with a defined reading, which is why R-15-189j's OFF→ON regeneration pass is obviated rather than scaled to capacity.
    **A window that refuses and a window that is absent are different facts, and only the first can be stated by claiming the address.** An IO address no device claims falls through to the RAM path and reads as zero ([sys/mem.sail](../model/model/sys/mem.sail)), so a slave left unmapped is silent rather than refusing, and *no requester-visible behaviour outside the mode transition* would be a sentence with nothing behind it. Claiming the window and answering every access with a fail-stop is what a program can run into, and [platform-memseq.s](../corpus/platform-memseq.s) runs into it one page from the contrast: the window faults in both directions at every width, and the unclaimed page above it reads zero.
    **Refresh and discharge are modeled differently on purpose, because only one of them has architectural content to execute.** A refresh restores a charge this model does not represent and disturbs nothing observable, so what it can get wrong is the cadence, and what the model carries of it is the deadline arithmetic and a refusal. The crossing goes the way it does for a reason that is not taste: the retention floor is multiplied up into the cycles the cadence is stated in rather than the sweep divided down into whole microseconds, which needs no runtime divisor and rounds nothing, where the other direction could round a cadence into a compliance it does not have.
    **The unit of work is the bank because the walk over the banks is R-15-198's.** Power and reset sequencing is one composition-time sequence table executed by the verified RoT firmware as the only sequencer, so the slave runs a phase and the table walks the phases. The split is also what keeps the exit path a property rather than a measurement: a composed bulk domain is gigabytes, and a whole-domain pass is a run of the emulator rather than something a `$[test]` asserts.
    **The fail-stop's negative reading has no source in a valid composition, and that is stated rather than papered over.** The completion indication is the pass's own answer, negative when a bank's write is refused or a phase reaches no bank; this model has no charge to leave behind and no syndrome to read, so a valid configuration always confirms. What the model holds is the **shape**, read once after a fixed worst-corner dwell with no poll and no retry, and the latch is real architectural state rather than a fault-injection register, so the property exercises the no-retry half through it. A machine that can leave charge behind joins that read as a case rather than as a mechanism, exactly as an uncorrectable codeword joins `cbo.scrub`'s (M0.6h).
    **One validator arm was dead the moment it was written, and running the seeded refusals is what found that.** `config_is_valid` short-circuits and the class record's own check refuses a zero bank count first, so a second message here saying the same thing is a branch no configuration reaches. What that arm has to be instead is the arithmetic's precondition, an early return rather than a refusal, and the general shape is worth stating: a validator group placed after another inherits what that one already refuses, so a message it repeats is unreachable rather than redundant.
* [x] **M0.17 · Per-class bank-count DSE with droop as a hard constraint** · 8 h actual · 0.7%
  * The instrument is a seventh derived view, [the bank-count exploration contract](bank-count-dse-contract.md), governed by R-15-247p: the candidate set with its ground, the pruning predicate and the two objectives stated as expressions over named symbols, one row per symbol with what owes it, the report's shape, and six refusals. **Seven symbols, of which six are pending and one is stated**, the stated one being the tag plane's array-efficiency share R-15-247a fixes.
  * Beside it [tools/bank-dse.py](../tools/bank-dse.py) scores every candidate on the arithmetic that exists without a coefficient: bank bytes, the refresh sweep against the deadline the retention floor sets, the mode-exit discharge dwell, and whether the region divides the count exactly. Every coefficient-dependent column prints the symbol it waits on rather than a number or a blank, and the verdict line states that **no candidate is admitted** and why. **K-58** in a new [banks](../tools/vos/checks/banks.py) group holds the contract's declared point against the composition's, holds it inside the candidate set, holds every symbol the report names against the contract's table, and refuses a pruning coefficient stated while the class's `qualified` flag is false.
  * Net change: 576 lines added and 44 removed across fifteen files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: the checker green on **all 59 rules against 58**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the seven views the register obliges all present and the new one carrying all four of the register entries its five body patterns select; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **fifteen against fourteen**; `bank-dse.py` exiting 0 over **11 candidates, 8 of which clear the shape constraints and none of which is admitted**; `check.py --fix` recomputing the derived-views figure rather than by hand; and both type checkers clean. No WSL run is in this item's evidence: `model/config/verifiedos.json` is read and not written.
  * Six findings.
    **The search cannot be run, and what it is missing is not detail but every coefficient that would make it a search.** Of the three quantities R-15-247p names, none exists as a number. The droop envelope wants a peak bank current and a delivery-network ceiling, both read off power vectors R-15-189i provisions against and nobody has produced. The read energy per bit wants a bitline capacitance, which R-15-247m puts on a repaired macro. The island bandwidth ceiling is neither R4's nor R5's: it is an *output* of the bank count under search times a per-bank width and a TDM schedule M0.8 and M1 have not produced, and R-15-228a forbids the memory plan from producing it. So the contract's table is six pending rows and one stated, and the instrument admits nothing.
    **The declared count is one doubling from the only shape constraint that can fail.** The refresh sweep at 4096 banks is 29,696,000,000 cycles against a 60,000,000,000-cycle deadline, which is 49.5% of it; at 8192 banks it is 59,392,000,000, which is 99.0%; at 16384 it fails outright. Nothing in the register or the configuration said so, because the figure is a product of a bank count, a cadence, and a retention floor that are declared in three different places. **This is the item's one substantive result and it points the wrong way from the objectives**: R-15-247p's two objectives both reward finer banking, R-15-247g's envelope rewards it too, and the deadline is the one term that punishes it, two candidates above the declared point.
    **The hard constraint's asymmetry is what the rule protects, and it fails silently rather than loudly.** A search whose pruning predicate has no operands admits everything or nothing depending on how it is written, and the way that stops being true is not somebody writing *admitted* in a report: it is a coefficient quietly acquiring a value. So K-58 reads the coefficient table's own status column against the composition's qualification flag in the direction that bites: while the class is unqualified, the two pruning symbols must be pending, because a droop envelope with operands would mean a macro had been measured and `qualified` is the one thing that records whether one has.
    **The estimate document disqualifies its own figures as inputs here, and that is load-bearing rather than modest.** [performance-estimates.md](performance-estimates.md) carries a read energy of 0.1 to 0.5 pJ/bit and a refresh energy of about 1 pJ/bit, and closes the same row by saying every figure in it is an order-of-magnitude estimate over a retention corner spanning a factor of sixty, that they deserve independent rederivation, and that they are not a freeze input. Filling `C_bl_per_row` from them would make a placeholder an architectural input, which is exactly what R-15-247m forbids and what M0.14's `qualified` flag exists to prevent, so the contract's refusal list names it.
    **Holding the number meant one more model file inside the checker's reach, and the carve-out moved to where it can be seen.** M0.13 opened it for the welded block size; the bank grant is the second fact of that kind, so the named list moved from the parse that first needed it to [vos/corpus.py](../tools/vos/corpus.py), beside the `model/` exclusion it carves out of. Both the selftest's sandbox and the parses read the one list, so a path a rule reads and the list omits fails every sandbox's baseline rather than passing on the host.
    **R-15-108's parameter list was short the per-class bank count, and the question that left open is now answered rather than carried.** This item found that R-15-247p and R-15-014a (viii) both put the count in the frozen set while R-15-108's criterion enumerated VLEN per class, core count per class, issue width and pipeline depth, the bank/macro/tier-to-island assignment, scratchpad sizes, the CBO block size, the FEC decoders' realization geometry, and the TDM-NoC schedule, with no bank count among them, and booked whether the assignment was that parameter under another name. It is not: an assignment distributes objects and arrays over the banks a map already grants, so it presupposes a set of banks rather than fixing how many exist, which R-15-228a states from the other side by making the map an input to the §8 plan and never an output of it. R-15-108's criterion now names both, and the two figures the composition declares, 64 banks on the first class and 4,096 on the second, are what a one-entry reading would have had to collapse.

**M0 subtotal:** 116.1 h · 10% · 107.6 h complete · open range 6–11 h.

### M1 · Toolchain spine (incl. the CHERI-CompCert prerequisite)

Build the *functional* CHERI-RISC-V CompCert backend **first** (no purecap CertiCoq GC is needed, the on-device path is GC-free, §0), then bring up both roles, the host-side **CertiCoq → Wasm** oracle and the **GC-free on-device lowering** (CompCert-C/VST through CHERI-CompCert; arena extraction via MetaCoq→Rust onto the Rust→CHERI compiler for the allocation-light components), producing runnable purecap artifacts from a trivial Gallina program on the M0 emulator.
Ship the frozen-dialect **assembler, linker, and image composer** (§0) in the same milestone; nothing downstream links without them.
Build the profile-freeze analyzer and backend provenance outputs (§0), then publish its ordered dictionary, outlining, operand-form, bitfield, stack-save, and indexed-address reports against the generated corpus.
Every later milestone is purecap and managed-runtime-free from here.

  * Condition: alive, not abandoned (pushed June 2026; blame merged March 2026). Compartment-aware CompCert holds through every pass with the correctness proof adapted (admits confined to `Stackingproof.v` pending recent changes); recomposition and blame proofs complete; back-translation complete on `ccs-backtranslation`; the top-level RSCC theorem is formalized in `security/RSC.v` but not yet integrated with those three. States Rocq 9.1 / OCaml 5.2.1 / Menhir as its requirement, which is a requirement rather than a measurement: what the pin builds under on this toolchain is M1.2's.
  * Carried features, the CHERI half: the capability backend lives in-tree under `cheririscV/` (33 files: `CapAsm.v`, `CapAsmgen.v`, `CapAsmgenproof*.v`, capability memory model `CapMemory.v`, Georges-et-al calling convention with uninitialized/directed capabilities), emitted via `-dcapasm` with an admittedly incomplete pretty-printer; per the abstract it is "for the most part also implemented in Coq, but not yet fully integrated with CompCert and not verified". Branch `fix-cap-backend` (`1881ed0f`, June 2024) is 15 commits of unmerged backend work ahead; the 2022 `cheri-backend` branch is superseded.
  * Implication for M1.2, where the usability claim is measured rather than inferred and the contingency is answered: the re-homing's real gap is the unverified, unintegrated capability backend and its ISAv8-lineage capability model versus the §4.1 64+1-bit purecap dialect, exactly the residual [inspirations.md](inspirations.md) states.
