  * **Contained.** The CHERI-CompCert backend is a build-time producer that this repository never redistributes, so its non-commercial term reaches nothing this repository offers and [COPYRIGHT.md](../COPYRIGHT.md)'s map is preserved whole. **Both other arms stay open rather than being foreclosed**, and that is part of the call: containment is reversible in a way the other two are not, so it is what a proof-of-concept spends while the first release's floor is recorded and its instruments are not (R-18-004a, R-18-004c).
  * What containment forfeits, stated rather than elided: the toolchain is undistributable, so a third party cannot rebuild the image from source, and the reproducible-bootstrap story and R-05-023a's validation instrument both lean on exactly that. The forfeit is recorded here so that whoever later reaches for the bootstrap claim finds the reason it is not available rather than discovering the term.
  * The arms not taken. **Obtain terms** is the only arm that keeps both the verified backend and an unencumbered platform, at a cost nobody here has yet asked for, and it is priced as two negotiations rather than one: the AbsInt Software Usage Agreement conveys rights in CompCert while `cheririscV/` is SECOMP's own contribution, in which AbsInt has no standing to grant anything. **Route around it** reaches purecap lowering without the CompCert backend, which is a redesign of the compilation lane rather than a substitution inside it: it forfeits the secure-compilation criterion the whole post-M10 hardening program is stated against, and it is the one arm on which VST's own CompCert trees can reintroduce the same term through the concurrency path; acquiring VST through the opam distribution is what does not convey it.
  * **This is one of three questions on the plan that can invalidate work rather than delay it, and it is a decision rather than an edit** (M4.1a and M6.0a are the others). M1.1 records the term and the pin's own `LICENSE` decomposes it: the CompCert verified compiler is under the INRIA Non-Commercial License Agreement, a **revocable** grant running "solely for educational, research, or evaluation purposes", which is not an open-source license and is incompatible with every license this repository's own content is offered under ([COPYRIGHT.md](../COPYRIGHT.md)). M1.2 is the act that spends it, so the call belongs here and is taken here, ahead of anything downstream resting on the term.
  * **The dual-licensed subset does not rescue this, and the decomposition is the finding.** `lib/`, `common/`, twelve `cfrontend/` files, `backend/Cminor.v` with its printer, `cparser/`, `export/`, four per-architecture files, and the build files are dual-licensed LGPL-2.1-or-later; `flocq/` and `MenhirLib/` are LGPL-3.0-or-later; `runtime/` is BSD-3-Clause. **The verified backend passes, the RISC-V backend, and SECOMP's own `cheririscV/` are on none of those lists**, and they are precisely what M1.2 takes. The permissive and copyleft arms cover the parts this milestone does not need.
  * **The same term is reachable through the proof stack, and that is the second place to hold it.** VST's `LICENSE` places "all parts of the VST opam distribution ... (including msl, sepcomp, veric, floyd, concurrency)" under BSD-2-Clause, and carries two CompCert trees beside them. `compcert/` is "dual licensed with a proprietary license and an open-source license" and contains Clight, which is what a sequential refinement requires; `compcert_new/` "contains some files that are not dual-licensed", and VST's own direction is that "you should not use compcert_new unless you satisfy the terms of the CompCert license". The condition is specific rather than general and attaches to VST's *concurrency* path, which §7's L2 linearizability and the per-core kernel of §5 are the two items here that could reach for. It is a clause of the call made in this item rather than a call of its own: under **obtain terms** or **contain it** it adds nothing, and only under **route around it** does it become a second route by which the same license re-enters. Acquire VST through the opam distribution, which does not convey it.
  * Three further non-permissive upstreams sit on this plan and they do not resolve alike. M6.0a is a fork of the same kind and from the same licensor, and needs its own arm because Inria grants Vélus separately from CompCert. M4.1a is a fork of the same kind for a different reason, because a translated kernel specification does reach the shipped image. M2.1 is not, because the emulator fork does not; it carries a containment rule instead.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and the instrument is quoted rather than inferred from lineage in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md), which decomposes the grant at the pin and identifies the material this milestone actually takes as sitting outside the dual-licensed subset. `upstream/SECOMP` is a gitlink and its contents are not tracked: `git ls-files upstream` returns the four submodule paths and no file beneath any of them, so a working tree populated at the pin holds that artifact under the upstream's own grant and this repository conveys none of it.
* [ ] **M1.2 · Re-home the backend to the purecap profile** · 75 h, range 55–95 · 6.5%
  * **The figure is priced against a measured delta rather than against the pin's description, and the delta is what moved it.** Eight axes separate `cheririscV/` from the frozen dialect, enumerated below. Four of them are replacements rather than re-parameterizations, one adds an obligation with no upstream analogue, one is a deletion that turns out to be entangled rather than cheap, one is **landed**, and the eighth, the address model, is named and left out of the sum rather than guessed at, so the range is a floor with one stated omission and not a total. The figure is reconsidered rather than merely carried: axis 1 landing takes work out of it and axis 7 turning out to be entangled with the ABI and the heap model puts a comparable amount back, so it stands where it was set. The old "add 20–40 h if the artifact is unusable" contingency is retired rather than carried: its trigger was a build, the build is green, and what it was reaching for is in the state of the tree that builds rather than in whether it builds. Functional and differential testing stay required, and what the rig can currently adjudicate is stated below rather than assumed.
  * **The artifact builds on this toolchain, and the ground for saying so is a build rather than a populated gitlink.** Staged outside the checkout from the gitlink's own bytes, the pin configures and builds whole: `./configure rv64-linux -ignore-ocaml-version`, then `make depend`, `make proof`, `make extraction`, `make ccomp`, every stage exit 0, 203 `.vo`, and a linked `ccomp`, with `make proof` at 14 min 13 s of wall and 1.9 GB peak, `make extraction` at 1 min 39 s and 1.1 GB, and the link at 2 min 30 s and 163 MB, over twelve cores shared with three concurrent lanes. All 14 files `SECARCH` names in `cheririscV/` compile. Three things the run settles that a reading does not. **The prover is not M1.5's**: `configure` tests `coqc --print-version` against a closed list whose last entry is 9.1.0, and the `rocq-9.1.1` switch carries `rocq` with no `coqc`, which is the branch that answers "Please install the Coq wrapper for this version of Rocq", so the build takes the distribution's Coq **8.20.1**, on that list and needing no `-ignore-coq-version`. OCaml is 5.4.0 against a 4.05-to-4.14 test, which is what `-ignore-ocaml-version` is for and what SECOMP's own README instructs. And `make proof` alone fails on a clean tree, the `%.vo: %.v` rule compiling `$*.v` where the stem resolves through `VPATH` only once `.depend` exists, so `make depend` comes first exactly as the `all` target orders it.
  * **`-dcapasm` emits, and what it emits is what bounds this item.** The flag is wired through `Clflags`, `Driver` and extraction's `print_CapAsm`, and on FP-free C it writes a Gallina program term rather than assembler text. Straight-line register-only code emits clean; nothing past it does. One `int` loop emits a `TODO: __Pbgew__`, a pointer-indexed loop seven such tokens, a struct field access eight, the missing mnemonics being the ordinary word loads and stores through a capability and the comparisons beside them. The pretty-printer is where this sits, and its arithmetic wants stating at the denominator it is actually against. `CapAsm.v` declares **258** instruction constructors, in the `Inductive instruction` running from line 233 to `Pfail` at line 578; the 479 `| P…` lines the whole file carries are those declarations plus every `exec_instr` arm beside them, so counting the file rather than the declaration doubles the denominator and is the reading trap here. Against 258: **228** have a live arm in `backend/PrintCapAsm.ml`'s 748-line instruction match, **202** of which print a `TODO: __<mnemonic>__` literal and **26** a term, and the other **30**, every one a double-precision floating-point form, have no arm at all and fall through, which is the half of the surface this profile deletes in its own right (R-15-039). That is M1.1's "admittedly incomplete pretty-printer" measured, and the incompleteness is the common case rather than the corner.
  * **The correctness proofs are outside the build and every admit is inside it.** `SECARCH` names 14 of the 33 files; the other 19 are not compiled, six of them the `*proof.v` files. Every `Admitted.` and `admit.` in the directory falls in the 14 that are: **35 `Admitted.` and 10 `admit.`**, in `CapMemory.v` (21 and 7), `OCapValues.v` (10), `CapAsmgen.v` (3) and `CapGlobalenvs.v` (1 and 3). What builds is the capability backend's definitions with its correctness argument stubbed.
  * **The step relation is empty at the pin, which is a sharper fact than the admits and is the one that decides what the re-homing starts from.** `CapAsm.v:1845` reads `Definition executeAllowedCap (c : occap) : bool := false.` with a `FIXME` beside it, and that constant gates all five step rules (`:1851`, `:1865`, `:1905`, `:1933`, `:1949`), so **no `step` is derivable and the target language's transition relation is vacuous**. There is no `semantics` record either, the definition that would plug `CapAsm` into CompCert's simulation framework being commented out at `:2052` under a `FIXME priority 1`, which is the reason the six `*proof.v` files sit outside `SECARCH` rather than merely unfinished. And five capability instructions are declared with their semantics commented out beside them: `PCgt` get-object-type (`:1369`), `PCgs` get-sealed-status (`:1381`), `PCseal_e` the sentry mint (`:1428`), `PCCseal` (`:1424`), and `PCinvoke` the sealed-pair domain crossing (`:1176`). `CapAsmgen.v:1434` emits `PCgs` as `make_return`'s intra- versus cross-compartment discriminator, so the generated return path names an instruction the machine does not define. The re-homing therefore starts from a design sketch whose definitions compile, not from a working translation whose parameters move, and that is what the estimate above is against.
  * **The re-homing delta, and the first thing it settles is the shape of the problem.** `cheririscV/` fixes no 128-bit capability *layout*: the capability is abstract in the Cerise lineage, `OCVmem : permission -> locality -> ptrofs -> ptrofs -> ptrofs -> ocsealable` (`OCapValues.v:262`), carrying base, top and address as three full-width `ptrofs` with no exponent, no mantissa and no compression anywhere in the directory, so the abstract state is three words wide where the footprint is two. There is accordingly no `cheri_compressed_cap`-equivalent algebra to re-parameterize, and the 128 bits enter only as a *footprint*: a memory chunk's size and alignment (`CapAST.v:70-71`, `CapMemdata.v:47`, `:109`), a typed slot's count (`CapLocations.v:48-50`, `:67-69`), and the sixteen-byte value-fragment quantity `Q128` (`CapMemdata.v:146`, `:152-153`, `:666-669`), whose all-or-nothing fragment check (`:369-381`) is this model's software surrogate for the validity tag. What has to move is therefore not field widths but the eight axes below.
  * **Axis 1, the capability's storage width, is the one this lane moved, and what it measures is where a re-parameterization's cost actually sits.** Upstream welds CLEN to twice XLEN by reading the capability's width off `Archi.ptr64` (`CapAST.v:57`, `:58`, `:171`), with no `Archi.capwidth` beside it, so a 64-bit machine admits only a 128-bit capability. The lane adds `CapAST.cap128` as a width switch of its own, sets it false, and repoints the **35** sites that select a capability's width at it over six files, `CapAST.v`, `CapMemdata.v`, `CapGlobalenvs.v`, `OCapValues.v`, `CapMachregs.v` and `CapAsmgen.v`, leaving `Archi.ptr64` to mean the pointer width alone. `CTcap` and `CMcap` then resolve to the already-present 64-bit forms, so a capability is 8 bytes at 8-byte alignment, one typed slot and `Q64`'s eight fragments, which is [cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail)'s `cap_size` exactly, and `CapAsmgen`'s `opldc`, `opsc` and `opUsc` select `PClwc`, `PCscw` and `PCUscw` where they selected the 128-bit forms. Three of the 35 are unreachable by search and were found by a failing proof: the `OCVcap`-against-`CTany64` arms of `has_type`, `normalize` and `load_result` (`OCapValues.v:550`, `:1724`, `:1790`) name no capability constructor, so no grep for one finds them.
  * **Axis 1 is landed, against a clean tree rather than an incremental one:** `make clean` and then the full sequence puts all fourteen `SECARCH` files and the 203 `.vo` back at exit 0, `make proof` at 21 min 37 s of wall and 1.9 GB peak over twelve cores shared with three lanes, with extraction and the link green on top. What it cost is the finding rather than the footnote, because the definitions move mechanically and the proofs do not. Upstream discharges every capability case by splitting on `Archi.ptr64`, so each such split now needs the second boolean beside it: **fourteen lines of tactic repair over two files**, in `normalize_type`, `normalize_idem` and `load_result_rettype` (`OCapValues.v`), and `decode_val_undef`, `solve_decode_encode_val_general`, `encode_val_shape` and `decode_val_shape` (`CapMemdata.v`). Two of those selected their residual goals **by index** (`1,2,3,4,5,6,9:`) and the wider split moves them, so both are rewritten to walk the disjunction instead, `repeat first [ left; reflexivity | right ]`, which is index-free and survives a further split rather than needing the indices recounted at each one. One `Section Examples` goal in `CapAsmgen.v` drops a `rewrite ARCHI` that has no subterm left to rewrite; its second component is existential, so the rewrite was never load-bearing. **The change reaches the output**: on a C program that spills a capability, the emitted term carries `PClwc` twelve times where the pin carried `PClcd`, and the stubbed-arm census moves with it, 52 tokens over 2 constructors becoming 64 over 3, because the printer's 26 real arms were written against the 128-bit selection. On a program that loads no capability the term is byte-identical, which is the honest scope of the demonstration. Two things the axis deliberately does not move: the argument-passing layout, `CapConventions1.v:234` and `:258` advancing an outgoing offset by a hard-coded two slots per argument, which is the ABI rather than the width and whose own acceptability and size proofs are commented out at `:356-384` and `:446-474`; and `CapValues.v`, which carries the same three tables but sits outside `SECARCH`, so repointing it would repoint something nothing builds.
  * **Axis 7 is not the cheap deletion it reads as, and the reason is recorded so it is not rediscovered.** `X32` is a **second reserved scratch** rather than a spare: `CapAsm.v:1670` states that no LTL register maps to `X31` or `X32`, and `CapAsmgen.v:919`, `:921` and `:928` use the pair together in the stack-offset sequences (`PCgl_s X32 SPC :: … :: Psubl X31 X32 X31`). Deleting the thirty-third register, which no five-bit field can encode, therefore needs a second scratch found inside 32, which is the ABI question axis 1 already declines to answer rather than a deletion. `DDC` is load-bearing for the same kind of reason: `CapAsmgen.v:1352` and `:1392` load each compartment's heap capability into it across the compartment switch, so removing it is entangled with a heap model this dialect does not have at all (R-08-011). The scalar floating-point file is the only genuinely mechanical part of the axis. What that moves is the order rather than the total: axis 7 is not the cheap one to take first.
  * **Axis 2, the permission field, is a replacement and not a narrowing.** SECOMP's is a twelve-element abstract enumeration `O | RO | RW | RWL | RX | E | RWX | RWLX | URW | URWL | URWX | URWLX` (`OCapValues.v:39-51`) ordered by a hand-written `permFlowsTo` (`:93-140`), crossed with a three-valued locality `Global | Local | Directed` (`:57-60`) under its own `locFlowsTo` (`:65-76`). The profile's is five bits naming one of 32 codepoints, sixteen authority shapes crossed with one global bit, decoded by a total `perms_expand` into a twelve-bit architectural bitmap and narrowed by `perms_narrow`'s largest-admitted-subset search ([cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail), lines 88-184). Neither order embeds in the other: the profile carries access-system-registers, the `load-global`/`load-mutable` transitivity pair, `store-capability`/`store-local-capability`, and a `Permit_Seal`/`Permit_Unseal` split no admitted set holds together (R-15-007o), none of which SECOMP has, and SECOMP carries four uninitialized permissions and a `Directed` locality the profile has none of. Locality does not survive as a field at all, `store-local` being bit 5 of the profile's expanded bitmap and riding the permission code (R-15-074), so the `locality` type and its uses across six compiled files go with it. The two together are read on 167 lines of the fourteen compiled files.
  * **Axis 3, uninitialized capabilities, is the axis the calling convention rests on, so deleting it is a design act rather than a deletion.** `URW`/`URWL`/`URWX`/`URWLX` (`OCapValues.v:48-51`) are neither read- nor write-allowed (`:190-200`); a `U` capability's authority is its *cursor* rather than its bound (`CapMemory.v:280`), it reads strictly below the cursor (`:331`), stores at or below it (`:322`), and a store at offset zero advances it (`:356-373`), which is the frontier discipline the frame layout is built on. `CapMemtype.v:123-133`'s `UNINIT` access kind threads it through every load and store, 26 `PCU*` constructors carry it into the assembly (`CapAsm.v:394-421`), and `CapAsmgen.v` compiles **every** stack access through one: `Mgetstack` and `Msetstack` (`:1063-1083`), `transl_load` and `transl_store`'s stack arms (`:1115-1218`), the frame carve `PCUtoPtr_s X31 SPC SPC :: PCsbase SPC SPC X31` (`:1333-1334`), and `store_zeros_routine`'s byte-at-a-time frame zeroing (`:1275-1285`). The canonical stack capability is `URWL` plus `Directed` (`CapGlobalenvs.v:186-190`). The profile has neither: its stack capability is `perms_stack`, the one shape carrying `store-local` ([cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) line 119), over a frame the static memory plan lays out at composition (R-08-011), so the carve, the zero-initialization and the spill discipline are all re-derived rather than edited.
  * **Axis 4, sealing, diverges in the field and in the authority, and axis 5's sentries diverge with it.** SECOMP's object type is `Definition seal := ptrofs` (`OCapValues.v:24`), so the otype space is the whole address space, and sealing authority is a separate seal-capability *object* holding a range and a cursor, `OCVseal : seal -> seal -> seal -> ocsealable` (`:263`), consumed by `seal_capability`/`unseal_capability` against a bounds check on that cursor (`:1480-1496`); there is no `Permit_Seal` and no `Permit_Unseal` anywhere in the directory, and `CapAsmgen.v:1374-1376` gives each *call site* its own otype by offsetting a per-compartment seal key. The profile's otype is four bits naming sixteen classes, three architecturally reserved and thirteen composition-allocated with no runtime type allocation ([cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail) line 58, [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) lines 49-65), and its sealing authority is two permissions the lattice never admits together. SECOMP's sentry is the `E` permission, upgraded to `RX` on installation into PCC while *preserving the cursor* (`OCapValues.v:1472-1476`), so it does not force entry at the base, and global function entries use a second mechanism again, a **sealed** `RX Global` capability (`CapGlobalenvs.v:180-184`); the profile's are two reserved otypes, a forward edge and a backward edge, with `cjalr` admitting each only in its own role (R-15-008, R-15-071).
  * **Axis 6, bounds, is the one place the delta adds an obligation rather than exchanging one.** SECOMP's bounds are exact by construction, three `ptrofs`, so nothing in `CapAsmgen` ever computes a representable alignment and no narrowing can round. The profile's are CHERI Concentrate at 8 and 6 mantissa bits, byte-exact to 128 bytes and rounding outward above that (R-15-007c), and R-15-007k requires **every** narrowing the profile's code emits to be exactly representable, discharged against the slot plan rather than by a runtime instrument, with `CRAM`, `CRRL` and `CSetBoundsExact` unadmitted. That is a side condition on the backend's emission with no upstream analogue. Its input is R-08-011's slot plan, which **M1.9** produces, so the narrowing rule is written here against a plan that lands two items later, and that ordering is a scheduling fact this cell records rather than a gap in the register: R-15-007k already owns the obligation and already locates it on the plan.
  * **Axis 7, the register file, carries one part that has no encoding.** `CapAsm.v:46-53` declares `X1` through `X32`, which with the separately modelled `X0` is **33** integer registers where stock `riscV/Asm.v` declares 32 and where a RISC-V register field is five bits wide, so the thirty-third is unencodable in this dialect or any other. Beside it sit a full 32-entry double-precision file (`:62-70`) the profile deletes with scalar FP along with the 30 constructors naming it (R-15-039), and `DDC` (`:103`) with its six `exec_load_ddc`/`exec_store_ddc` forms (`:1228-1251`), which is the hybrid mode R-15-001c deletes. Two register conventions are broken beside them and both are the pin's rather than the profile's: `SPC`, the stack capability, is `X3` (`:133`) while `CapAsmgen.v:1290` reserves `X3` for the caller's seal key, so `:1318`'s `PCseal X4 SPC X3` seals `X3` with itself, and the declared `SPPC := X2` (`:135`) that RISC-V's ABI would put the stack in is used nowhere. The merged reading is the one thing that already agrees, `CapAsm.v:43-44` modelling exactly the merged integer-and-capability file [reg_type.sail](../model/model/core/reg_type.sail) carries.
  * **Axis 8, the address model, is named and deliberately left out of the estimate.** SECOMP addresses are `ptrofs` offsets inside CompCert's block model, split three ways by `heap_ptr | stack_ptr | seal_ptr` (`OCapValues.v:401-404`) with `derive_offset` reading the region, over a runtime `Mem.alloc`. The profile is one flat 36-bit physical space (R-15-002a), dense by construction (R-15-002b), with no heap and composition-time allocation. Whether that is a re-homing of the memory model or a replacement of it is what this axis turns on, and nothing measured here answers it, so the figure above excludes it and says so rather than carrying a number nothing stands behind.
  * **One further finding is recorded because it is load-bearing and is not a difference from the profile but a defect in the pin.** `is_directed_store` (`CapMemory.v:296-304`, mirrored at `CapMemtype.v:320-330`) is the clause that stops a stack capability being stashed below itself, and its own comment says it checks the stored value's maximum read authority against *the address it is stored at*. The pattern variable in `| OCVcap c =>` shadows the function's `c` parameter, so `derive_offset k c ofs` reads the address off the value being stored rather than off the authority it is stored through, and the condition becomes a statement about the value alone. Under the `UNINIT` kind, whose offsets are negative by construction (`CapMemtype.v:126`), it then reads as `a <= a + ofs` and fails for the offsets the discipline uses. Nothing in this repository rests on it; it is recorded so that a lane re-deriving the directed-store rule reaches for the comment rather than the code.
  * **The prerequisite this box did not hold is held, and what each output turns out to be is the finding.** The generated `compcert.ini` puts `-mabi=lp64d` on the preprocessor, the assembler and the linker, and no `riscv64` cross toolchain is installed, so `ccomp` reaches a host `gcc` that rejects the flag. Relaxing the **preprocessor** line alone is what `-S` needs and is what this lane does; the assembler and linker lines keep the flag, assembling and linking an image needing the cross toolchain that is M1.4's to stand up. On a C program shaped to [`base-integer`](../corpus/base-integer.s)'s and [`base-memory`](../corpus/base-memory.s)'s subjects, the RV64I arithmetic and W forms, every branch condition, loads and stores at each width through a pointer, and an indexed walk, `ccomp -S -dcapasm` exits 0 and writes both. The `-S` half is 87 lines of stock lp64d RISC-V carrying **no capability mnemonic at all**, `x2` as a stack pointer with `sd` and `ld` through it, because `-S` reaches the `riscV/` backend and not `cheririscV/`: it is a baseline to diff against rather than purecap output. The `-dcapasm` half is a 280-line Gallina term carrying **56 `TODO: __<mnemonic>__` tokens over 38 distinct constructors**, and the census locates the printer's incompleteness rather than counting it: the ordinary long-word arithmetic and the branches (`Paddl` ten times, `Pcvtw2l` six, all six `Pb*l` forms), the capability loads and stores (`PClb` through `PCsw`, with `PCld` four times), and the uninitialized stack forms the frame discipline runs on (`PCUlb` through `PCUsw`). No corpus member is an input to either: all twenty-five are hand-written dialect assembly and `ccomp` takes C. One limit of the `-S` half is worth recording beside its use as a baseline: on a function with twelve pointer arguments the **stock** `backend/PrintAsm.ml` raises an uncaught `Failure "not implemented"`, where `-dcapasm` compiles the same program and writes its term before the crash. The baseline is therefore narrower than the capability path it is meant to be diffed against, which is the reverse of the expected order.
  * **The differential rig's shapes do not meet, and what is missing is an executor rather than a corpus.** [differential-corpus.md](differential-corpus.md) §6 adjudicates two executors record by record over its §4 commit-trace schema, and neither `ccomp` output is a record stream. Three things stand between the shapes and each is nameable rather than a gap in the corpus. There is no encoder from `CapAsm` to the frozen dialect's binary, which is **M1.4**'s. There is no executable interpreter of `CapAsm`: `exec_instr` is Coq, extraction targets `Asm` and not `CapAsm`, and the relation such an interpreter would implement is the empty one `executeAllowedCap` gates. And the register files disagree, 33 integer registers with a `DDC` beside them against the merged 32, so an `X` record would not align even where both sides emitted one. This item's instrument is therefore not yet the trace rig, and naming which members it would run is premature; what it has instead is the build, the delta, the axis measured above, and the `-S` baseline the re-homed output will diff against.
  * **Two duties R-18-014a places on this backend are scoped in here rather than discovered later, and one of them has no item carrying it.** R-18-014a makes backend *completeness* include selection of the frozen §15 capability indexed load and store (R-15-007e) and of bitfield extract and insert (R-15-067a) wherever the pattern matches. Three of those rows are decided at the **final freeze** and not before: the indexed form's scale immediate is a composition-time parameter selected against the emitted mix (R-15-007g), and the bitfield pair's field-specifier form, whether the insert form is carried, and **whether the pair is carried at all** are re-derived from actual generated output (R-15-067d). So each is one selection pattern written against a row that can move, and one of them may be deleted outright; the dependency runs both ways, the freeze wanting generated output and the output wanting the frozen rows, and R-15-014a settles it by making the provisional profile a total compilation target that the backend targets without waiting. What that leaves is rework at the margin, priced inside the range above rather than as a separate arm. The second duty is **outlining and tail merging**, which R-15-036o assigns to this backend as the owner of the §13 duplication transforms and R-18-003c requires landed before the final freeze: it is neither a re-homing nor baseline target support, it presupposes a backend already emitting correct purecap code, and **no checklist item carries it**. That is an owed item rather than a placement inside this one, and it is recorded here so the freeze does not discover it.
  * **Gate satisfied at M1.1a: contained.** The backend is a build-time producer this repository never redistributes, so the re-homing may proceed and the non-commercial term reaches nothing offered here. Two things follow into this cell rather than out of it: the work must not create a path by which the backend or a derivative of it is conveyed, and the cell still prices the re-homing alone, no arm's own cost inside it. The forfeit the arm carries, an image a third party cannot rebuild, is M1.1a's to state and M1.4's and R-05-023a's to live with.
  * **The containment decides the shape of this item's evidence, and it leaves one question open that is not a technical one.** A re-homed `cheririscV/` is a derivative of the backend, so it cannot live in this repository under [COPYRIGHT.md](../COPYRIGHT.md)'s map, and every measurement above is accordingly taken in a build lane outside every checkout and reported here rather than landed as a diff: the delta, the axis, and the two `ccomp` outputs are the artifact, and the patched tree is not. **The re-homed tree lives at `c:\Users\symbi\source\repos\verifiedos-cheri-compcert`, a local git repository with no remote, published nowhere**, on the same footing as the CHERI-QEMU fork M2.1 stands up. That is not a distribution, so the non-commercial term is untouched and containment holds; nothing there is vendored here and nothing here cites it as a dependency. Its first commit is the pin extracted verbatim, so the re-homing reads as a patch series against upstream rather than as a tree, and the axis above is its second, `1e79ed1`. What that buys is that M1.3, M1.6 and M1.7 resume from disk rather than rebuilding from the pin, which is what a build lane could not give. Two questions stay open and neither is technical: whether anybody maintains a fork of a non-commercially-licensed compiler over the life of this plan, and whether M1.1a's route-around-it arm should be re-weighed now that the thing being contained is measured, that arm having been priced against containing a *verified* backend where what builds has an empty step relation.

* [ ] **M1.4 · Re-home LLVM MC/`lld` and compose static images** · 22.5 h, range 15–30 · 2.0%
  * Exclude general dynamic linking.
  * **The upstream is pinned and its terms are read, so this item starts against a named edition rather than against whatever is current.** `upstream/llvm-project` is a gitlink at `ca7933e4`, the commit `llvmorg-22.1.8` names, and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries the row. `LICENSE.TXT` at that pin is **Apache-2.0 with LLVM-exception**: permissive, no copyleft term, no non-commercial restriction, and so the only licence on the three that gate this plan's toolchain which constrains neither the arm taken nor the arm left open. The exception waives the notice conditions of Apache §4(a), (b) and (d) for portions embedded in object form by compilation, which is the clause a compiler's own runtime pieces raise rather than one this item spends. The gitlink is unpopulated on purpose, this item not having started; a clone that needs it runs `git submodule update --init upstream/llvm-project`.
  * One reading trap the pin records against: an annotated tag and the commit it names are different objects, and `git ls-remote --tags --refs` prints the **tag object**. `llvmorg-22.1.8` is `e0130735` as a tag and `ca7933e4` as a commit, and a gitlink stores the commit, so the pin above is the second. A row transcribed from the first would disagree with `git submodule status` on a tree nobody had populated yet.
  * **The deferred half of this item has a start-from, which the hardening program's opening is where to weigh.** Verified assembly, linking and image construction are deferred exactly like the backend's own proof (§0), and the Coq line covering two of those three exists: **CompCertELF** carries verified separate compilation from C through to ELF object files on top of Stack-Aware CompCert, and a **framework for developing verified assemblers for the ELF format** instantiates a parameterized template for the complete x86 and RISC-V assembly languages, generating verified instruction encoders and decoders from *declarative instruction specifications* through CSLED at about 1.1% average slowdown once optimized. A declarative instruction specification is a thing this repository already holds twice, in the model's own `encdec` clauses and in [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py)'s mnemonic table, and it is exactly the input that template consumes, which is what makes this worth weighing rather than merely noting.
  * Four things bound how much of it is available, and the first is the one that bites earliest. Both artifacts are CompCert extensions, so the first question at the milestone that would take either is whether the **INRIA Non-Commercial License Agreement** [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) decomposes reaches them; that is read from each artifact's own licence file at that milestone rather than inferred here from the lineage, and the containment arm M1.1a took is what it would be read against, an assembler being a build-time producer and so on the right side of that line. Neither is CHERI, so a capability-carrying encoding, a tag-bearing image and the sealed forms sit outside what the template covers and are the half that stays authored. Neither is this dialect, so the RISC-V instantiation is a template to re-home rather than a backend to adopt, which is the same act M1.2 prices for the compiler and not a smaller one. And whether this or R-05-023a's decompilation into logic is the covering instrument for the source-correspondence theorem is a call at the hardening program's opening: this cell records a candidate and decides nothing.
* [x] **M1.5 · Run the CertiCoq-to-Wasm oracle** · 3 h actual · 0.3%
  * The loop closes end to end ([tools/wasm-oracle/](../tools/wasm-oracle/)): `demo.v`'s `Nat.eqb (fib 20) 6765` compiled by `CertiRocq Compile Wasm` and run on stock Node prints `true`, the §0 inner loop with no cross-toolchain, no image, and no machine model involved.
  * Release rather than pin: the oracle installs **CertiRocq 0.9.1 for Rocq 9.1** (`rocq-certirocq.0.9.1+9.1`, MIT), which carries the merged CPP 2025 CertiCoq-Wasm backend (`theories/CodegenWasm`, proven against WasmCert-Coq) that the 0.9 opam release predates, and which depends on `rocq-metarocq-erasure-plugin` and `rocq-metarocq-safechecker-plugin` at `>= 1.5.1`, released MetaRocq rather than the unreleased 9.1 branch. Neither a source commit nor a compatibility patch is carried here. Rocq is 9.1.1 by the release's own `rocq-core {>= "9.1" & < "9.2~"}` constraint, not staleness, and it is this oracle's alone: **M1.2 does not share it** and builds against the distribution's Coq 8.20.1, for the two reasons M1.2 measures.
  * **The retirement the release made available is taken, and it reaches exactly what it was booked to reach.** The pinned commit `4f53ca97` and its two-site patch are gone, and the patch is not merely unnecessary but inapplicable: 0.9.1 already carries `inlining` inside `make_unsafe_passes` and no longer holds the `erasure_config` field the patch moved it from, and the two plugin sources it edited have moved from `plugins/cplugin` and `plugins/plugin` to `cplugin/` and `plugin/`. The prover does not move, so nothing this oracle stands on moves. Two caveats keep this a refresh and not a rebuild, and both stand as booked. A release that adds a backend is not thereby a release that has exercised it here, so the retirement is green **only against the same `demo.v` round trip**; and CertiRocq's `< 9.2~` constraint still fixes the prover, so a later Rocq bump is a separate act with the proof gate on the other end of it. What this does not reach is the claim above that the same version serves M1.2, which rests on SECOMP's constraint rather than on CertiRocq's and **is not refreshed by a CertiRocq release**.
  * **The round trip runs in an opam switch rather than in the container, because the container's base image has no arm64 build.** `rocq/rocq-prover` publishes `linux/amd64` alone at `9.1`, `9.1.1`, `9.1-ocaml-4.14-flambda` and `9.1-native-ocaml-4.14-flambda`, and this box is arm64, so a container here is an emulated one and the switch is what runs. The release is installed into a switch of its own, beside rather than into the proof gate's `rocq-9.1.1`, which that gate reads while this installs. What the evidence therefore covers is the half the retirement turns on, the package and the dependency closure opam resolves for it; the [Dockerfile](../tools/wasm-oracle/Dockerfile) installs the same package by name and version, and its base image and repository configuration are the half this evidence does not reach.
  * Refresh evidence: `rocq-certirocq.0.9.1+9.1` installed from `rocq-released` on `ocaml-base-compiler.4.14.2`, resolving `rocq-core` 9.1.1, `rocq-metarocq-erasure-plugin` and `rocq-metarocq-safechecker-plugin` at 1.5.1+9.1, `coq-wasm` 2.2.0 and `coq-compcert` 3.17; `rocq c demo.v` emitting a 32,415-byte `demo.oracle_demo.wasm`, and `node --stack-size=10000000 run_demo.mjs demo.oracle_demo.wasm` on Node 22.22.1 printing `true` at exit 0. `run_demo.mjs` is unchanged and needed no change: 0.9.1 exports `main_function`, `out_of_mem` and `result` under the same names the harness reads.
  * Environment findings, booked for later lanes: the container recipe exists for the prover rather than for privilege. CertiRocq pins Rocq 9.1.1 and the host gate runs the same version: [tools/proof-gate.py](../tools/proof-gate.py) compiles the shipped proofs under the `rocq-9.1.1` opam switch, so one prover version serves the container and the gate. It is a switch of its own rather than a package in the Sail one because `rocq-core` caps dune below the version the Sail packages are built against; the two therefore sit in separate places rather than on one PATH, which is why [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) resolves the prover instead of assuming it. Neither privilege nor toolchain argues for a container: the distribution carries gcc 15.2.0 and runs as root with no login user for `sudo` to matter to, and no container runtime is installed on this box in either the guest or the host, so a lane that wants one stands it up rather than finds it. The WSL VM idles out between commands and takes containers with it, which is the stock WSL2 `vmIdleTimeout` (60 s after every instance stops) rather than a runtime problem; the switch that disables it, `[wsl2] vmIdleTimeout=-1`, exists only in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`, global to every distribution and permanent until a human deletes it, so the fix is scoped into the repository instead: every loop in [tools/model.py](../tools/model.py) starts a detached, idempotent, self-expiring keepalive (8 h default, `VOS_KEEPALIVE_HOURS=0` opts out, `model.py keepalive --stop` ends one early), which holds one instance up so the timer never starts and leaves nothing behind when it lapses; the Docker lanes take the same lease directly (`wsl -u root -e python3 tools/model.py keepalive`), and the `certicoq-oracle` container keeps `--restart unless-stopped` for teardowns that happen anyway. Cisco Umbrella re-signs `release-assets.githubusercontent.com`, so the Windows-exported proxy root CA must be installed into any Linux trust store that fetches GitHub release assets.
  * WSL Containers (`wslc.exe`) declined, revisit at GA: Microsoft's in-box container runtime is Docker-shaped, unlicensed, and runs in its own session VM whose memory returns to the host, but it needs WSL ≥ 2.9.3 from the pre-release channel (this box is 2.7.11), it is public preview with GA targeted for fall 2026, and it moves the wrong finding. The idle-out is not a runtime problem, and on the proxy it regresses: [microsoft/WSL#40945](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/issues/40945) (open, filed 2026-06-29 against 2.9.3) reports the `wslc` backend session VM receives *no* proxy environment, `wslc system session run env` returns only `PATH` where an ordinary distro at least gets `http_proxy`, and Windows root CAs still do not propagate into any Linux store, so the registry pull happens in a session VM this lane cannot reach with the Umbrella CA and the documented workaround is to pull in a distro and `wslc load` the tar. Preview also lacks Compose and `build --platform`, and does not support build contexts on the WSL filesystem (immaterial here: [tools/wasm-oracle/](../tools/wasm-oracle/) builds from the Windows side). The live reason to revisit is idle memory, not container lifecycle.
* [ ] **M1.6 · Stand up GC-free lowering routes** · 18.5 h, range 12–25 · 1.6% · Parallel with M1.4
  * **Two of the routes have a packaged start-from, and the absence this item carries is on the target rather than on the extraction.** The MetaCoq-to-Rust half is released rather than a research artifact: `AU-COBRA/coq-rust-extraction` is a Rocq plugin built on ConCert's verified MetaCoq erasure procedure and distributed on opam, erasure being the step that carries a computational-soundness proof. **MCQC** is where §0 puts it, `mit-pdos/mcqc`, Gallina to C++17 with no runtime and no collector. Between them they are the precedent §0 claims and not more than it.
  * Three caveats separate that precedent from a route. `coq-rust-extraction`'s verified half is the *erasure* and not the Rust emission, so what it buys is a proof about the step above the backend rather than a certified backend; its demonstrated targets are smart contracts rather than systems code; and the arena discipline is the caller's to impose rather than something the plugin supplies. MCQC is a Haskell tool over Coq's JSON extraction, which makes it a standing existence proof rather than a maintained tool this lane rides. And the terms of both are unread here, as every start-from's are until the milestone that would incorporate one reads them.
  * **The genuine absence is the target, and it is the priority-zero item §0 already books.** No Rust toolchain emits RV64 purecap. The CHERIoT fork builds `compiler-builtins`, `core` and `alloc` for a **32-bit** CHERIoT target, with compartments, MMIO and the platform calling conventions still unimplemented; CyberHive's `rust-cheri` targets **Morello** rather than RISC-V. So the arena route's extraction half is nearly free and its compiler half is unbuilt by anyone, which is the reverse of the way the pairing reads.
  * One 2026 artifact is recorded because it presents as a further route and is not one on this plan's own rule: a Rocq-to-microcontroller pipeline exists that extracts to Scheme and runs it on a bare-metal continuation-passing virtual machine. A VM in the image is the managed runtime §0 bans, in the same sentence that bans the collector and for the same two reasons, so it is inadmissible here on the discipline rather than on quality. Naming it costs less than re-deriving that when somebody proposes it.

### M2 · Fast emulator

The CHERI-QEMU fork (§10), curated to the frozen profile: corpus-lockstep green against the M0 emulator, and booting M1's purecap hello-world as its exit test.
It gates on M0, not M1, so it proceeds in parallel with the toolchain spine; from here it is the daily driver and the Sail emulator is the CI cross-check.

Gate M2.2–M2.3 on measured Sail-emulator performance. If Sail is sufficient through M6, defer the 42 h those two items price beyond M8.

* [ ] **M2.1 · Fork CHERI-QEMU and narrow compressed capabilities** · 13.5 h, range 9–18 · 1.2% · Parallel
  * **State: the library is narrowed and cross-checked, and the fork is cut as far as anything can compile before M2.2.** The two upstreams are gitlinks, `CTSRD-CHERI/cheri-compressed-cap` at `78a34ba5` and `CTSRD-CHERI/qemu` at `d0bb921c`, which is the tip of that project's own default branch `qemu-cheri` and carries `VERSION` 7.0.0. The 64+1-bit instantiation agrees with the Sail model over **21,546 vectors the model itself emitted, with no disagreement and no parameter mismatch**, beside the model's fifteen format properties ported and holding. The base is cloned at that commit on branch `verifiedos/m21` in the separate repository the containment rule puts it in, the format is in the vendored library's header chain and its target-selection macros, and **the library builds inside the fork's tree with its own suite at 590 of 590 passing**, so the five formats it already carried are unregressed. The curation is **four files, 820 insertions and 2 deletions**, which is the size a re-parameterization should be.
  * **The fork made one assumption the frozen profile breaks, and finding it is most of what cutting the fork bought.** `cheri_defs.h` derived the capability width from the target word size: 32-bit target to a 64-bit capability, 64-bit target to a 128-bit capability, `#error` otherwise. A capability is twice a register on every target that fork carries, and the frozen profile is the combination the chain has no arm for, **a 64-bit capability on a 64-bit target**, the register being the capability with a 36-bit address inside it and the tag out of band. The width is now stated by the target with the old inference kept as the default, and the RV32 arm is refused outright rather than given a plausible format, this profile having none (R-15-005). That is where *the capability is the register* stops being a sentence and becomes a fact the emulator is built on, and anything downstream assuming `CHERI_CAP_SIZE == 2 * TARGET_LONG_SIZE` is now wrong where a compiler can find it.
  * **Where the build forces a choice the refusal is the choice.** The instantiation supplies no `flags` accessor, no execution-mode enum, no user permissions, no reserved-field accessor, no level bits, and none of `is_representable_cap_exact`, `get_alignment_mask` or `checked_setbounds`. A zero-width definition or a plausible zero would let a port read a field this format does not have and be wrong silently; left absent, each is a compile error at the line that wanted it, and that error list is M2.2's worklist rather than a defect here.
  * **What does not compile, stated exactly.** `qemu-system-riscv64cheri` does not link and cannot until M2.2, for two independent reasons. The 79 symbols below are the first. The second is a fact about this machine rather than about the tree: it carries no QEMU configure stack, no meson and no `glib-2.0` or `pixman-1` development packages, and installing one on a VM three lanes share to watch a build fail for reasons already enumerated is not a trade this item takes. The format-selection edit is therefore checked by a test that quotes the macro chain verbatim and holds that it pastes to `cc64v_`, which is the half of that edit capable of producing a wrong-but-valid identifier; the other half is checked when M2.2 builds.
  * **The narrowing is a new parameterization and not a selection among the library's existing ones, which is what this item's estimate is against.** Upstream's 64-bit instantiation is CHERI Concentrate over a **32-bit** address in a 64-bit capability, where this profile is a 64-bit capability over a 36-bit address, so the phrase *already carrying 64- and 128-bit instantiations* names a neighbour rather than a starting point. Three things move that are not widths and none is reachable by `#define`. The exponent is an explicit five-bit field carrying its own case flag IEEE-754 fashion, where upstream sets an internal-exponent bit and steals three bits from **each** mantissa to hold the exponent, so the frozen format is *more* precise in the normalized case at the same mantissa width and upstream's split-exponent macros have no reading at these widths. The permission field names one of 32 enumerated sets where upstream's is one bit per permission, so every operation over it is a lattice operation. And the address is narrower than its container, where upstream's 64-bit format fills a `uint32_t` exactly, which makes upstream's cursor sign-extension a no-op there and would move every bounds decode in the top half of the space here. What **is** inherited unchanged is the part that is genuinely Concentrate: the three-bit region correction, the representable limit, the derivation of the top mantissa's high two bits, the wrap correction, and the shape of setbounds, along with the field-macro layer, included from the pin rather than copied.
  * **The oracle is the model and the vectors are its output, not a transcription.** The rig compiles the model's own `prelude`, `core/xlen.sail`, `core/cap_format.sail` and `core/cap_common.sail` together with a generator that calls those functions and prints what they return, then requires the C to reproduce every line. That is upstream's own method, its `test/regen_sail_c_code.sh` pointing Sail-generated C at its C library, with the vectors crossing as text so that no adapter sits between the two implementations. It reaches decode over every exponent field, `setCapBounds` across the byte-exact threshold and at every power of two, address movement and both offset forms, the representability check, sealing over all sixteen object types, the permission lattice over all 32 codepoints with every single-bit mask, and the bounds check.
  * **Finding, and it is about the algebra rather than about either implementation: `setCapBounds`'s containment property holds over a domain that nothing states.** The domain is a requested top at or below 2^36. Above it the decoded top wraps below its own base, so the derived region does not contain the requested one; the model does exactly this and the cross-check agrees on the case, so it is not a divergence. It is unreachable with a tag, `CSetBounds` computing `inBounds` and clearing the tag on precisely the test that fails there. What is owed is that **R-15-007a's representation-correctness proof state the condition** rather than carrying containment unconditionally, and that the format's `$[test]` harness reach the edge it presently stops short of. Reported here rather than closed: no register entry governs this item, R-17-048a being the rationale for owning a second-lineage executor rather than a condition on one.
  * **The Rust device-model option this item was to price is measured and refused at this base.** [The alternatives entry](architectural-alternatives.md) books it contingent on the CHERI-QEMU base having rebased far enough forward to carry QEMU's Rust build; the pinned tree is QEMU 7.0.0 with no `rust/` and system emulation still under `softmmu/`, five major releases short of the release that first carries it. Taking the option means carrying the CHERI capability layer across that rebase, which is larger than the fork it would serve. The option reopens at a later re-pin, the fork being actively committed to, so it is refused at this pin rather than closed.
  * **Where M2.1 stops is where the library's consumers begin, and that boundary is measured rather than asserted.** `target/cheri-common/` at the pinned base binds the library through one macro pair and reaches it at **124 call sites naming 79 distinct symbols**, and four families of those name state the frozen format does not carry: the capability-mode flag (`get_flags`, `update_flags`, the `Mode` enum and its two values), the reserved field (`get_reserved`, `PERMS_RESERVED_ONES`), user permissions (`MAX_UPERM`, `UPERMS_ALL`, `UPERMS_SHFT`), and the level bits (`PERM_LEVEL`, `PERM_ELEVATE_LEVEL`, `PERM_STORE_LEVEL`, `MANDATORY_LEVEL_BITS`). Six further permission bits and two object types have no reading in an enumerated lattice or in a two-edge sentry split, and the rounding surface the fork calls (`get_alignment_mask`, `checked_setbounds`) is the one R-15-007k deletes. The instantiation therefore implements none of them, deliberately: an absent field given a zero-width definition still reads as a field a port will try to use. Deleting those readers is the decode surface's work and is M2.2's, not this item's, which is why the re-price stays on one cell.
  * **The terms are read at the pin rather than asserted from the project's reputation, and both of them are.** QEMU's own `LICENSE` releases the emulator as a whole under **GPL version 2**, makes a file carrying no licensing information of its own version 2 or later at the recipient's option, restricts four paths to version 2 and no later version (`bsd-user/`, `linux-user/`, `hw/vfio/`, `hw/xen/xen_pt*`), and leaves the Tiny Code Generator mostly BSD or MIT. The fork is copyleft on every one of those arms, which is all the containment rule needs, and the version-2-only term reaches it only if it keeps the user-mode trees, which one bare-metal machine type does not want. **The library this item exists to narrow is a separate and permissive work**: `CTSRD-CHERI/cheri-compressed-cap` is **BSD-2-Clause**, SRI International and the University of Cambridge, so an instantiation of it at the frozen 64+1-bit widths is not a derivative work of QEMU and is not bound to the fork's repository. That is a fact about where the work may sit and not an obligation to move it: the instantiation sits beside the fork, because the library and the decode surface that will use it are read and changed together, and its copy of upstream is taken from the library's own repository rather than from the fork's vendored subtree, so nothing about its provenance depends on the placement. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries both reads at the pins, along with the one detail the tree-wide read adds to the library's own: a single `test/` file under `Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception`, in the fuzz harness the narrowing does not take.
  * The containment rule that follows is a rule rather than a gate: the fast emulator is a development instrument that no shipped image links against or embeds, so it lives in its own repository under those terms and nothing it touches propagates to the platform. What the rule forbids is the shortcut, vendoring any part of it into this tree, which would put a copyleft term on a repository that currently carries none. The differential rig reaches it as a separate executor over the trace schema, which is the interface that keeps the two apart.

### M3 · Boot chain

RoT core + firmware (§2), M-mode firmware (§3), crypto core (§4) → both emulators reach the Machine-mode kernel.

* [ ] **M3.1 · Add RoT configuration and peripherals to the curated Sail tree** · 18 h, range 12–24 · 1.6%

* [ ] **M3.2 · Implement RoT firmware in Gallina** · 22.5 h, range 15–30 · 2.0%
* [ ] **M3.3 · Implement M-mode firmware in Gallina** · 18 h, range 12–24 · 1.6%
* [ ] **M3.4 · Integrate verified cryptographic artifacts** · 26.5 h, range 18–35 · 2.3%
  * Prefer pinned upstream verified implementations over fresh authoring.
  * **That preference is honoured where a Coq-native artifact exists and cannot be for the two post-quantum schemes, because none exists anywhere.** Every ML-KEM and ML-DSA verification effort in the field grounds its correctness in F\*, EasyCrypt, CBMC, HOL Light or Isabelle; Rocq appears in that ecosystem only as the Jasmin compiler's meta-theory, and the one result joining a Rocq proof to ML-KEM states the join to the EasyCrypt half as informal. The single Rocq-native lattice artifact is a verified number-theoretic transform, and it is the transform alone rather than a scheme, in an unmerged personal fork of Fiat-Crypto. So authoring the two schemes in Gallina from FIPS 203 and FIPS 204 is the only route consistent with the two-language rule rather than a shortfall against the preference, and §0's "named Coq-native destination" for the interim widening is a destination this repository builds rather than one it waits for.
  * **Three routes shorten the authoring and none of them removes it, so each is named with what it is worth and what it is not.** The first is the verified NTT above, read at its own claim rather than at the summary of it: it specifies the transform in Rocq and *derives* verified complete and incomplete NTTs for several schemes, which makes it a parameterized derivation over the largest component ML-KEM and ML-DSA share rather than one transform. It is still a component and not a scheme, it is unmerged, and it publishes no repository with the paper, so it is read and re-derived here rather than pinned. The second is **hax**, whose Rocq backend takes the Rust that libcrux's ML-KEM is already written in; its own project grades that backend **experimental** where the F\* one is stable, which puts it below the bar an admitted artifact clears and makes its output a draft to review rather than a thing to take. The third is the **Jasmin-to-SSProve** line, which composes the Jasmin compiler's correctness theorem with a translation into SSProve and has carried AES against a machine-readable specification in a few thousand lines of Coq; `mlkem-libjade` is end-to-end verified ML-KEM in Jasmin, so the route exists in principle for this scheme too.
  * Four caveats hold that third route to what it is. It lands in **SSProve**, which is §5's reduction layer rather than an implementation lane, so what arrives is a Coq object about a Jasmin program and not the Gallina reference this milestone owes. Jasmin's assembly target is AMD64, which is not a machine on this plan. `mlkem-libjade`'s functional-correctness proof presently **assumes its SHA-3 is correct**, an assumption its maintainers expect to discharge during 2026, so an oracle pinned there carries that hole until they do. And the specification the Jasmin proof is stated against is EasyCrypt's transcription of FIPS 203 rather than FIPS 203, which is the widening R-05-022 books rather than an escape from it.
  * What none of the three changes is the disposition: no Coq-native ML-KEM or ML-DSA implementation exists, so the destination §0 names is still one this repository builds. What they change is the first day of building it, and the estimate above is priced against authoring from the standards rather than against any of them, so it is generous by however much one of them turns out to carry.
  * **The dispositions, per primitive.** Field arithmetic is **Fiat-Crypto**, incorporated: Coq-native, permissive, and the only candidate admissible to the trust base. SHA-256 and the DRBG take **VST's own BSD-2-Clause Coq layer**, which is the survey's find and which no document here had named: `sha/` carries the Gallina specification and its refinement proof, and `hmacdrbg/` carries the functional specification, the NIST known-answer vectors, and an FCF security proof. SHA-3, both AEADs, ML-KEM and ML-DSA are **authored in Gallina** against the standards. Everything else is an oracle and enters nothing.
  * **Two clauses of that carry a reason beyond availability.** The DRBG is HMAC-DRBG-SHA-256 because R-05-004a makes the DRBG's computational security a named assumption the masking theorems rest on, and VST's FCF proof is the Coq-native object that later discharges it, so the choice made for a functional reference pre-pays a deferred obligation; a Keccak-based construction would ride the vector unit better and forfeit that proof, which is a deliberate trade rather than a default. And the Gallina Keccak is validated against **the same FIPS 202 and ACVP vectors as the Sail unit** R-15-056 through R-15-059 freeze, so the standard is transcribed once and the two transcriptions become a differential pair rather than two independent chances to be wrong.
  * **Oracles are pinned in pairs where the primitive is authored, and never as a source of files.** A defect in an oracle's own *specification* is invisible to a differential test against its *behaviour*, which is what a 2026 defect series against one such library demonstrated, so ML-KEM and ML-DSA take two oracles of independent verification lineage rather than one, and the FIPS vectors are the authority that breaks ties. One candidate is excluded outright and on terms rather than on quality: **the most prominent Jasmin ML-KEM artifact carries no licence file at all**, so it may be pinned and read and may never be copied, vendored, or extracted from.
  * **The vectors are fetched rather than vendored.** The ACVP sets carry no usage restriction, their own notice granting use, copy, distribution and modification, but they carry two notice conditions, keeping the notice intact and marking modifications with date and nature, and one set alone is about 110 MB. So they take the fetched-at-build-time shape [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) already uses, a URL and an integrity hash against a pinned commit, which is also what makes a vector revision reproducible rather than silently floating under the acceptance evidence a milestone records.
  * Two things this cell records and does not decide. **Fiat-Crypto is offered under three licences at the taker's option and the election is a licensing call**, the permissive arms keeping notice and patent-termination provisions out of the tree and the Apache arm buying an express patent grant. And **VST is acquired through its opam distribution rather than by pinning the repository**, because that repository carries `compcert/` and `compcert_new/` in tree and pinning it would re-raise the non-commercial term M1.1a already decomposed; the Coq sources are taken and the C is not, being OpenSSL-derived and mbedTLS-derived respectively.

* [ ] **M3.6 · RoT discharge and admission sequence** · 16.5 h, range 11–22 · 1.4%
  * The crown-jewel specification R-15-247d confers, riding R-15-198's sequence table rather than standing beside it. Ordered: requesters in reset, then tag-plane discharge, then a fixed worst-corner dwell, then a single completion read, then fail-stop on a negative reading, then the domain addressable; and separately admitted after that, the data sanitization before measured execution.
  * Covers cold boot, watchdog reset, deep-sleep wake, every OFF→ON transition involving a second-class domain, and the R-15-247q mode-exit path.
  * Acceptance: no path admits a partially sanitized bank, and no timing on either the success or the timeout path varies with discharge speed.

### M4 · Kernel

Gallina microkernel (§5), one instance per emulated core; capability/IPC tests green host-side (Wasm) and on-emulator (RV64, both machines).

* [x] **M4.1 · Decide revocation sweep quanta** · 1.5 h actual · 0.1%
  * **Dropped.** R-08-007's incremental, preemptible, per-core background-slot form stands unamended, and the [quiescent-point declination](architectural-alternatives.md) carries the evaluation, the arms not taken, and the one result that would reopen it. No register edit, which is the arm's whole shape: the call was whether to amend R-08-007, and the answer is that the proposal's payoff has no referent here.
  * **The two grounds that decide it.** There is no root set to make a composition-time artifact: R-08-004 deleted the capability derivation tree and the traversal went with it, so R-08-005a's bitmap **is** the mark, one architectural bit per granule written by the kernel at teardown, and R-08-007b's `creclaim` is a linear pass over granule groups rather than a reachability trace. And the mutator-overlap argument the rule exists to delete is one this design never has to make, because R-08-005 puts *freed ⇒ unreachable* at **access** time and R-08-005b makes the revoked case a defined result of the load instruction: it holds whether or not a quantum runs beside a compartment holding live capabilities. Oberon needed the quiescent point because it had no load barrier to buy instead.
  * **What the arms not taken forfeit.** Accepting would have bought a deleted proof obligation, which is the currency §17 counts, at the price of amending R-08-007, R-08-007a, R-08-007b, R-08-008, R-11-008, R-11-020, R-17-023 and the four §15 entries that argue from the sweep, plus resolving three collisions (R-15-220 fixes partition-switch cost at three terms and a boundary-bound quantum is a fourth; R-07-036 and R-08-007's own *never in another partition's time* forbid borrowing the swept domain's slot; R-11-008's standing reservation is either kept, buying nothing, or retired, changing two more entries) and authoring the §11 stack-depth and callee-graph root-set analysis the proposal assumes and no entry specifies. Deferring was the third arm and is declining by another route: the payoff is purchasable only before M4.2 writes the incremental form, so a deferral past it costs the same thing without recording why.
  * Net change: 75 lines added and 31 removed across six files, shared with the three provenance decisions this commit also closes.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named and the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits; R-08-007 is unamended, so no coverage cell, absence-contract row, or crown-jewel row moves and the review gate is not re-entered; the two documents that described the tension as open no longer do, and the critique's work-list item 11 is closed rather than left standing; the checker green on all 64 rules; and the co-read ledger unmoved, no register entry or prose span having been touched.
* [x] **M4.1a · Decide the kernel-specification provenance fork** · 1 h actual · 0.1%
  * **Authored instead.** M4.2 derives its Gallina objects from the design this repository already specifies rather than from seL4's `spec/` and `proof/` artifacts, so nothing GPL-2.0-only is translated and the kernel carries the same licence as everything else here. What seL4 supplies stays what it always supplied and carries nothing: the endpoint and non-interference *design*, which is an idea rather than an artifact.
  * **This is the one of the three where taking the term is not reversible**, which is why it resolves differently from M1.1a and M6.0a. Those two contain an instrument that never reaches the shipped image; a translated kernel specification *is* the image. Under GPL-2.0-only the kernel could not be combined with anything Apache-2.0, M1.1a's contained-toolchain arm included, and no later decision undoes a derivation once it is written.
  * What it forfeits: this cell's translation saving, and the scrutiny that rides a mechanical `hs-to-coq` carriage of an artifact many people have read. M4.2's own note that a tool-carried spec cannot admit a silent transcription error is the thing given up, and the replacement is that the design being transcribed is written down here and reviewed here.
  * The arm not taken beside it. **Narrow the roster**, taking only what seL4's BSD-2-Clause `lib/` and `tools/` halves supply and hand-authoring the rest, is the middle arm and the only one needing a per-object read before it can be sized; it is declined for buying a fraction of the saving at the cost of a per-file provenance argument on every object M4.2 touches.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and the term is read from the files' own tags rather than from the project's reputation, decomposed in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md): the split is by directory, `spec/` and `proof/` GPL-2.0-only against `lib/` and `tools/` BSD-2-Clause, and the syscall exception does not reach code derived from the kernel's specification.
  * **The second decision that can invalidate work rather than delay it, and it was found by reading tags rather than by reasoning from lineage.** The seL4 proof repository tags every file, and the tags split on directory: `spec/` and `proof/` are **GPL-2.0-only**, while `lib/` and `tools/` are BSD-2-Clause. The executable-spec objects M4.2 would take are on the GPL-2.0-only side, sampled across the Haskell executable spec, the generated design spec, the abstract spec, and the refinement proofs, all four tagging the same way.
  * **This item takes code, not lineage, and the two carry different terms.** What the design takes from seL4 is stated as two ideas and no code, which carries nothing. What M4.2 takes is artifacts, and a translation is a derivative work of its source whatever the target language, so the kernel built on it inherits the term. Apache states its own license incompatible with GPL version 2, which makes this a fork rather than an attribution chore: the kernel is either GPL-2.0-only or it is not derived from those objects. The syscall note in seL4's license does not reach the question, exempting user-level code that calls the kernel and not code derived from the kernel's specification.
  * The arms. **Take the term**: translate, and carry the kernel as GPL-2.0-only, which splits this repository's licensing and forecloses combining the kernel with anything Apache-2.0, M1.1a's contained-toolchain arm included. **Author instead**: derive the Gallina objects from the design that is already specified here rather than from seL4's artifacts, which costs this cell's translation saving and keeps one license across the platform. **Narrow the roster**: take only what the BSD-2-Clause halves supply and hand-author the rest, which is the middle arm and needs the per-object read to size.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and every source object M4.2 would touch is listed with its repository and its tag, read from the file rather than from the project's reputation.

### M6 · Userland spine

Init/supervision tree (§8) brings up the reference components; admission checker (§9) validates the package set; the package composer emits the finite typed handler/translator graph and pre-admitted media templates, and the contained object router exercises private namespaces, intents, live queries, deterministic translation caching, and protocol-bound credential handles over the existing IDL and rings.
The ring data plane is brought up in its contract order: the common ring schema and lifecycle authored in the IDL profile with the reference client/server bindings and Coq interface skeleton generated from it, then one copy-based service carrying the SPSC, notification, and capacity proofs, then one DMA service adding the extent, cancellation, teardown, and quiescence proofs, so the contract's constants and generated-proof interfaces are validated on two real services before every other server rides them. No service is grandfathered: one that cannot state its finite capacities, lifecycle semantics, cleanup bounds, and per-operation WCET is not admitted through the ring profile.

* [x] **M6.0a · Decide the control-plane compiler provenance** · 1 h actual · 0.1%
  * **Contained, and the fork is admitted on Inria's own terms.** M6.1 lowers the init system's control plane with Vélus as a build-time producer this repository never redistributes, so §4's reciprocal clause is not engaged by anything here; and where a fork of it *is* published, it is published under the same terms and conditions as the Agreement, which §4 expressly permits. SECOMP is the standing demonstration that a fork of an Inria-licensed verified compiler is a workable base as long as the licence is preserved. Both other arms stay open.
  * **The reciprocity is what makes this a different call from M1.1a rather than the same one twice**, and the difference cuts the useful way. CompCert's agreement has no counterpart to §4, and its grant is nontransferable, so containment there means the toolchain reaches nobody. Vélus §4 says what a published fork must be, which means a third party *can* obtain the control-plane compiler, on Inria's terms rather than on this repository's. That recovers part of what M1.1a's containment forfeits, for the control-plane lane only.
  * What it still forfeits: §3's limitation makes noncommercial use a property of each act of use rather than a standing characterization, and §7 terminates the grant immediately on non-compliance, so nothing built this way survives a change in what the project is for without a new grant. The arm is right for a proof-of-concept and is not a position a shipped product can hold.
  * The arms not taken. **Obtain terms** must be priced as a second negotiation even if M1.1a's are obtained: same licensor, same form of instrument, separate grant, so terms for CompCert do not extend here. **Author instead** writes the control plane directly in Gallina and forfeits Lustre's structural WCET, determinism, and causality argument, which is the whole reason §8 assigns the control plane to it; it is the cheapest arm to reach for later, because §8 already keeps a Gallina reference model of the same state machine as its host-side oracle, so that arm starts from an artifact rather than from nothing.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and `INRIA/velus`'s own licence file is read and quoted rather than inferred from CompCert's lineage in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md), which carries §2, §3, §4 and §7 verbatim.
  * **The third fork of this kind on the plan, and a decision rather than an edit.** `INRIA/velus`'s own `LICENSE` is the **Inria Non-Commercial License Agreement for the Vélus verified Lustre compiler**, and Vélus incorporates a modified CompCert as a submodule of its own. Four clauses are operative. §2 grants a "revocable, nonexclusive, nontransferable, royalty-free and worldwide license ... to use the Software solely for educational, research, or evaluation purposes", and permits Derivative Works "solely for academic, non-commercial research endeavors", defining the term to reach any work "derived from, or based upon the Software". §3 provides that "Any other use is commercial use", forbidding use "in connection with any activities which purpose is to procure a commercial gain", which makes the restriction a property of each act of use rather than of the project. §4 adds a reciprocal clause the CompCert agreement has no counterpart to: a fork must be distributed "under the same terms and conditions as in this License". §7 terminates the grant "immediately without notice" on non-compliance.
  * **The same licensor and the same form of instrument as M1.1a, under a separate grant.** The AbsInt Software Usage Agreement conveys rights in CompCert; Inria licenses Vélus on its own paper, so terms obtained there do not extend here and the two are priced as separate calls. M6.1 is the act that spends this one, so the call is made here, while the lowering is still a plan and nothing downstream rests on it.
  * The arms. **Obtain terms**: a separate Inria agreement, the only arm that keeps the verified Lustre route and the structural WCET, determinism, and causality that are the whole reason §8 assigns the control plane to it. **Contain it**: keep Vélus a build-time producer in its own repository, never redistributed, which is M1.1a's second arm in the same shape and carries the same forfeit, a toolchain with which a third party cannot rebuild the image. **Author instead**: write the supervision tree's control plane directly in Gallina, forfeiting Lustre's structural timing argument and the causality checking a synchronous compiler does for free, at a cost inside this cell's own range rather than a redesign, because §8 already keeps a Gallina reference model of the same state machine as the Wasm oracle and that model is the start-from.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and `INRIA/velus`'s own license file is read and quoted rather than inferred from CompCert's lineage.
* [ ] **M6.1 · Build the init supervision tree in Lustre via Vélus** · 15 h, range 10–20 · 1.3%
  * **Gate satisfied at M6.0a: contained.** Vélus is a build-time producer this repository never redistributes, so the Lustre route stands and the structural WCET, determinism, and causality argument comes with it. What the arm carries into this cell is that nothing built here may be conveyed except on Inria's own terms, and that the Gallina reference model §8 keeps as its host-side oracle is now doing double duty as the standing route out if the grant ever has to be given up.
  * **The form the arm takes when this item opens is a fork on the licensor's own terms, which §4 expressly provides for**, the Agreement requiring that a distributed derivative go out "under the same terms and conditions as in this License". So the route is the one `upstream/SECOMP` already demonstrates against the same licensor: fork, preserve the instrument, convey nothing from this tree. It is chosen for now rather than forever, and the reason it can be is that every arm stays open behind it, the §8 Gallina model being an artifact rather than a plan. What the arm still does not buy is a commercial position: §3 makes noncommercial use a property of each act of use and §7 terminates the grant on non-compliance without notice, so a change in what the first release is for needs a new grant rather than a new reading. That is a product decision this cell records and does not take.
