### M7 · Full emulated system

The composed stack boots in the spec's §9 order on the **fast emulator**, the plan's intermediate goal; the composed Sail emulator (§10) re-runs the same boot as the golden cross-check; the Wasm track keeps running the same components host-side for iteration.
The composed system is also the first artifact able to measure **allocation churn**, so the measurement is booked here: the static-composition low-churn argument and the static-code-overlay deferral both wait on the same measured roster, and the elective applications widen the measurement later rather than gating it.
The same roster carries the ring-parameter measurement: queue depths, batch sizes, notification cadence, and slot budgets are read off the composed system against the §12 per-operation accounting, so the contract's constants are chosen from a measured roster rather than estimated per service.

* [ ] **M7.1 · Boot the composed stack on the fast emulator and rerun it under Sail in CI** · 22.5 h, range 15–30 · 2.0%
* [ ] **M7.2 · Measure allocation churn across the composed roster** · 4.5 h, range 3–6 · 0.4%
  * The kernel-mediated teardown rate and the capability-bearing footprint swept per teardown, read off the composed roster and compared against the background slot class and quarantine pool the composition sized. R-08-008a is what reads the figure: the sweep's economics are booked there as an expectation about that rate, and this is the reading that settles it. Naming the reader adds nothing to the item, the measurement and the roster being the ones it already takes.
* [ ] **M7.3 · Measure ring parameters across the composed roster** · 4.5 h, range 3–6 · 0.4%
  * Queue depths, batch sizes, notification cadence, and slot budgets read off the composed system against the §12 per-operation accounting.

**M7 subtotal:** 31.5 h · 3% · open range 21–42 h.

### RTL track

The RTL track, in parallel from the M0 freeze:

* [ ] **R1 · Curate scalar CVA6-CHERI RTL and required platform devices** · 80 h, range 55–105 · 7.0%
  * CVA6-CHERI re-parameterized to the 64+1-bit dialect and curated per the profile and absence contract (§11: MMU deleted, static-only prediction, TSO store buffer), plus the RoT core, tag-carrying interconnect, boot ROM, UART, and block device; absence-contract state enumeration and synthesis-configuration provenance recorded at first elaboration.
  * **What stands is the reading, the measurement, and the format's authored half.** The upstreams are pinned, their licences are read at their own files, the re-parameterization delta is enumerated per site, the absence-contract evidence is recorded at a first elaboration that comes back clean, and [rtl/](../rtl/) carries the curated configuration, the capability-format package and the provenance record. What is not yet curated is the datapath itself: the caches, the store buffer, the prediction rule, the platform devices, and the bounds construction the format package leaves owed.
  * **The upstreams are pinned and each licence is read at the file at its own pin, and every one is permissive.** Six gitlinks stand under `upstream/`: the CHERI-CVA6 datapath (`lowRISC/cva6-cheri` at `36a1dc5c`), the tag controller (`Capabilities-Limited/axi_cheri_tagcontroller` at `173646d5`), the bring-up SoC (`lowRISC/mocha` at `032d6dd1`), and the three RoT references (`lowRISC/opentitan` at `bf4a2b24`, `lowRISC/ibex` at `8b8ee086`, `microsoft/cheriot-ibex` at `930feb29`). The two Solderpad readings and the five Apache readings carry no reciprocity, no field-of-use restriction and no source-disclosure condition, and the CERN Open Hardware family is on no route this plan takes, so this milestone incorporates without a decision of the kind M1.1a, M4.1a and M6.0a each had to take. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries the rows, the Solderpad election quoted from the instrument, and the two readings that do not resolve at a single file.
  * **The tag object and the commit it names are different objects, and the pin is the commit.** Cloning `lowRISC/mocha` at `v0.1.0` prints `warning: refs/tags/v0.1.0 fa8952e6 is not a commit!` and lands on `032d6dd1`, which is what `git submodule status` reports. A row transcribed from `git ls-remote --tags --refs` would name `fa8952e6` and disagree with the gitlink, which is the reading trap M1.4 records, met here on the first pin of the item.
  * **The tag ladder is re-taken at the tags, dated 2026-08-23, and the repository's own release table disagrees with it.** The `v0.1.0` annotated tag reads *"Release that supports booting CHERI Linux with tag v0.1.0. This is also known as the second minimal viable product (MVP-2)"*, tagged 2026-06-23, so §11's identification of the pin is confirmed at the object rather than inferred. `v0.1.1` is a minor release above it, tagged 2026-07-10, adding an FPGA reset improvement. What §11 does not yet say is sharper than the roadmap lag it does say: the README's release-timeline table at the pin still schedules `0.0.2` as MVP-2 for June 2026 and `0.1.0` as **RC-1** for December 2026, no `v0.0.2` tag was ever cut, and the number the table reserves for the release candidate is the number MVP-2 shipped under. What survives from that row is the date and the sign-off content, not the version it names.
  * **The re-parameterization delta is measured and stands as [its own document](rtl-reparameterization-delta.md).** It enumerates the sites per file and line across the format package, the width identity outside it, the permission and object-type and mode sites in the functional units, and the tag plane and bus user bits, stating per site what the frozen format requires and whether the site follows a parameter, carries a literal a parameter does not reach, changes shape, is rewritten against the model, is deleted, or is behaviour the imported tree does not have at all. Two findings pace the item. `CLEN = 2 * XLEN` collapses onto `XLEN`, a single declaration that 25 files of `core/` name, and it takes with it a sixteen-way store rotate into an upper half that no longer exists. And the bounds encoding is **not** a narrowing of the imported one: the frozen format has no internal-exponent flag and steals no mantissa bits, so `set_cap_reg_bounds`, `encode_bounds`, `decode_bounds`, the representable-limit comparison and the malformed predicate are written against `cap_common.sail` rather than edited from the imported source, and five behaviours the frozen definition requires have no imported counterpart at all.
  * **The absence-contract evidence is recorded at first elaboration, which is where §11 puts it.** The curated C-class configuration elaborates the CVA6-CHERI top under Verilator 5.032 with **zero errors and zero warnings**, against a baseline of the same tree at its own stock CHERI configuration. Eleven module types disappear and **none appears**: `bht`, `btb` and `ras` (A-04, A-05, A-06), `cva6_mmu`, `cva6_ptw`, `cva6_tlb` and `cva6_shared_tlb` (A-14), `pmp_entry`, `amo_buffer` (A-13), `compressed_decoder`, and `perf_counters`. Cell instances fall from 416 to 271 and declared variables from 5,862 to 5,071. **The synthesis-configuration provenance is not restated here**, R-15-103 asking that it bind a build rather than a reading: it is [the provenance record](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md), one row per claimed absence naming the parameters that remove it, held against both the absence contract and [the configuration package](../rtl/vos_c_class_config_pkg.sv) by rule K-76, and re-derived on demand by [`tools/rtl.py`](../tools/rtl.py).
  * **Four rows have no disabling parameter, and that is the honest half of the same measurement.** A-09 and A-10 have none: `cva6_icache`, the five write-through data-cache modules, and 84 `prim_ram_1p`, 18 `sram_cache` and 18 `sram` instances all survive the curated configuration, so the caches are authoring work and not a parameter. A-11's tag cache sits in the imported tag controller rather than in the core. The static-only prediction rule is not what zeroing the arrays gives: the residue is always-not-taken, where R-15-019 wants a fixed function of encoding and displacement sign. And `NrPMPEntries` at zero removes `pmp_entry` while leaving the `pmp` and `pmp_data_if` shells instantiated, so the PMP deletion is a parameter for the array and curation for the wrapper. All four are booked in [the record's own §4](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md#4-what-no-parameter-reaches-and-what-that-costs) rather than counted twice.
  * **Curated RTL has a home and it holds only what this repository wrote.** [rtl/](../rtl/) carries the authored half and the imported cores stay behind their gitlinks, so nothing binds this repository that a pin already covers: the configuration package, the capability-format package, and the provenance record are there, and no imported file is copied in. It takes the repository's default line endings rather than `model/`'s verbatim rule, there being no upstream here to stay byte-identical to. The lane that drives it is [`tools/rtl.py`](../tools/rtl.py), and Verilator is pinned at 5.032 with its own [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) row.
  * **One scope tension is reported rather than resolved.** The tag-carrying interconnect this cell names is a net-new block R-15-092 requires **authored** in Kôika or Kami and proven, and §11's artifact of record is generated SystemVerilog where authored. But §11 also says of that route that nothing is owed before it opens, *well after the co-simulation gate*. Taking the fabric inside R1 opens route (a) before that gate. The library election is already recorded and nothing is blocked by it, but Coq and Kami authoring is a different kind of work from curation and is not inside the estimate below.

  * These are **qualification gates, not re-open conditions**: a macro that misses one is a part that does not qualify, not an architecture that reverts.

  * The RTL is net-new and the design under it is not, which §11 states and this cell does not restate: what the CHERI-RVV line settles is the per-element question and not any of the hardware, so the estimate stands whole. Where it reaches this item is the amortized check, which M0.8b's per-element discard and R-15-085a's two vector-memory rows both price as though the question were closed, and which is the boundary a magnitude would come from if R-17-041's ever acquire one.

  * Close the simultaneous-activation set against measured IR-drop, thermal-coupling, and power-signature limits at composition capacity and at the frozen bank granularity, then feed the admitted set back into the §11 mode-transition budget and R-15-247g.
  * It lands after the gate because it wants measured silicon behaviour that M0.17's exploration and R5's macro evidence only bound; the item is named in the tool's after-gate list beside R4, R5, M9, M10, and the post-M10 obligations so the gate figure does not quietly absorb it.

Everything past M10, the CHERI-CompCert **secure-compilation criterion** (robust preservation; the *functional* backend already landed in M1), the **R-05-023a validation instrument** (the decompilation-into-logic producer of the §5 post-CompCert records, whose first non-fast-path artifact also opens the checker-binary question booked in [architectural-alternatives.md](architectural-alternatives.md)), the binary-level constant-time verifier, the masking obligations (the *d*-probing and composition theorems on the crypto artifacts with the Coco-class netlist discharge, R-05-004a, R-15-053a, §4), the R-16-008f detection theorem, the certifying-Rust *certificate* mode, the full VST refinement proofs, WCET, and RTL ⊑ Sail refinement, is the hardening program of §5/§6/§18, each piece replacing a golden-model component *in place* and checked against the reference this plan produces.
One statement is owed at that program's opening rather than inside it, and its five positions stand ahead of it rather than waiting on it: the RTL ⊑ Sail arrow is the least-built layer of the stack (R-17-039) and the hedge deletions spend it (R-17-037), so what each of the PMP-backstop, IOMMU, MTE, shadow-stack, and initialization-plane rejections becomes under a late or partial close is stated per rejection and read per block (R-17-039a). What the opening owes is therefore the *instantiation* and not the writing: which blocks landed on which rung, what the FEV evidence covers on each, and which imported cores still stand at evidence tier when the program begins. Partial coverage is what the three-route ladder above produces by construction, so that reading is the ordinary one rather than a contingency the program hopes to avoid.
Two more are owed at the same opening: the combined-adversary route is already selected (R-17-058d, the ineffective-fault countermeasure carried into the masked datapath, its combined claim a reduction theorem spending the two stated axioms), so what the opening states is that theorem's verification plan beside the masking obligations rather than a choice, the four items below carrying what that theorem and the masking obligations beside it cost; and the R-15-053a bring-up characterization, its stated limits and per-structure micro-benchmarks, is rehearsed on the FPGA and executed at first silicon, the rehearsal validating the harness and never the axiom, an FPGA's leakage being the wrong silicon.

**The opening's statement and the work it states are priced separately, and the four items after it are the work.** R-05-004a's masked datapath and R-17-058d's reduction carry the hardening program's only first-of-kind formalization, and the three Coq objects §4 names do not cost alike: their nearest start-froms are a released machine-checked development in another prover, a body of proofs in another prover's logic, and nothing machine-checked anywhere, which is three prices rather than one. The composition theorem therefore takes two cells, its two halves being exactly the pair a single figure would blend. What sits in a cell here is the Coq object alone; the tool ensemble beside it produces evidence and enters no trust base (§4), and the rest of the hardening program stays prose, so the grand total covers the golden model, the RTL track and these four rather than the program.

* [ ] **Post-M10 · Publish opening hardening obligations** · 6 h, range 4–8 · 0.5%
  * Instantiate R-17-039a's five fallback positions against the route coverage the program actually has, and state the reduction-theorem verification plan, the masking obligations, and the first-silicon characterization plan.
  * The first of those four is re-priced rather than dropped: the positions are written and the ladder makes them per-block, so what is left is reading each against the rungs admitted for each core, which is a shorter act than the writing was.
  * **The masking half of that plan has a machine-checked source to transcribe for one of its three theorems, and the source is in the wrong prover.** A 2026 line of work carries **PINI-class composition theorems for arithmetic masking over prime fields** in **Lean 4**, with the composed pipeline cases, the renewal argument for fresh masking between stages, the k-stage generalization, and the Barrett-reduction bounds beneath them, stated with no admitted goals and instantiated against a published post-quantum accelerator to diagnose a real flaw in its masking. That is precisely the PINI-class composition theorem §4 books as original formalization, and transcribing a machine-checked development is a different act from transcribing a paper.
  * Four caveats decide how much of that is transcribable, and the first two are structural. It is **Lean 4 and not Rocq**, so it is a source and never an import: the load-bearing artifact is still the Coq statement, and §4's claim that no proof-assistant masking development exists *in Coq* is untouched. It covers **arithmetic** masking over prime fields, which is the ML-KEM and ML-DSA half, and not the Boolean half the AES and Keccak datapaths need, where the EasyCrypt gadget proofs remain the nearest source; so the two halves now have machine-checked sources in two different provers and neither is this project's. It touches **R-17-058d's ineffective-fault reduction theorem not at all**, so the combined-adversary half of the opening statement is unchanged and still original. And what decides how far it is relied on is a dated reading under R-18-001a rather than an impression: read 2026-08-23, the development is released as MIT-licensed repositories with archival deposits, sorry-free and carrying no non-standard axiom, pinned to Lean 4 v4.30.0-rc1 against one Mathlib revision and reproducible by a script each repository ships, while carrying no independent replication and stating its own scope as first-order, one probe per wire. So its theorem statements and proof architecture are worth transcribing and its proofs are still not something this repository stands on, the posture §4 already takes toward the untrusted evidence-producing ensemble beside it.

That ambient is the item's one import and everything over it is authored: the circuit and leakage representation, the probe expansion the two extensions define, the adversary, and the sanity lemmas that make the statement usable rather than merely well-formed.

No proof assistant carries the glitch and transition extensions, and none ties either to the netlist the R-15-053a discharge names.

This is the crown jewel whose wrongness no theorem beneath it catches, so the item is priced as specification authoring under R-05-150 review and not as proof engineering.
* [ ] **Post-M10 · Transcribe the arithmetic composition half** · 24 h, range 14–34 · 2.1%
  * PF-PINI over ℤ_q, the renewal argument that a fresh mask between pipeline stages erases the earlier stage's multiplicity, its k-stage generalization, and the algebraic-to-hardware Barrett bridge, re-proved in Coq against the Lean development's statements.
  * **The source is an artifact rather than a paper, which is what this cell rests on**: eighteen kernel-verified theorems over five files in the composition repository, MIT-licensed with an archival deposit beside it, so its terms permit whatever a transcription could carry, no provenance fork of the M1.1a kind arises, and nothing is vendored (R-18-001a, read 2026-08-23).
  * **Two gaps decide the cell and both are the source's own.** It is first-order by its stated scope, one probe per wire, where R-05-004a's obligation is *d*-th order, so the order generalization is authored rather than transcribed. And Lean 4 with Mathlib is not Coq with mathcomp: statements and proof architecture cross, tactic proofs and the algebraic hierarchy beneath them do not, so what this item buys is a reachable target and a proof plan rather than a port. It is the cheapest of the four for exactly that reason, and a transcription costed as though it were an original formalization, or an original formalization costed as though it were a transcription, is the error this split exists to prevent.
* [ ] **Post-M10 · Author the Boolean composition half** · 40 h, range 22–58 · 3.5%
  * PINI at arbitrary order over GF(2) on the NI and SNI ladder beneath it, glitch-robust, for the HPC and DOM gadget families the datapath carries, instantiated on the AES S-box and the Keccak χ the R-05-004a secret path runs through.
  * **The nearest source is EasyCrypt's Boolean masking proofs, and it is a source in the weakest of the three senses** (R-18-001a, read 2026-08-23). EasyCrypt is its own logic and its own checker rather than a Coq library, its masking line is NI and SNI over GF(2^n) and predates PINI, and the 2026 movement in the area is in checkers rather than in provers. One 2026 result is pinned for its technique and not its artifact, probing security routed through conditional independence in a separation logic for exactly that, and it is stated on paper with no mechanization.
  * **This is the largest of the four.** It is the half the AES and Keccak datapaths cannot do without, it is original in the prover and near-original in content at arbitrary order, and the transcription beside it buys it nothing: a prime field's uniformity arguments do not carry to GF(2), which is why the prime-field case was the gap and the Boolean one was not.

  * The selected route's combined claim, machine-checked on the artifact: every fault the single-fault-per-activation hypothesis admits on the masked datapath's activation is either effective, landing in the R-16-008f detection statement, or confined to one share's computation, landing inside the R-15-053a probing bound at unreduced order.
  * **Nothing machine-checked exists for it in any prover, and that is the reading rather than a gap in it** (R-18-001a, read 2026-08-23). Every verification vehicle in the class is a standalone checker: VERICA and FIVER, and the general random-fault and random-probing model beside them, whose verification is delivered as extensions of VERICA and IronMask. The constructions carrying their own combined model are CAPA, M&M and the CINI-class gadgets R-17-058d declines as the pin. The nearest mechanization is on the fault side alone and one level above where this design states it, the every-fault-detected-or-ineffective shape carried in Coq at CompCert's RTL level, which is a template for the statement and supplies none of the combined case.
  * **The range is the widest of the four and the widening is the pricing**, this plan's convention being to price a softer class by widening its own items rather than by a second figure over the total. What narrows it is that the theorem is small where the others are large, a case split over two axioms already stated rather than a new model. What does not narrow is that it stands over two objects this plan does not cost, the R-16-008f detection statement and the countermeasure's own construction, so what is priced here is the reduction and never its premises.

**M8–M10 subtotal:** 224.5 h · 20% · open range 134–315 h.

## Build-loop instruments

These items sit outside the milestone subtotals but inside the grand total, and each carries its own estimate cell like any other item. Every change must be benchmarked before adoption; canonical `-O2` RelWithDebInfo remains the exit criterion.

* [x] **Initial check/emit/FAST tooling** · 0.9 h actual · 0.1%
* [x] **I0 · Standardize the tools on one language** · 6 h actual · 0.5%
  * Every tool in [tools/](../tools/) is Python 3.14, the one interpreter both lanes carry: the host has no `bash` and the WSL guest no `pwsh`, so three languages could not be reduced any other way. The shell prelude's two load-bearing tricks survive the move and improve: the OCaml stack the Sail emission needs is `resource.setrlimit` in the parent rather than `ulimit -s`, and a stage's cost is `os.wait4` on the child that was asked about rather than `/usr/bin/time` reporting the running maximum over every child so far.
  * Shared parses move into `tools/vos/` and the checker's rules into one module per group, so no fact is parsed twice; the entry points collapse from sixteen to five, the four model loops and the trace rig becoming subcommands of [tools/model.py](../tools/model.py). [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) states the lane rule, the conventions, and the three edits a new rule costs.
  * Exit evidence: the checker's output is unchanged line for line except where it now names the package rather than a file, and it runs in 0.95 s against 2.2 s; the mutation selftest kills all 50 mutants in 19 s against 129 s; `model.py build` is green at 150/150 with the same `ALL_DONE` marker; `sweep` returns the same 146/52/1 of 199; `trace-diff --corpus` returns the same 54 prefixes at 97 in 2.2 s against 7.1 s; and the proof gate returns the same 19 constants closed.
* [ ] **I1 · Move sources to WSL ext4 and uncap local parallelism** · 2 h, range 1–3 · 0.2%
  * The item's four clauses do not resolve alike and two of them are closed. `ctest -j$(nproc)` is closed as done, the build setting `test_jobs=cpus`. Moving the checkout to `~/src/VerifiedOS` is closed as refused, on the measurement below rather than on the rejection that measurement overturned. The two left standing are Remote-WSL and cache-hostile WSL memory reclamation, and they share one shape: each is a setting on the human's box rather than in this tree, the second reachable only through `[wsl2] autoMemoryReclaim` in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`, which is global to every distribution, permanent until a human deletes it, and outside anything this repository should own, the same boundary [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) draws around the idle timer that sits beside it. The item is therefore open on a posture call and not on a measurement, and no benchmark closes it.
  * **Half of it has landed, the rejection under the other half is overturned, and the move that overturning clears the way for is measured and does not pay.** `ctest -j$(nproc)` is `test_jobs=cpus` today, so the parallelism clause is closed. The `/mnt/c` rejection [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) carried measured *reading* 1.19 MB of Sail sources across 125 files, where the stage that pays is `configure`, which walks the whole 824-file cmake project and pays per stat rather than per byte. Measured on a quiet toolchain with the arms alternated, a re-configure is **17.0, 17.4 and 20.4 s at 10% to 12% of a core** from `/mnt/c` against **0.9, 1.0 and 1.1 s at 47% to 48%** from a byte-identical ext4 copy, and a fresh configure with the downloads already in the tree is **26.7, 29.5 and 34.9 s** against **11.3, 7.3 and 7.6 s**. CPU time is 1.7 to 2.4 s against 0.4 to 0.5 s, so the gap is wait rather than work, and the arms do not overlap at any repeat. The suspect is real and worth about 17 s of every build.
  * **What the same measurement then removes is the move.** The tax is symmetric and the host lane pays it in reverse: `tools/check.py` costs **1.0, 1.1 and 1.6 s** over the NTFS checkout against **11.4, 13.5 and 18.5 s** over the same tree on the `wsl.localhost` share, and that is the loop run after every document edit rather than once per build. Host git refuses such a checkout outright until `safe.directory` is relaxed, reporting `dubious ownership` and that the `.git` file `may refer to a non-local directory`. The item therefore stays open on a half that is not a measurement: what would pay is moving the *working posture* into the guest, which is what this entry's Remote-WSL clause has always said and which is a workflow call rather than a benchmark. The premise is settled and the naked move is refused; relocating the checkout alone would buy 17 s of a build and spend more than that on every checker run.
* [x] **I2 · Use one shared content-keyed SMT memo cache** · 0.9 h actual · 0.1%
  * **Declined, and on the pinned compiler's cache format rather than on a benchmark.** Sail's memo cache is one flat file of fixed records, a 16-byte digest of the SMT query and one byte of verdict, read whole into a map at startup by `load_digests` and rewritten whole from that map at exit by `save_digests` (libsail 0.20.2, `constraint.ml`). There is no lock, no atomic rename, and `open_out_bin` truncates in place. A build tree's cache measures 1,418,412 bytes, which is 83,436 records with no remainder, over verdicts 6,612 unknown, 31,725 sat and 45,099 unsat. Benchmarking is the wrong instrument here: what a shared path does is not slower, it is unsound, and a measurement that failed to trigger it would say nothing.
  * Three consequences, each alone enough. Concurrent runs do not **merge**: each writes back what it loaded plus what it learned, so the later writer's file lacks everything the earlier learned during the overlap, which is worst exactly when the cache is cold and the learning is largest, that is, in the case sharing was proposed to fix. A reader arriving mid-rewrite sees a prefix, or sees one byte outside the verdict range and takes Sail's invalid path, which empties both maps and then **replaces the shared file with only that run's own entries**: measured deterministically, one such byte destroyed **77,260 of 83,436 records**, and Sail exited 0 having printed `SMT cache file 'sail_smt_cache' is invalid` into a build log nothing reads for it. And two writers interleave at whatever offset each buffer flushed; records are 17 bytes and OCaml flushes at 65,536, which is 1 modulo 17, so the sixteenth boundary falls exactly on a verdict byte and leaves one run's genuine digest carrying another run's answer to a different obligation. That last is a false answer to a typechecking obligation in a build whose output is a verification oracle, which is the trade I4 refuses on `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS` and is refused here on the same ground.
  * **What was done instead is copy-on-create, which buys the same cold start with one writer per file.** A lane standing up copies a warm cache from the primary worktree's tree rather than pointing at it, so the ~25 min a cold cache costs is paid once on this machine and not per lane, and a stale copy costs misses and nothing worse. That landed with I7, and the standing boundary is unchanged: content-keying is by obligation and not by prover, so no copy crosses a solver version or a distribution.
  * Finding, closed by I7 rather than by this item: the shared cache I2 proposed already existed for the typecheck loop, `/root/build/verifiedos-typecheck-smt-cache` being one file every checkout wrote, and it was live across four concurrent lanes. It is per lane now.
* [x] **I3 · Benchmark a current z3 binary on a cold emission** · 0.8 h actual · 0.1%
  * **The cold emission separates the solvers where the typecheck could not, and it separates them in the pin's favor.** Two alternated repeats per arm in a disposable lane, cold each time by deleting the memo cache and dirtying the emission edge: the pinned z3 **5.1.0** emits cold in 216.3 and 212.9 s against the distribution **4.13.3**'s 274.7 and 286.2, about 214.6 against 280.5 at the midpoints, **+31% for the distribution's solver**, every distro arm auditable by the tool's own WARNING line in its captured log. The open cell's reading, that the workload does not separate them and the pin stands on reproducibility rather than speed, was true of the typecheck it measured (cold 46.9/59.3 s pin against 44.5/48.2 distro, warm 5.5/5.8 against 5.7/8.0, spreads wider than gaps) and is false of the emission: the pin now stands on both grounds.
  * **The ~25 min cold figure the tools quoted was roughly 7× stale, and the staleness is the curation's.** A cold emission today is ~3.6 min pinned and ~4.7 distro against a warm 36 to 39 s in the same lane, so the once-measured figure described a model whose surface the batches since deleted. [tools/model.py](../tools/model.py)'s seeding rationale now carries the measured pair; the seeding keeps paying about three minutes per lane standup, just no longer catastrophically.
  * Three observations ride the numbers. **The two solvers do measurably different work for byte-identical output**: 4.13.3 writes 23,306 memo records where 5.1.0 writes 17,558 and peaks ~117 MB higher, while the emitted C++'s md5 is unchanged across all four cold runs and the warm anchors, so the difference is solver-side effort and never artifact. **Per-arm determinism is exact**, both repeats of each arm writing byte-identical cache sizes. And **one emit regenerates about a fifth of the seeded cache**, 17,558 records against the seeded 84,849, so the cache a lane copies accumulates more than the emission's own obligations and a lane is returned to warm by a restore, never by a re-run.
  * Exit evidence: quiet-box gate empty; four cold runs and three warm anchors all exit 0, logs under /root/logs; the seeded cache saved before the first deletion and restored byte-exactly at 1,442,433 bytes, a 35.7 s warm emit confirming the lane; no cache crossed a solver boundary.
* [x] **I4 · Add shared ccache, then emit only when generated output changes** · 1.1 h actual · 0.1%
  * The ccache half is the half that is a choice rather than a measurement: the binary is installed and [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) puts the two compiler launchers on every configure when it finds one, against a `CCACHE_DIR` shared by every build tree, with no `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS` set on purpose, a false hit in a build whose output is a verification oracle not being worth the hit rate.
  * **The second clause is declined, because measuring it removed its premise.** The clause was that a regeneration producing byte-identical C++ still pays for the 150 s single-threaded compile the critical path ends in. It does not, and the ccache half is why: a `touch` on one model source, with no byte of the model changed, re-emits C++ whose md5 is the one it replaced, and the compile and link that follow cost **4.5 s over 23 edges**, the 10.5 MB unit among them a direct hit. Ninja's floor with nothing touched at all is 2.9 s of that 4.5 s, so what emitting-only-on-change would actually save is **1.6 s** against a 41.2 s emission, under 4% of the cycle, and only in the minority case where a model edit leaves the generated C++ identical. Against that stands a permanent divergence in a vendored upstream `CMakeLists.txt` that every future reconciliation of the pin would have to carry. The emission's own 41.2 s is not addressable this way at all: what the output will be is not knowable without producing it.
  * **Finding: the shared `CCACHE_DIR` does not reach across build trees, and the claim that it did was wrong.** ccache hashes the command line along with the source, so the fast tree, which differs from the canonical one by `-g`, and a second worktree, which differs by every `-I`, share no entry with it. At scale: a lane's first 367-unit build against a cache already holding 939 entries took 367 misses and no hits at all. `CCACHE_BASEDIR` is the lever that would make lanes share and it is deliberately not pulled, because the object a second lane would get back carries the first lane's directory in its debug info, which is a wrong artifact by the same standard that keeps `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS` unset. [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) now records what the cache does and does not reach, in place of the claim it carried.
  * Exit evidence, taken in one lane of I7's layout: emission after a source `touch` 41.2 s, generated C++ byte-identical at 10,539,981 bytes over 336,909 lines, follow-on compile and link 4.5 s against a 2.9 s null-rebuild floor, 18 ccache direct hits across the two commands. The generated unit compiled alone at `-O2 -g` is 139.1 s wall and 115 s CPU with a 1.02 GB peak, and that wall figure is an upper bound rather than a clean one: a sibling lane was building and the compile held 83% of a core.
* [x] **I5 · Benchmark generated-TU flags, clang versus gcc, and mold** · 0.9 h actual · 0.1%
  * Measured in a disposable worktree lane on a quiet toolchain: eighteen compiles of the generated unit in round-robin order so drift spreads across the variants rather than down the list, two or three repeats each, medians reported, and repeats drifted mildly downward, so no thermal penalty hides in the tail. The sweep ran with ccache disabled into a scratch directory, so no lane cache was invalidated and the cold-cache pricing caveat the open cell carried never applied. The unit has grown to 10,835,851 bytes over 344,963 lines since the freeze figures, and the clean baseline is **81.8 s wall at a 1.20 GB peak** for gcc 15.2.0 at `-O2 -g`: the 139.1 s on record was the upper bound it was booked as, roughly 40% of it the sibling lane rather than the compiler.
  * **clang 21.1.8 compiles the same unit at the same flags in 38.7 s, 2.1× faster than gcc, accepting the extracted command verbatim** (two `-Wparentheses-equality` warnings against generated comparisons, nothing dropped); with `-g` off the pair is 21.3 s against 61.3 s. The flip is not taken inside a benchmark cell: the canonical emulator is the executable reference and the iterate tree's value is fidelity to it, so a compiler change is its own small act carrying its own build, ctest, and corpus evidence, and what this item hands that act is the 43 s it buys on the largest term of a real-edit build.
  * The `-g` split is located rather than adopted: dropping `-g` saves 20.5 s on gcc and 17.4 s on clang, `-g1` recovers most of that at 63.4 s while keeping line tables (the object 10.5 MB against 28.9), and `-fno-var-tracking-assignments` locates about 14 s of gcc's `-g` cost in var-tracking. Canonical `-O2 -g` RelWithDebInfo stays the exit criterion and the fast tree already drops `-g`, so no tree changes flags on this measurement.
  * **`-ffile-prefix-map` is compile-time-free and its premise is removed, the third closure of that shape** (I2, I4's second clause, I6 and I8 are the others): 81.5 s against 81.8 is noise, but the cross-lane ccache hit it was in scope to enable does not materialize even with `CCACHE_BASEDIR` set per lane, because ccache strips the map's values from its hash anyway and the defeating input is the checkout's `-I` paths under /mnt/c, which no single `CCACHE_BASEDIR` covers at the same time as the /root/build tree paths. Diagnosed from ccache's own debug-mode hash input, where every differing line is a worktree path; the two lanes' units were md5-identical, so the miss is the layout's and not the content's.
  * **mold is declined on its own measurement**: the simulator link is 0.70 s under GNU ld, 0.24 s under mold and 0.27 s under lld, and a 3× win on 0.7 s is half a second against a warm build stage whose floor is 2.9 s. mold 2.40.4 and lld 21.1.8 stay installed and idle.
  * Exit evidence: quiet-box gate empty before the window; 18 of 18 compiles and 9 of 9 links exit 0, logs under /root/logs; per-variant medians as above; [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py)'s recorded baseline updated from the contended figure to the clean pair.
* [x] **I6 · Benchmark the same Sail 0.20.2 binary built with flambda** · 0.7 h actual · 0.1%
  * **Declined on its own measurement, which came in far under even the re-ranked forecast.** A flambda-built Sail 0.20.2 (OCaml 5.4.0 on both sides, `flambda: true` the only differing axis) emits the model's C++ in a median **33.8 s against the stock binary's 34.4**, three alternated repeats per arm on a quiet toolchain with every run inside ±0.7 s of its arm's median: 0.6 s, about 1.7%, where the re-ranked estimate had been roughly 12 s. Warm typecheck is 11.96 s against 12.00, indistinguishable. The emitted C++ is byte-identical by md5 across the arms, both hold 98% of a core, and the flambda binary is a third larger on disk (36.5 MB against 27.4). A second Sail switch carried through every future pin bump buys half a second of a 34 s stage, so the switch is deleted rather than kept, and what the item leaves behind is that the emission's cost lives in Sail's own evaluation, not somewhere a compiler mode of the compiler that built it can reach.
  * Two facts the A/B surfaced outlive it, both about the harness rather than flambda. **The emission bakes the sail binary's absolute path at configure time**, eight occurrences in `build.ninja`, so an arm is a configured tree and not an environment: the flambda arm ran in a second tree under the same lane, its memo cache pre-seeded by copy exactly as `_seed_tree` would have, its first emission doubling as the proof the seed took (34.7 s, not the ~25 min cold case). And **the no-edit route to a second compiler's Sail is a second opam root whose switch is literally named `default`**, because [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) applies `opam env --switch=default` and honors `OPAMROOT`; recorded here because a future Sail-version experiment takes the same route.
  * Exit evidence: quiet-box gate empty; six timed emissions and seven typechecks all exit 0, logs under /root/logs; both arms' `sail --version` identical at 0.20.2; the flambda root and its second tree removed with the benchmark's lane rather than carried.
* [x] **I7 · Run canonical builds in the background and standardize worktree lanes** · 1.4 h actual · 0.1%
  * One WSL toolchain serves as many checkouts as there are git worktrees, and they shared one build tree between them. Each checkout now carries a **lane**, derived from the checkout rather than declared: a linked worktree's `.git` is a file naming its administrative directory under the primary checkout's `.git/worktrees/`, so the lane is git's own name for the worktree and is unique within the repository by construction. The primary worktree has no lane and keeps every path it had; a linked one builds under `/root/build/lane-<name>/`, one directory holding that lane's build tree, its iterate tree, and its SMT cache, so a lane is retired by deleting it. The M0.4 oracle's tree is the one every lane still shares, because it is stock upstream at a pinned commit and no curation here reaches it.
  * **Three things collided and only one of them was loud.** Measured while four lanes ran against this toolchain: cmake refuses outright to aim an existing cache at a second source tree, so `/root/logs/model-build.log` carries `CONFIGURE_EXIT=1` and `The source ".../VerifiedOS-m08c/model/CMakeLists.txt" does not match the source ".../VerifiedOS/model/CMakeLists.txt"`; that refused run had already opened the shared log with `w`, truncating the primary lane's from 10,667 bytes to 444, so a build that never started destroyed the record of the last green one. The third is silent and is the one lanes exist for: `sweep`, `corpus`, `trace-diff`, `devicetree` and `reference` all read the simulator back out of the build tree and none of them can tell whose model generated it, so a lane building after another lane reports on the other lane's machine.
  * A build **holds its lane** for as long as it runs, and a second one over a live one is refused by name rather than merged into it. The lock is `flock` and not a pidfile, so a killed build leaves nothing to break and there is no liveness test to get wrong; `build --background` hands the lock to the run it detaches by inheritance rather than dropping it, and `wait` blocks on that lock in the kernel before reading the log for the verdict. Waiting on the lock rather than on `ALL_DONE` is what ends a wait on a build that died without writing one.
  * **What a lane costs is a copy and not a cold build**, which is what makes one worth having and is why [I2](#build-loop-instruments) declines to share instead. A lane standing up copies the warm SMT memo cache and the downloaded riscv-tests from the primary worktree's tree, so the ~25 min a cold cache costs is paid once on this machine rather than once per lane: measured, a lane from nothing is green in 237.7 s. A build tree is 362 MB, so four lanes are about 1.5 GB against 947 GB free, and disk is not the constraint. What a lane genuinely does pay is the compiles, because no ccache entry crosses a worktree: that is I4's measured finding, 367 misses and no hits on a first build.
  * **Defect found by standardizing the lane, and fixed here: `git describe` cannot run in one at all.** A linked worktree made by the host's git writes a *Windows* path into its `.git` file, and inside the guest that path is not absolute, so git appends it to the worktree and looks for `/mnt/c/.../VerifiedOS-inst/C:/Users/.../worktrees/VerifiedOS-inst` and exits 128 with `not a git repository`. cmake's `git describe` is one of those runs, so every lane configured with `Failed to run git describe: 128` and stamped its emulator `unknown commit`, silently restoring the state M0.10 exists to end. `model.py reference` is the gate that catches it and in a lane it caught it, exit 1. The configure now carries a `GIT_DIR` translated by `wslpath`, scoped to that one child so it cannot follow a later `git` into the oracle's tree and answer for this repository there; the lane's emulator reports `636aa07-dirty` and `reference` exits 0.
  * Finding, reported rather than closed: the sibling lanes had already worked the collision around by hand, `/root/build/verifiedos-model-m08c` being a second tree named for one worktree. That workaround is per-agent and undeclared, and it reaches neither the log nor the typecheck cache, which every lane still shared as one file; the derivation reaches all three.
  * Exit evidence: `model.py lane` on the primary checkout prints the four paths it always used, unchanged. A lane standing up from nothing is green at 375 of 375 edges in 237.7 s, configure 29.6 s, build 172.9 s at 715% CPU, ctest 35.2 s, seeded from the primary tree rather than cold. A second `model.py build` against a live one exits 1 naming the holder's pid and writes nothing. `wait` returns the finished log's verdict at exit 0, and against a stand-in holder it returned 6.0 s after that holder exited, with no interval anywhere in it. In the lane, `corpus` is 21 of 21 with 193 checks over 5,231 records, `devicetree` compiles with no warning at 4,466 bytes inside its region, and `reference` names the model at `636aa07-dirty` over 62 properties. Every later figure in this section was taken in that lane, and the ones marked contended were taken with a sibling lane building.
* [x] **I8 · Optionally move quick checks to push CI and canonical tests to nightly CI** · 1 h actual · 0.1%
  * **Declined, and the measurement removes the premise by finding no edit loop to take either half off.** Warm on a quiet host, five arms round-robined so drift spreads across them rather than down the list, three repeats each after a discarded warmup, medians: `check.py` **0.92 s**, `typecheck.py` **0.64 s**, `test.py` **8.87 s**, `check-selftest.py` **19.93 s**, `gate.py` **19.78 s**. The whole pre-land host surface is the wave plus the tools' own suite, **28.7 s**, and the loop actually run after a document edit is the 0.92 s one. A push-triggered runner subtracts none of that, because its verdict arrives after the push while this gate is what decides whether the change lands at all; what it adds is a second opinion on a third machine, at the two costs below.
  * **"CI" is already spoken for in this plan, and what it names there is the gate's predicates and their instrument rather than a trigger or a host, so the two subjects do not meet.** Four places could conflict and none does: the open M7.1 reruns the composed boot under Sail as the golden cross-check, the §10 rig promises one CI runner diffing one trace format across three executors, [the measurement contract](freeze-measurement-contract.md) §9 states twelve rejection predicates `G-1` to `G-12` over a freeze report, and [the differential corpus](differential-corpus.md) §8 is the one that resolves *where*, answering `tools/check.py` on the host and `tools/model.py corpus` in WSL. So the runner §10 promises is built and is the model tool's own rig, and not one of the four fixes a trigger, a schedule, or a machine. This item's subject is exactly that residue, which is why declining it leaves all four standing as they are.
  * **Push CI would spend the pin discipline, and two rules price it.** K-67 holds the ty and ruff pins at every site [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) states them, against the `TY_VERSION` and `RUFF_VERSION` [typecheck.py](../tools/typecheck.py) fixes; K-75 holds the interpreter floor at every site under [tools/](../tools/) that states it, against the version [ty.toml](../tools/ty.toml) fixes, the two settings files spelling one figure in two dialects and the README restating it five times. Both windows stop at this directory. A workflow installing the two checkers restates both pins and the floor outside both of them, and names a runner image beside those that no rule owns anywhere. Each of those is a derived fact nothing owns, which is the thing the working rules refuse outright, and owning them is a rule apiece, three edits and a mutant each, against an item priced at 1.5 h.
  * **The nightly half cannot ride a stock hosted runner, and the way it fails is silent rather than loud.** This lane is two opam switches, `default` at ocaml-system 5.4.0 carrying Sail 0.20.2 and `rocq-9.1.1` at ocaml-base-compiler 5.4.0 carrying Rocq 9.1.1 spelled `rocq c`, over a z3 **5.1.0** unpacked beside the build trees and prepended to PATH ahead of Ubuntu 26.04's own 4.13.3, on Python 3.14.4; no stock image carries one of the three. [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py)'s `_prepend_z3_path` warns and continues when that prefix is absent rather than stopping, so a runner without it typechecks green against the distribution's solver, and a nightly gate reporting about a machine other than the one it names is worse than no nightly gate. The configuration that would make a nightly canonical build affordable is a restored SMT memo cache, and that is [I2](#build-loop-instruments)'s measurement rather than a fresh question: libsail 0.20.2 rewrites the cache whole at exit with no lock and no atomic rename, concurrent runs do not merge, and content-keying is by obligation and not by prover, so no copy crosses a solver version or a distribution, which is precisely what a restore onto a hosted image is.
  * **Finding: this tree already carries CI workflow files, and their being inert is what keeps them green.** `model/.github/` holds a composite action, a `dependabot.yml`, and six workflows, vendored byte-identically with the model tree; GitHub reads workflows from the repository root's `.github/` alone and there is none here, so not one of them has ever run. That is load-bearing rather than incidental: `ci.yml`'s ninja line names `generated_smt_rv32d`, and `model/model/CMakeLists.txt` reduced the prover-backend loop to the single RV64 arm because RV32 is not a configuration of this model, so the target does not exist; and the action all of them share pins Sail 0.20.2, this tree's own pin, while installing neither the pinned z3 nor Rocq at all. Making one live is a permanent divergence in a vendored path that every reconciliation of the pin then carries, which is the cost [I4](#build-loop-instruments)'s second clause refuses on the vendored `CMakeLists.txt` and which is refused here on the same ground.
  * Exit evidence: five arms at three repeats each all exit 0, on a box sampled at 4.5% to 15% before the window, with one selftest repeat at 31.75 s against its arm's 19.09 and 19.93 kept in the median rather than dropped; `origin` is reachable and its `refs/heads/main` is this work's base, so the decline is on the cost and not on an absent remote; no workflow file is added, and `model/.github/` stands byte-identical to its pin.
* [x] **I9 · Hold the co-stated facts and derive the reading view** · 4.1 h actual · 0.4%
  * The one drift K-61 cannot see by construction, a fact stated in two of its pairs moving in one while the other stands green, was surveyed across both documents against the whole holder inventory and closed where it was found: rule K-68 pins the surveyed facts at every site that states them, nine new K-24 quantities read each restated count from the artifact that owns it so `--fix` rewrites a grown list's own restatements, K-69 holds the figures one entry fixes with the repair always running from the owner, and K-60 answers every free-prose VLEN token against the composed geometries.
  * Where position was the defect the bookmarks moved rather than the machinery: the clock-discipline block leaves the interrupt entry's pair for the entries that state it, nine further `-2` bookmarks fold restating lines into their own entries' pairs, four prose restatements become citations per the register's own cite-don't-restate discipline, and the truncated tag-plane share is corrected to the value its arithmetic gives and pinned as a K-54 site.
  * The reading loop is priced down rather than restructured: `co-read.py --show --all` prints every pending pair in one read and `--where` opens both sides at file:line, and `tools/read-view.py` derives the join the two documents never gave a reader, the prose with every entry rendered beneath the bookmark that cites it, written into ignored `out/` and never a source. [spec.md](spec.md) now opens with the precedence banner every other document in the constellation already carried.
  * Exit evidence: check.py green at 69 rules with K-68 holding 18 co-stated facts at 45 sites; the selftest kills every mutant with the K-69 repair lane proving pristine-fixpoint both ways; 31 co-read pairs re-read and blessed the same day their spans moved; the view weaves all entries with none left homeless; test.py and typecheck.py green throughout.
* [x] **I10 · Flip the model build's compiler to clang** · 0.5 h actual · 0.0%
  * The adoption I5 priced, taken as its own act with its own evidence: [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py)'s `_compiler_args` now passes clang for the model's C and C++ on every configure that finds one, found-or-absent in `_ccache_args`'s shape, so a machine without clang builds under cmake's default and nothing pretends otherwise. The canonical and fast trees were retired by deletion, the canonical's memo cache saved aside and restored into the fresh tree exactly as a lane seed would be, and the fast tree left to reseed from the canonical on its next use.
  * **The fresh-tree build stage nearly halves**: 81.4 s at 259% CPU against the 175 to 181 s the gcc lanes measured the same day, 149 s over the three stages together, and the largest term of a real-edit build is now the 38.7 s clang compile of the generated unit rather than gcc's 81.8 (I5).
  * **What cmake actually does with an existing tree handed a new compiler was worth exactly one probe, and the tree already being retired was the specimen**: neither acceptance nor a refusal by name, but a silent wipe-and-reconfigure that carries only the compiler variables forward, dropping the build type passed on the same command line and dying on the project's own no-build-type guard. An existing tree ends in an error either way, so retirement by deletion is the migration, which is the lane idiom already.
  * **The flip revealed eight latent warnings and introduced none**: clang's `-Wuninitialized-const-pointer` fires eight times in the vendored berkeley-softfloat-3 sources, a `commonNaN` passed uninitialized by const pointer, a diagnostic gcc does not carry. The vendored tree stays byte-identical to its pin, so the warnings are booked rather than repaired; the two `-Wparentheses-equality` warnings in the generated unit stand as I5 found them.
  * Exit evidence: build green with all three stage exits 0 and `CMakeCache` naming /usr/bin/clang and /usr/bin/clang++; the differential corpus at **24 of 24 with every trace digest unmoved**, which is the gate a compiler flip answers to; `devicetree` unchanged at 4,466 bytes with no warning; `reference` green at 77 properties over corpus v6's 224 checks and 6,658 records; the profile sweep unchanged at 199 refusals of 199; the legacy rig unchanged at a prefix of 67 on all 54 members; the restored memo cache serving at 1,442,433 bytes; test.py and both type gates green.

**Instrument subtotal:** 20.3 h · 2% · 18.3 h complete · open range 1–3 h.

## Estimate and schedule basis

* Completed estimates are actual elapsed session intervals with overlapping build waits apportioned approximately.
* Open work classes:
  * A · pinning, configuration, and mechanical curation
  * B · semantic porting and tool re-homing with differential tests
  * C · fresh systems authoring with functional tests
  * D · RTL and FPGA work
* Confidence: every open item's range spans roughly a factor of two about its midpoint, and a class believed softer is priced by widening its own items' ranges rather than by a second figure stated over the total.
* Grand total: the sum of the item cells, 1,147.9 h midpoint over a 797.9–1,497.9 h range.
* Measured gating may move 42 h of the midpoint beyond M8, the sum of M2.2's and M2.3's cells over a 28–56 h range. It is an author's adjustment stated beside the derived total rather than folded into it, so `--fix` does not reach it and its three sites, the M8 gate line, M2's entry condition, and this one, are held equal by hand.
* At 10–20 attended hours per week across two or three lanes, M8 is approximately 5–10 months away. Review capacity, not lane count, is the constraint beyond three lanes.
